## Confirmed fixes

### F1 — ALIGN-GLOBAL-001: `ScoringMatrix.GapOpen` ignored; no affine-gap global alignment
- Defect: every `GlobalAlign` overload is linear NW (each gap position = `GapExtend`); `GapOpen` never read, so `BlastDna` (2/−3/−5/−2) could not be applied as BLAST scoring. Repro: `GlobalAlign("ACGTTTTACGT","ACGTACGT",BlastDna)` charges the 3-nt gap as 3×(−2) instead of −5+3×(−2).
- Sources: Gotoh 1982 gap cost w(k)=v+u·k (GapOpen excludes first extension); BLAST+ manual NBK279684 blastn defaults (reward 2, penalty −3, gapopen 5, gapextend 2 — search snippets); Flouri et al. 2015 (bioRxiv 10.1101/031500, snippets + gata-bio/affine-gaps README) for correct border initialisation.
- Reference cross-check: new `GlobalAlignAffine` = Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner(mode='global', open_gap_score=GapOpen+GapExtend)` = parasail 1.3.4 `nw_trace` on 1048 cases (8 schemes), 0 mismatches; every returned alignment re-scores to its score. Gotoh original border gives A/CAC = −9 vs correct −11.
- Fix: `GlobalAlignAffine` (DnaSequence + string overloads), three-state M/X/Y DP with explicit inter-matrix traceback, Flouri border X(i,0)=o+i·e, Y(0,j)=o+j·e, overflow-safe −∞ sentinel. Linear `GlobalAlign` kept (documented as linear-only).
- Tests: `BlastDnaPreset_MatchesNcbiBlastnDefaults`, `Affine_SingleLongGap_ChargedOneOpening_BlastDna` (5), `Affine_ReferenceScores_MatchBiopythonAndParasail` ×4 (−17, −6, 16, −3), `Affine_TwoSeparateEndGaps_EachChargedAnOpening_FlouriInitialization` (A/CAC = −11, `-A-`/`CAC`), `Affine_ZeroGapOpen_EqualsLinearNeedlemanWunsch`, `Affine_EmptyInputs_BorderValues` (""/ACGT = −13), `Affine_DnaSequenceAndStringOverloads_Agree`.

### F2 — ALIGN-LOCAL-001: `GapOpen` ignored; no affine-gap local alignment
- Defect: `LocalAlign` is linear SW only. Repro: `LocalAlign("ACGTACGTAAAAAAACGTACGT","ACGTACGTACGTACGT",HighIdentityDna)` = 74 (6-gap charged 6×(−1)); affine value 64 (−10 + 6×(−1)).
- Sources: Smith & Waterman 1981 (J Mol Biol 147:195), Gotoh 1982 (J Mol Biol 162:705) — WebSearch snippets; parasail `src/sw.c` and Biopython `Bio/Align/_pairwisealigner.c` (raw.githubusercontent.com, opened) for the zero-floored three-state local recurrence.
- Reference cross-check: 3192 cases (1596 linear + 1596 affine, 8 schemes) — scores 100% equal to Biopython 1.88 `mode='local'` and parasail 1.3.4 `sw_trace`; alignments re-score to their score; ungapped strings = `s[Start..End]`. Seqeron end cell (first row-major max) ∈ Biopython co-optimal set in all checked cases.
- Fix: `LocalAlignAffine` (DnaSequence + string); `GlobalAlignAffineCore` and local share `AffineAlignCore(local)`. XML docs on `AlignmentResult` coordinates (0-based inclusive, −1 for empty local) — docs only, no signature change.
- Tests (`SequenceAligner_LocalAlign_Tests` 7→20): `LocalAlignAffine_LongGap_ChargedOneOpening` (64 vs linear 74), `LocalAlignAffine_ReferenceValues_MatchBiopythonAndParasail` (12 @ (7,12)/(0,5); 10 @ (6,10)/(3,7)), `LocalAlignAffine_ZeroGapOpen_EqualsLinearSmithWaterman` (13, `GTT-AC`/`GTTGAC`), `LocalAlignAffine_OpeningCostPreventsBridging` (8), tie/zero-stop/empty/null tests for linear and affine.

### F3 — ALIGN-SEMI-001: `GapOpen` ignored; no affine fitting alignment; spec errors
- Defect: `SemiGlobalAlign` is linear-only. Repro (test `SemiGlobalAlignAffine_LongGap_ChargedOneOpening`): affine 65 vs linear 75. TestSpec NEG claimed the last-row max for no-match input is at j=1 (it is j=0: AAAA/CCCC → `AAAA----`/`----CCCC`, −4); INV-4 was vacuous.
- Sources: parasail README semi-global variant table (raw.githubusercontent.com, opened); Biopython 1.88 `Bio/Align/__init__.py` (end_insertion_score).
- Reference cross-check: linear and new affine scores equal Biopython `PairwiseAligner(mode='global', end_insertion_score=0)` and parasail `sg_dx` on 1628 random/edge + 400 long (≤200 bp) cases; all alignments ∈ Biopython co-optimal set. Discriminator ACGTAA/CCACGT (1,−1,−1) = 2 (overlap aligner would give 4).
- Fix: `SemiGlobalAlignAffine` (DnaSequence + string) via `AffineAlignCore(AlignmentType)` (M(0,j)=0, end at first max of last row); XML docs state variant, reference equivalents, ties; TestSpec/Evidence corrected.
- Tests: `SemiGlobalAlign_ReferenceAlignments_MatchBiopythonAndParasail` ×3 (12 `--GA-TTACA--`/`CCGATTTACAGG`; 2; 1), `SemiGlobalAlignAffine_ReferenceValues_MatchBiopythonAndParasail` ×3 (0; −4; 7), `…LongGap_ChargedOneOpening` (65), `…OpeningCostPreventsGap` (6), `…ZeroGapOpen_EqualsLinear` (12), `…EmptyInputs` (0; −5).

### F4 — ALIGN-MULTI-001: `MultipleAlign` (center-star) was not an optimal pairwise alignment for k = 2
- Defect: the pairwise stage of the "center-star" was `AnchorBasedAligner` (exact-match anchors force the alignment path), so the MSA of two sequences was not the Needleman-Wunsch optimum and the center was a 4-mer cosine heuristic, not Gusfield's. Repro: `MultipleAlign(GGAAGCCAATAATCGTGCACGAAGGTGACTGCAGGAGAGTACGAAGGT, AGACAGCCAAAATCTTCAGAGTTCGACTGGAGGTGACTACGAAGAT)` TotalScore 9 vs optimum 24; 4 of 2000 random related pairs suboptimal (22 vs 27, −35 vs −3, …).
- Sources: Gusfield 1993 (Bull Math Biol 55:141, WebSearch records: center-star, bound 2 − 2/k) and Gusfield 1997 §14.6.1 (method description); Feng & Doolittle 1987 (once a gap, always a gap).
- Reference cross-check: Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner(mode='global', match 1, mismatch −1, open = extend = −1)`: 24 / 27 / −3 for the three repro pairs; independent Python Gusfield center-star (own NW with diagonal > up > left, center = argmax of total pair score, gap-slot merge) reproduces the rows of 300 random sets (k = 2..6, 3 scoring schemes) exactly (0 mismatches); the k = 2 SP equals the Biopython optimum in all 62 two-sequence sets.
- Fix: `MultipleAlign` = Gusfield center-star with the canonical linear NW for every pair (pair scores from the linear-space NW pass, alignments via `GlobalAlignLinearSpace`, exact Hirschberg above 4 M cells so memory stays bounded); center = best total pair score (ties: lowest index). The former behaviour is kept as the explicitly approximate additive `MultipleAlignAnchored` (public). `ReconcileAlignments` → `MergeCenterAlignments` (no unused score, shared by both variants); `MultipleAlignClassic` (benchmark baseline) now merges properly instead of right-padding.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_MultipleAlignStar_Tests` ×16 (3 Biopython pair optima, 3/4/5-sequence independent-reference rows, center rule, determinism, 150 random validity sets, empty member, large pair via Hirschberg path, anchored-variant contract).
- Commit: `114ab66e`.

### F5 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (B05 request): `AnchorBasedAligner` chaining, gap scoring, large gaps, comments
- Defects: (1) comments claimed "all MEMs"/MUMmer while `FindExactMatchAnchors` returns one MEM per diagonal run; (2) `ChainAnchors` dropped one of two overlapping anchors (greedy `RemoveOverlaps`) and maximised the NUMBER of anchors (LIS) rather than the covered bases; (3) one-sided gap runs scored `GapOpen + k·GapExtend` while DP gaps and the `GlobalAlign` fallback were linear, so `Score` disagreed with the returned rows; (4) segments > 5000 bases were "aligned" by a diagonal heuristic; (5) empty input returned `AlignmentResult.Empty`. Repro (2): anchors (0,0,10) and (6,20,10) → old chain covered 10 bases, new 16.
- Sources: Delcher et al. 1999 (MUMmer; suffix tree, longest colinear subset, DP between matches); Abouelhoda & Ohlebusch 2005 (PDF opened: maximum-weight chain of colinear non-overlapping fragments); Hirschberg 1975.
- Fix: comments accurate; weighted colinear chain (O(a²) DP) with front-trimming of overlapping anchors (a suffix of an exact match is an exact match); linear scoring everywhere (`Score` = linear re-score of the rows, ≤ unconstrained optimum); exact linear-space Hirschberg (`GlobalAlignLinearSpace`, score identical to NW on 200 random pairs × 4 schemes with forced recursion) for segments above 25 M cells; empty side → proper all-gap alignment.
- Tests: `AnchorBasedAligner_Tests` ×17 (chain overlap/coverage/crossing + 300 random-anchor invariants, identical, empty side, linear scoring, 120 related pairs ≤ `GlobalAlign` with valid rows, repeated blocks, Hirschberg vs Biopython 24 / 27 and random schemes).
- Commit: `114ab66e`.

### F6 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (B06 request): `SelectCenterSequence` private 4-mer hash
- Verified real (own 256-slot hash + cosine). Alignment already references Core, so `SequenceExtensions.CountKmersSpan(4)` is usable; DNA input is upper-case ACGT so every window is a valid 4-mer: integer counts, exact dot/norm sums and the (i, j) accumulation order are unchanged, hence the same center on every input (KmerHash removed). Kept only for `MultipleAlignAnchored` (the exact star uses Gusfield's pair-score center). Request B06 → **done**.
- Commit: `114ab66e`.

### F7 — ALIGN-MULTI-001: T-Coffee extension only for primary pairs; percent identity over gap columns
- Defect: (a) `BuildExtendedLibrary` extended only the pairs present in the primary library, so a residue pair that no direct alignment joins but two alignments join through a third sequence scored 0 in the progressive DP; the paper ("Extension will be carried out on each pair of residues of A and B") and T-Coffee's `residue_pair_extended_list` (evaluate.c, opened) score every pair. (b) The percent-identity weight divided by the alignment length including gap columns; T-Coffee `get_seq_sim` (aln_convertion_util.c, opened) divides by aligned non-gap columns with integer division. Repro: CTT/ACT/CAA, pair (S1.1, S2.0): extended 0 (old) vs 133 = min(200, 133); ACGTTACGT/ACG--ACGT weight 78 (old) vs 100.
- Sources: Notredame, Higgins & Heringa 2000 (PDF pp.207-210 opened); cbcrg/tcoffee `evaluate.c`, `aln_convertion_util.c` (raw files opened); T-Coffee technical documentation.
- Reference cross-check: independent Python T-Coffee (own NW/SW, `get_seq_sim` weights, on-demand triplet extension, zero-gap progressive DP): 0/300 row mismatches after the fix; 170/300 differed before. Hand values: ACGTTACGT/ACGACGT/ACGTACGA primary 200, extended 385 = 200 + min(187, 185).
- Fix: `ExtendedLibrary` (primary + neighbour index + cache) evaluating any pair on demand; `PercentIdentity` per `get_seq_sim`; primary-library building shared (`BuildPrimaryLibrary`, diagnostics duplicate removed).
- Binary cross-check (Debian `t-coffee` 13.45 installed by apt, run with the same explicit primary library and the same guide tree, `-extend_mode slow_triplet`): its alignments differ from the paper-formula DP in most random sets (k = 3: 105 of 120) because T-Coffee rescales every extended pair score by its residue's partner-weight total (`evaluate.c` L5245-5250, not in the paper), but under the paper's objective (sum of extended weights) the T-Coffee alignment never scored higher than ours for k = 3 (0/120); for k = 4-5 it scored higher in 7/150 sets, where its tree traversal/normalisation changes the merges. The extension rule itself is therefore confirmed by the paper text and `residue_pair_extended_list`, not by identical rows.
- Tests: +3 in `SequenceAligner_MultipleAlignConsistency_Tests` (weights 200/385, indirect pair 0 → 133, rows `-CTT`/`AC-T`/`-CAA`).
- Commit: `114ab66e`.

### F8 — ALIGN-MULTI-001: progressive distance was not the ClustalW distance; UPGMA ties decided by rounding noise
- Defect: guide-tree distance was 1 − identities / alignment length ("Clustal-style" claim); ClustalW 1.83 `pairalign.c` uses 100·count / MIN(len1,len2) (file opened). Separately, mathematically equal averaged UPGMA distances (e.g. 0.3 + 0.4 vs 0.7) could tie-break differently in `double` (1 of 300 random sets differed from an exact-rational Python run).
- Fix: distance = 1 − identities / shorter length; strict-smaller test beyond 1e-9 so the documented lowest-index tie rule decides. Affects the guide tree (and so the progressive / iterative / consistency rows) only.
- Reference cross-check: independent Python (Fractions): progressive 0/300, iterative 0/300, consistency 0/300 mismatches.
- Tests: the engineered iterative demonstration (which depended on the old tree) re-derived: TCA/TCT/GCAC/TAAC progressive `T--CA T--CT GCAC- TAAC-` SP −7 → iterative `TCA- TCT- GCAC TAAC` SP −3 (hand column sums, Python refinement); `ConvergesWithinCap` updated.
- Commit: `114ab66e`.

### F9 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (cross-batch d): MCP affine pairwise tools and honest `gapOpen` descriptions
- Defect: `global_align` / `local_align` / `semi_global_align` expose `gapOpen` but call the linear aligners (it is ignored); no MCP path to `GlobalAlignAffine` / `LocalAlignAffine` / `SemiGlobalAlignAffine`.
- Fix: additive tools `global_align_affine`, `local_align_affine`, `semi_global_align_affine` (delegate to `SequenceAligner.*Affine`, same validation as the linear tools); the linear tools' descriptions and `gapOpen` parameter text now say it is ignored.
- Tests: `AffineAlignTests` ×7 (Biopython/parasail values −17, 12 @ (7,12,0,5), 7 `--GA-TTACA--`; linear tools ignore `gapOpen`).
- Commit: `faab47f7`.

### F10 — ALIGN-MULTI-001: MCP exposed only the star MSA, mis-described as "progressive"
- `multiple_align` description/title said "anchor-based progressive" (it was a center-star). Now described as the exact center-star; additive tools `multiple_align_anchored`, `multiple_align_progressive`, `multiple_align_iterative` (`maxIterations`), `multiple_align_consistency`, all delegating to the SequenceAligner methods through one `RunMsa` helper. Docs (`docs/mcp/tools/alignment/*`, server README, MCP_STATUS, traceability, generated skills catalogs, bio-alignment skill text), contract test list, `gen-catalog.py` counts (alignment 27 → 34, total 486 → 493). Tests: 5 new in `MultipleAlignTests`.
- Commit: `faab47f7`.

### F11 — ALIGN-STATS-001: residues compared case-sensitively
- Defect: `CalculateStatistics("acgt","ACGT")` counted 0 matches / 4 mismatches; EMBOSS (and every aligner of this class, which upper-case their input) treats the residues case-insensitively. Repro: lower-case rows from a user/FASTA soft-masked alignment.
- Source: EMBOSS needle/water 6.6.0 binary (lower-case input identical to upper-case) and `ajalign.c` (`ajSeqcvtGetCodeK` maps both cases to one code).
- Fix: column residues upper-cased before comparison and matrix lookup (statistics and markup).
- Tests: `CalculateStatistics_LowerCaseRows_AreCaseInsensitive`, oracle table (each row re-run in lower case), MCP `AlignmentStatistics_LowerCase_IsCaseInsensitive_AndUnknownMatrixRejected`.

### F12 — ALIGN-STATS-001: rows of unequal length accepted
- Defect: `CalculateStatistics` / `FormatAlignment` looped over the first row's length (`IndexOutOfRangeException` or a silently ignored tail of the longer row).
- Fix: `ArgumentException` for unequal rows (MCP wrapper already rejected it; now one contract). Tests: `CalculateStatistics_And_FormatAlignment_UnequalRows_Throw`.

### F13 — ALIGN-STATS-001: no gap-opening count and no positives (BLAST / Biopython parity)
- Defect: `AlignmentStatistics` had only gap columns; BLAST tabular `gapopen` and Biopython `open_gaps` (number of gap runs) were unavailable, and the similar count was folded into `Similarity` only.
- Sources: NCBI BLAST tabular fields (gapopen = gap openings; identities/positives/gaps), Biopython 1.88 `Alignment.counts` (`gaps`, `open_gaps`, `mismatches`, `positives`, `identities`); EMBOSS needle header (Length/Identity/Similarity/Gaps).
- Cross-check: 301 alignments, `GapOpens` = Biopython `open_gaps` and `Mismatches` = Biopython `mismatches` in every row; HBA/HBB example 4 openings. Gap *columns* = EMBOSS "Gaps" = Biopython `gaps` (total gap length).
- Fix: additive optional record fields `AlignmentStatistics.Positives` and `GapOpens` (defaults 0, existing constructors unchanged), MCP DTO fields `positives`, `gapOpens`. Denominator convention documented explicitly (Length incl. gap columns).
- Tests: `CalculateStatistics_OnAffineGlobalAlignment_SingleLongGap_OneOpening` and the oracle; MCP `AlignmentStatistics_ScalarModel_PositiveMismatchIsSimilar_AndGapOpensCounted`.

### F14 — ALIGN-STATS-001: no residue-pair similarity; markup differed from EMBOSS; identical X/X counted similar
- Defect: similarity could only come from a scalar mismatch score, so protein (BLOSUM62/PAM250) and IUPAC-DNA (EDNAFULL) similarity — the very example of the needle documentation (HBA/HBB: 90 similar of 149) — was unreachable; markup printed a space for mismatches and the doc claimed the `.` tier unreachable and `:` for score > 1.0 (AlignFormats web page), while the needle/water 6.6.0 binary writes `.` for aligned non-similar columns; MCP `format_alignment` described `.` for mismatches but printed a space. Repro: `FormatAlignment("ACGT","ACGA")` gave `||| `, needle gives `|||.`.
- Sources: EMBOSS 6.6 `ajax/core/ajalign.c` `alignConsStats` / `alignSim` (raw.githubusercontent.com, opened), needle/water 6.6.0 binaries + data files `EBLOSUM62`, `EPAM250`, `EDNAFULL`, Biopython 1.88 `substitution_matrices` (BLOSUM62, PAM250, NUC.4.4), NCBI blastn defaults (BLAST+ manual: reward 2, penalty −3, existence 5, extension 2).
- Rule recovered from the source and confirmed by single-pair experiments with the binary (both orders, all BLOSUM62 pairs / all IUPAC pairs): similar iff `M(x,y) > 0 ∧ (M(x,x) > 0 ∨ M(y,y) > 0)`; markup `h = [M(x,x)>0] + [M(x,y)>0]`: 2 → `|` (identical) / `:`, 1 → `.`, 0 → space (gap columns space). So an identical X/X (−1) or N/N (−1) is an identity but neither similar nor barred; a first-row ambiguity residue gets `.`/space even if the pair is positive (needle R/A `.`, A/R `:`) — an EMBOSS quirk, reproduced and documented.
- Oracle numbers: 301 alignments (needle global + water local; DNA, IUPAC DNA / EDNAFULL, protein / EBLOSUM62 + EPAM250 incl. B/Z/X; 12 439 columns): Length, Identity, Similarity, Gaps, markup line equal in all; mismatches and open gaps equal Biopython; HBA/HBB literal example 149 / 65 / 90 / 9. Matrices: 576 + 576 + 256 cells equal the EMBOSS data files; 576 + 576 + 225 shared cells equal Biopython 1.88.
- Fix: `SubstitutionMatrix` (Infrastructure, additive: Blosum62, Pam250, EdnaFull, `TryGetScore`/`GetScore`), `CalculateStatistics(AlignmentResult, SubstitutionMatrix)` and `FormatAlignment(AlignmentResult, int, SubstitutionMatrix)` through one column-classifier core shared with the scalar overloads (statistics and markup cannot diverge), scalar overloads keep their signatures (identical similar iff Match > 0, non-identical iff Mismatch > 0, mismatch mark `.`); MCP `alignment_statistics` / `format_alignment` gain `matrix` (BLOSUM62 | PAM250 | EDNAFULL) and scalar `match` / `mismatch`; `ScoringMatrix` XML docs now state the gap convention (library/BLAST+/Biopython `GapOpen + k·GapExtend` vs EMBOSS `gapopen + (k−1)·gapextend`); `BlastDna` = blastn defaults and `HighIdentityDna` match/mismatch = EDNAFULL verified by test.
- Tests: oracle test over the 301-row embedded TSV (`TestData/Alignment/needle_water_stats.tsv`, `scripts/alignment/generate_alignment_stats_oracle.py`, regenerates byte-identical), published HBA/HBB counts + needle markup blocks, X/X, quirk cases (7 + 3 TestCases), matrix spot values/symmetry/alphabets, scalar presets; MCP: published example through the tool with `matrix = "blosum62"`, `format_alignment` with BLOSUM62 `::.: |`.

### F15 — GENOMIC-COMMON-001: `PositionInFirst` was an arbitrary suffix-tree occurrence, not the first
- Defect: `FindLongestCommonRegion` (via `LongestCommonSubstringInfo`, "one implementation-specific occurrence") and `FindCommonRegions` (`FindAllOccurrences(...)[0]`, DFS order) reported a suffix-tree-layout-dependent position. Repro: `FindCommonRegions(AGGAAGAAGGAGGCCGATTACT, CGTGGAAGGAGGCCGA, 2)` last region `GA` at 15 (first occurrence 2); `FindLongestCommonRegion(CACAGATAAAGGACTGAGGCTCTACG, GA)` → 15 (first 4). The documented contract says "first occurrence".
- Source: definition (Wikipedia "Longest common substring" via the existing Evidence; any maximal answer is acceptable, so a deterministic layout-independent representative is required). Oracle: independent Python (`scripts/analysis/generate_common_region_oracle.py`): DP over all end cells + suffix automaton (asserted equal) for the LCS, brute-force `in` matching statistics for the regions, positions = `str.find`. Old code agreed with the oracle on every LCS length/sequence and region string/start, and differed in `PositionInFirst` only.
- Fix: `FindLongestCommonRegion` uses `FindAllLongestCommonSubstrings` (ascending, duplicate-free lists → `[0]`); `FindCommonRegions` takes the minimum of the occurrence list. Layout-independent, tie rule unchanged.
- Tests: `FindCommonRegions_PositionInFirst_IsLowestOccurrence`, `FindLongestCommonRegion_PositionInFirst_IsLowestOccurrence`, `…RepeatedOccurrences_ReportsFirstInBoth`, oracle test (283 pairs, embedded `TestData/Analysis/common_region_oracle.tsv`), 150-pair in-test brute force; MCP `FindCommonRegions_PositionInFirst_IsFirstOccurrence`.

### F16 — GENOMIC-COMMON-001 (B05 request): `FindCommonRegions` quadratic → matching statistics
- Defect: per-start binary search with `Substring(i, mid)` + `Contains` copied up to m characters per probe: O(m²) time and allocation regardless of `minLength` (random 100 kbp pair 2.6 s; extrapolated ≈ 4 min at 1 Mbp).
- Source: Chang & Lawler 1994 matching statistics (search snippets; paper not opened), MUMmer MEMs (Kurtz 2004) through the canonical `SuffixTree.FindMaximalExactMatches` (B05 F20). The per-start longest right-maximal match is ms(i); ms(i) ≥ ms(i−1) − 1.
- Fix: no public per-position matching-statistics API exists on this branch (`SuffixTreeAlgorithms.CollectMaximalMatchingStatistics` is private; src/SuffixTree untouched per instructions), so ms(i) is assembled from public primitives, exactly: ms(i) < K = ⌈log₄(n·m)⌉ + 2 by carry-over + `Contains(span)` extension (O(m·K), no substring copies); ms(i) ≥ max(K, minLength) from the MEM stream (ms(i) = max over MEMs with start ≤ i of end − i; exact because the maximising diagonal's maximal run is a MEM of length ≥ ms(i)); expected MEM count between unrelated random sequences O(1). Strings are deduplicated with an alternate span lookup (no allocation for duplicates). `ArgumentNullException` for null arguments (eager; was `NullReferenceException`).
- Results: random 1 Mbp pair (Debug) LCS 2.8 s, regions min 1 (666 618) 3.8 s, min 20 1.7 s; 1 Mbp with 1 % substitutions, min 20: 861 267 regions (131.6 Mchar) 4.6 s. Output equal to the oracle (283 pairs + 120 extra 200-2500 bp pairs with planted blocks and min up to 40, run ad hoc) and to the in-test brute force.
- Tests: `FindCommonRegions_OneMegabasePair_IsLinearAndCorrect` (1 Mbp + planted 5 kb block, < 60 s guard), oracle + brute-force differential tests, `CommonRegionMethods_NullSequences_ThrowArgumentNullException`, lower-case equality, non-ACGT (N/U) rejected by `DnaSequence`, `FindCommonRegions_ExtremeMinLength_Handled` (int.MinValue … int.MaxValue).

### F17 — GENOMIC-COMMON-001: MCP/doc claims
- `find_common_regions` described "All common regions" (it is the right-maximal per-start set, prefixes omitted) → description, README, tool doc and `.mcp.json` corrected (catalog regenerated, `gen-catalog.py --check` fresh, links OK). `find_longest_common_region` doc example (`ATGCATGC`/`CATGCAT` → `ATGC`, 0/1) was wrong: the LCS is `ATGCAT` (length 6, 0/1). Both wrappers already delegate (no re-implementation). Algorithm doc, TestSpec (INV-6..8, B1-B7), Evidence and the unit report updated.


### F18 — GENOMIC-MOTIFS-001 (B05 + B06 requests): `GenomicAnalyzer.FindMotif` unsorted and a copy of `MotifFinder.FindExactMotif`
- Defect: `FindMotif` returned `SuffixTree.FindAllOccurrences` as is (DFS order) while `MotifFinder.FindExactMotif` / `FindKnownMotifs` sort ascending, and the two methods were otherwise the same code (upper-case motif → suffix-tree search; B06 KMER-POSITIONS-001 duplication note). Repro: `FindMotif(GATATATGCATATACTT, ATAT)` = `[1, 9, 3]` (Rosalind SUBS sample, 1-based 2 4 10 = 0-based 1 3 9); `FindMotif(null, "A")` threw `NullReferenceException`. MCP `find_motif` doc said "not necessarily sorted".
- Source: Rosalind SUBS sample (as in the unit report), `str.find` scan oracle (200 random AC-alphabet sequences/motifs in the test, ascending = `IndexOf` loop).
- Fix: `FindMotif` = `ArgumentNullException` guard + `MotifFinder.FindExactMotif(...).ToList()` (the canonical suffix-tree wrapper, ascending, null/empty motif → empty). Decision on the cheaper engine: `MotifFinder.FindExactMotif` is documented/validated as the suffix-tree wrapper of PAT-EXACT-001 (B05) and its cost model is index reuse, so it was left unchanged; results are identical to `KmerAnalyzer.FindKmerPositions` (KMP), which `FindKnownMotifs` now uses itself (F20). XML doc states the cost (0.9 s / 171 MB at 1 Mbp, 23 s / 1.7 GB at 10 Mbp for the first query on a `DnaSequence`, then O(m + occ)) and points to the KMP call for one-off huge scans. Both MCP tools (`find_motif`, `find_exact_motif`) now run the same engine.
- Tests: `FindMotif_PositionsAscending_EqualToMotifFinderAndStringIndexOf` (200 random), `FindMotif_RosalindSubsSample_AscendingNotTreeOrder`, `FindMotif_Contract_NullEmptyAndOverlap`; MCP `FindMotif_Positions_AreAscending_AndEqualFindExactMotif`.
- Cross-batch: B05 request (sort) **done**; B06 request (duplicate) **done** (delegation to the canonical, recorded under Duplication removed).

### F19 — GENOMIC-MOTIFS-001: IUPAC-degenerate motifs and reverse strand were "not implemented" (silent no-hit trap)
- Defect: the known motifs of the literature are IUPAC consensus strings (TATA box `TATAWAW`, Bucher; AP-1 `TGASTCA`) and sites occur on both strands, but `FindKnownMotifs` matched IUPAC letters literally (a DNA sequence never contains them → silently no hit) and searched the forward strand only; the algorithm doc listed both as "not implemented, rely on other methods".
- Source/oracle: EMBOSS fuzznuc 6.6.0 (Debian `emboss`, run locally, `-pmismatch 0 -complement Y -rformat gff`) — observed semantics: overlapping hits, IUPAC base sets, '-' hits = forward-strand windows equal to the reverse complement with forward coordinates, palindromes hit on both strands. Independent Python `re` lookahead with NC-IUB classes + IUPAC reverse complement = fuzznuc on all 2448 (sequence, pattern) pairs (816 with forward hits, 776 with reverse hits; sequences incl. AC-only, homopolymers, tandem repeats; 16 exact + 20 IUPAC patterns), asserted inside the generator (`scripts/analysis/generate_known_motif_oracle.py`, reproducible byte-identical).
- Fix: additive `FindKnownMotifHits(sequence, motifs, bothStrands = false, allowIupac = false)` → `KnownMotifHit(Position, IsReverseStrand)` (sorted by position, forward first at a tie); IUPAC via the canonical `MotifFinder.FindDegenerateMotif`, reverse pattern via the canonical IUPAC-complete `DnaSequence.GetReverseComplementString`; non-IUPAC symbol with `allowIupac` → `ArgumentException`. `FindKnownMotifs` / `FindMotif` keep the exact forward-only contract (documented). MCP `find_known_motifs` gains optional `bothStrands`, `iupac` and the additive result field `reverseMatches`. No new catalogue: the fixed regulatory library (TATA/Kozak/CAAT/GC box…) is `MotifFinder.FindRegulatoryElements` (B05 MOTIF-REGULATORY-001, consensus already verified there).
- Tests: `FindKnownMotifHits_BothStrandsIupac_MatchesFuzznucOracle` (2448), `…ForwardOnlyIupac…`, `ExactMode_AllEntryPoints_MatchOracle`, `FindKnownMotifHits_FuzznucWorkedExample`, `…InvalidIupacSymbol_Throws…`; MCP `FindKnownMotifs_BothStrandsAndIupac_MatchFuzznuc`.

### F20 — GENOMIC-MOTIFS-001: every `FindKnownMotifs` call built a suffix tree (complexity)
- Defect: a handful of motifs on a fresh sequence (every MCP call builds a fresh `DnaSequence`) paid the O(n) tree build with its large constant for nothing: Release, random DNA, 6-mer: tree build 62 ms / 924 ms (171 MB) / 4.8 s (684 MB) at 100 kb / 1 Mbp / 4 Mbp against 0.85 / 6.9 / 24.5 ms per KMP scan (= 73 / 135 / 197 scans; B06 at 10 Mbp: 23.3 s, 1.7 GB vs 71 ms).
- Fix: below 128 distinct queries (motifs × strands) each motif is one `KmerAnalyzer.FindKmerPositions` scan (canonical KMP, B06; ascending by construction, case-folded); from 128 queries the cached suffix tree is used and sorted. Same positions either way. B06's rule "route through the cheaper canonical only if behaviour is preserved" holds: identical output (tested), only the cost profile changes; `FindMotif` itself stays on the PAT-EXACT-001 index (F18).
- Tests: `FindKnownMotifs_ScanAndIndexRoutes_ReturnIdenticalPositions` (6 motifs vs 206 motifs vs 70 × 2 strands against `IndexOf`), `FindKnownMotifs_SingleMotifOnLargeSequence_IsLinearScan` (3 Mbp).

### F21 — GENOMIC-ORF-001 (B02 request): hard-coded stop codons
- Defect: `StopCodons = { "TAA", "TAG", "TGA" }` / `IsStopCodon` (private copies of the standard code). Wrong for every other NCBI table: table 2 (vertebrate mitochondrial) has stops TAA/TAG/AGA/AGG and reads TGA as Trp. Repro: `ATGTGAAAATAA` → ORF `ATGTGA` (table 1) vs the 12-nt ORF in table 2; `ATGCCCAGAGGG` → no ORF vs `ATGCCCAGA` (AGA is a stop). B02's request ("`GeneticCode.Standard.IsStopCodon`") verified real.
- Source: NCBI gc.prt tables (via `GeneticCode`, = Biopython `CodonTable`, B02-verified), NCBI `COrf::FindOrfs(genetic_code)` / ORFfinder `-g` (orf.cpp, opened).
- Fix: stops come from the canonical `GeneticCode` through the new shared `Core/OrfScanner` (64-entry lookup built from `IsStopCodon`); additive overload `FindOpenReadingFrames(DnaSequence, int, OrfSearchOptions)` with `OrfSearchOptions.GeneticCode` (default Standard; tables 27/28/31 rejected as in `Translator.FindOrfs`: every stop also codes an amino acid). MCP `genetic_code`.
- Tests: oracle rows over every NCBI table; `FindOpenReadingFrames_TableTwo_TgaReadsThrough_AgaTerminates`, `…DualCodingStopTables_ThrowEagerly` ×3; MCP `FindOpenReadingFrames_GeneticCode_ChoosesTheStopCodons`.

### F22 — GENOMIC-ORF-001: quadratic rescan per ATG
- Defect: every ATG scanned forward to its first stop; a stop-free ATG-rich stretch is O(n²) (documented as "pathological" in the algorithm doc). Repro: `ATG`×300 000 + `TAA` (900 kb), `minLength` 899 000: ≈ 300 000 × 150 000 codon comparisons (minutes) → 335 ORFs in well under a second now.
- Fix: one pass per frame with a list of pending starts, flushed (ascending) at each stop; identical output order (frame, then ascending start). Also no per-ORF `IsCodon`/`CompareOrdinal`, codon lookup by 6-bit index.
- Tests: `FindOpenReadingFrames_LongStopFreeAtgRun_IsLinear` (335 ORFs), `…FiveMegabaseRandomSequence_ScansAllSixFramesQuickly` (5 Mbp).

### F23 — GENOMIC-ORF-001: `minLength` claimed to match NCBI ORFfinder, but NCBI does not count the stop codon
- Defect (documentation/semantics): TestSpec/Evidence/XML doc said the threshold "matches NCBI ORFfinder's nucleotide length filter" (stop included). NCBI `COrf` (`FindForwardOrfs`: "length in base pairs … Returned range does not include the stop codon", test `to + 1 >= min_length_bp + from`) and EMBOSS getorf `-minsize` both exclude the stop: an ORF with 72 coding nt + stop passes `minLength 72` in NCBI/getorf but needs `minLength 75` here.
- Source: `orf.cpp` / `orf.hpp` (raw.githubusercontent.com/ncbi/ncbi-cxx-toolkit-public, opened); getorf 6.6.0 run locally (stop excluded from `-minsize`, 377 comparisons); ProteinIQ's ORFfinder 0.4.3 wrapper docs ("the terminating stop codon does not count toward the threshold").
- Fix: default unchanged (`Length ≥ minLength`, stop counted — the contract of the reported `Length`, existing callers and the MCP default); the NCBI/getorf convention is the new option `OrfSearchOptions.MinLengthExcludesStop` (MCP `minLengthExcludesStop`); docs, XML docs and the MCP description state both conventions. The DUP_MAP note that `minLength = 100` means 300 nt in one tool and 100 nt in the other is now documented on the tool.
- Tests: `MinLengthExcludesStop_FollowsNcbiOrfFinderConvention`, oracle rows (both conventions, equal to the COrf port run with its own `min_length_bp` and to getorf `-minsize`).

### F24 — GENOMIC-ORF-001: reverse-strand coordinates undocumented / no input-strand coordinates
- Defect: `OrfInfo.Position` of a reverse-complement ORF is a coordinate of the reverse complement (not stated anywhere; the MCP tool said "0-based in the scanned strand" only in its doc page). A caller could not locate the ORF on the input without redoing the reverse complement arithmetic; NCBI ORFfinder/COrf report forward-strand coordinates.
- Source/oracle: NCBI `s_FindOrfs` (`from' = size − to − 1`, `to' = size − from − 1`); the oracle's coordinates are Biopython-reverse-complement based.
- Fix: additive `OrfInfo.GetForwardStrandRange(L)` → 0-based inclusive `(Start, End)`, stop included (reverse: `L − Position − Length`, `L − 1 − Position`), `OrfInfo.IsTerminated`; XML docs on every member; MCP result gains `start`, `end`, `isTerminated`. `Position` itself is unchanged (backward compatible, same convention as `Translator.FindOrfs`).
- Tests: `GetForwardStrandRange_ReverseComplementOfSpan_EqualsOrfSequence` (200 random sequences), `…WorkedExamples` (`CC+TTAGGGCAT+GG` → 2..10, `TTAGGGGGGCAT` → 0..11), oracle (forward coordinates); MCP `InputStrandCoordinates_AndTerminationFlag`.

### F25 — GENOMIC-ORF-001: NCBI ORFfinder options and the three ORF engines (B02/B11 request)
- Defect: the algorithm doc listed "alternative initiation codons and non-standard genetic codes" under *Not implemented, rely on `GenomeAnnotator.FindOrfs` / `Translator.FindOrfs`", although NCBI ORFfinder (the method's own cited reference) has them, plus most-upstream-start-per-stop reporting and 3'-partial ORFs; DUP_MAP §3: three ORF engines with no shared core.
- Evaluation (B02/B11 request): the three engines differ only by policy — start set (ATG / table starts / ATG-GTG-TTG), nested starts (all / first), open ends (dropped / reported), length unit (nt incl. stop / aa) and coordinate convention — so the scan was factored into one parametrised core, `Core/OrfScanner.FindRegions(strand, code, isStartCodon, nestedStarts, includeOpenEnded)` (new file, additive; Core cannot depend on Analysis, so the core lives there). `GenomicAnalyzer.FindOpenReadingFrames` is a thin adapter (options → predicate, length filter, `OrfInfo`); `Translator.FindOrfs` (getorf `-find 1`, B02 finished batch, "(B09 for B02)": no behaviour change, B02's getorf oracle tests unchanged and green) now builds on the same core instead of its own character loop. `GenomeAnnotator.FindOrfs` (B11) keeps its own loop → see Cross-batch dedup requests.
- Fix: `OrfSearchOptions` { `GeneticCode`, `StartCodons` (AtgOnly | GeneticCodeStarts | AnySenseCodon = ORFfinder `-s`), `NestedOrfs` (true = Rosalind / `COrf longest_orfs = false`; false = ORFfinder default / getorf), `IncludeOpenEnded` (COrf 3'-partial ORFs), `MinLengthExcludesStop`, `SearchBothStrands` }; defaults reproduce the 2-argument method bit for bit. MCP parameters `genetic_code`, `startCodons`, `nested`, `includeOpenEnded`, `minLengthExcludesStop`, `bothStrands`.
- Tests: oracle (below), `StartCodons_AtgOnly_AlternativeStarts_AnySenseCodon`, `NestedOrfs_False_…`, `IncludeOpenEnded_…`, `SearchBothStrands_False_…`; differential `Differential_TranslatorFindOrfs_EqualsOutermostStartsOfTheGeneticCode` (tables 1, 2, 4, 11, 240 sequences) and `Differential_GenomeAnnotatorFindOrfs_AtgInitiatedOrfsAreTheSameSet` (150 sequences); MCP `StartCodonModes`, `NestedOpenEndedLengthConventionAndStrands`.

### F26 — GENOMIC-ORF-001: independent oracle
- The 2026-06 validation re-derived the expectations from the Rosalind sample with a Python re-implementation but locked only 14 hand-written cases. New oracle `scripts/analysis/generate_orf_oracle.py` (reproducible byte-identical): 1746 (sequence, options) rows (tables 1–33 except 27/28/31; start modes; nested/longest; open ends; length conventions; both/forward strand), three independent engines asserted equal at generation time — (1) definition-based finder with Biopython 1.88 `translate`/`CodonTable`/`reverse_complement`, (2) line-by-line port of NCBI `COrf::FindForwardOrfs`/`s_FindOrfs` (1515 rows; also run with COrf's own `min_length_bp`), (3) EMBOSS getorf 6.6.0 `-find 3` (377 rows, tables whose EGC start set equals gc.prt 4.6: EGC.3 / EGC.13 carry older start sets and are excluded; getorf never writes a START that is the last complete codon — dropped from that comparison). The library equals the TSV on all 1746 rows (ordered, with strands, frames, forward coordinates, termination).
- Tests: `FindOpenReadingFrames_MatchesBiopythonNcbiCOrfAndGetorfOracle`, `…DefaultOverload_EqualsDefaultOptions` (embedded `TestData/Analysis/orf_oracle.tsv`). The previous unit tests (14) pass unchanged.
- Commit (F21–F26): `a6662c0a`.

### F27 — GENOMIC-REPEAT-001 (B04 request): `GenomicAnalyzer.FindPalindromes` was an unvalidated O(n·L²) copy of the canonical scan
- Defect (verified against the pre-change code, `GenomicAnalyzer.cs:~206`, DUP_MAP §17): verbatim copy of `RepeatFinder.FindPalindromesCore`'s even-length loop without the validation. Repros: `FindPalindromes(ACGT…, minLength: 2)` reported every AT/CG/GC/TA dinucleotide; `minLength: 5` (odd) scanned only odd lengths and silently returned nothing; `FindPalindromes(null)` → `NullReferenceException` on enumeration; per window one `Substring` + `GetReverseComplementString` (O(n·L²) character work, ≈ 10⁷ short-lived strings at 1 Mbp × 4–12); results length-major (all 4-mers, then 6-mers …) instead of the Rosalind REVP position-then-length order. (The "IUPAC windows counted" part of the request cannot occur through this `DnaSequence` API: the type is ACGT-only; the string overload / MCP already use the canonical ACGT-only scan.)
- Source: Rosalind REVP ("every reverse palindrome … length between 4 and 12", sample output order position then length); Biopython 1.88 `Seq.reverse_complement`; the canonical `RepeatFinder.FindPalindromes` (REP-PALIN-001, B04 F12/F13).
- Reference cross-check: `scripts/analysis/generate_palindrome_oracle.py` — Biopython `reverse_complement` (== pure-str translate/reverse) over every window of 130 sequences × 7 (min,max) ranges = 910 rows, 18 000+ hits; new facade 0 mismatches; REVP sample `4 6, 5 4, 6 6, 7 4, 17 4, 18 4, 20 6, 21 4` reproduced. 1 Mbp, 4–12: ≈ 0.4 s.
- Fix: `FindPalindromes(DnaSequence, int, int)` = `RepeatFinder.FindPalindromes(...).Select(→ PalindromeInfo)`. **Contract change (intended, documented in XML doc, `Repeat_Detection.md` §5.5, `Palindrome_Detection.md` §5.4):** `minLength` even and ≥ 4, `maxLength` ≥ `minLength` else `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` at the call; `null` → `ArgumentNullException`; position-then-length order. Default 4–12, 0-based positions, all nested/overlapping windows unchanged. No caller in `src` used the old behaviour (MCP `find_palindromes` already called `RepeatFinder`); the two smoke tests in `GenomicAnalyzerTests` stay green.
- Tests: new `GenomicAnalyzer_FindPalindromes_Tests` (11 + 5 cases): Biopython oracle (910 rows), REVP sample, equality with `RepeatFinder` on 60 random sequences, validation (null, minLength 2/3/5/0/−4, max < min), odd `maxLength` rounding, empty/short input, 1 Mbp performance + expected count window.
- Commit: 8056783e (F27–F31 together).

### F28 — GENOMIC-REPEAT-001: `FindRepeats` review (semantics, independent oracle, null contract) (commit 8056783e)
- Review of the suffix-tree traversal rewrite (3d49621e, cherry-picked a83e6634): semantics = every distinct substring with ≥ 2 occurrences (overlaps counted, no non-overlap requirement, minimum count 2, case-insensitive via `DnaSequence`, ACGT-only), length ≥ max(1, minLength), ordered by smallest suffix starting with the repeat then length, positions ascending. Correct; recorded as a decision record in `Repeat_Detection.md` §2.2 (it lists every prefix/suffix of a longer repeat; `FindMaximalRepeats` is the non-redundant form). The only existing oracle was the pre-rewrite implementation (same family of reasoning, culture-sensitive `OrderBy` on suffixes) → added fully independent oracles.
- Defect (small): `FindRepeats(null, …)` surfaced as a `NullReferenceException` on enumeration (iterator) → `ArgumentNullException` at the call (also `FindLongestRepeat`).
- Reference cross-check: `scripts/analysis/generate_repeat_oracle.py` — 628 rows over 157 sequences × minLength {1,2,3,5}: brute force over all substrings == suffix array + Kasai LCP + lcp-intervals (asserted in the script); C# matches count + SHA-256 of the ordered `seq:positions` list on all rows; in-test brute force 480 random strings (ACGT/AC/A/ACG) incl. order; 100 kb existing k-mer cross-check retained; 1 Mbp random + planted 200-mer: `FindRepeats` + `FindMaximalRepeats` ≈ 4.7 s incl. the tree build, every reported repeat verified against the text.
- Call-through evaluation (task item): `RepeatFinder.FindDirectRepeats` and `SuffixTree.FindMaximalRepeatedPairs` (B05 F26) return maximal *pairs* (one result per pair of occurrences, with spacing / length caps), not strings with occurrence sets; `FindRepeats` lists non-maximal repeats too → **not switched** (semantics differ, not identical); exact difference documented. Relation tested (F30).
- Tests: `GenomicAnalyzer_Repeat_Oracle_Tests` (R1, R4, R8, R9); existing `GenomicAnalyzer_FindRepeats_Tests` (11) and `GenomicAnalyzerFindRepeatsOracleTests` unchanged and green.

### F29 — GENOMIC-REPEAT-001: `FindLongestRepeat` tie-break was the tree's child-storage order (commit 8056783e)
- Defect: the sources require "a" longest repeat, but which one depended on the Ukkonen child order (`SuffixTree.LongestRepeatedSubstring` documents "unspecified … can differ from the persistent tree"). Repro: `TTGGCACGTAACGTCTTGG` has two longest repeats (`TTGG`@{0,15}, `ACGT`@{5,10}); the tree order visits the `A` branch first.
- Fix: `FindAllLongestRepeats(DnaSequence)` (new, delegates to the canonical `SuffixTree.FindAllLongestRepeatedSubstrings`, B05: ordered by first occurrence) and `FindLongestRepeat` = its first entry → deterministic (leftmost first occurrence), identical for every tree implementation. Same `RepeatInfo` for the existing single-winner cases (M1–M5 unchanged). MCP `find_longest_repeat` text states the tie rule.
- Oracle: brute force (generate_repeat_oracle.py, all longest repeats on all 157 sequences); tests R3, R7 + MCP `FindLongestRepeat_LengthTie_ReturnsTheLeftmostFirstOccurrence`.

### F30 — GENOMIC-REPEAT-001: maximal repeats were "not implemented" (users were pointed to pair APIs) (commit 8056783e)
- Gap: `Repeat_Detection.md` §5.3 "Not implemented: maximal-repeat classification". Implementable and sourced (Gusfield 1997 §7.12; UCF slides and Abouelhoda–Kurtz–Ohlebusch 2004 as restated in search snippets): a string is a maximal repeat iff its suffix-tree node is left-diverse (internal nodes are right-maximal by construction).
- Fix: `GenomicAnalyzer.FindMaximalRepeats(DnaSequence, int minLength)` — same traversal/order as `FindRepeats`; the visitor keeps a stack of bit sets (leaf = bit of the character before its suffix, suffix 0 = sentinel bit; node = OR over its subtree, kept when ≥ 2 bits) → linear, ≤ n results, only the full node label emitted. MCP `find_repeats` gets the additive optional parameter `maximalOnly` (default false; no new tool, so no tool-count change).
- Cross-check: Python oracle (brute-force left/right diversity == SA/LCP-interval BWT-style left characters) on 628 rows; in-test brute force on 480 strings; strings equal the distinct strings of the maximal pairs of `RepeatFinder.FindDirectRepeats` (no spacing/length limits) on 240 random strings (a pair differing on both sides exists iff the occurrence set is left- and right-diverse); every longest repeat is maximal; homopolymer `AAAAA` → `A…AAAA` (text ends are distinct symbols). 1 Mbp see F28.
- Tests: R2, R5, R6, R9 + MCP `FindRepeats_MaximalOnly_ReturnsGusfieldMaximalRepeats`.

### F31 — GENOMIC-REPEAT-001: MCP documentation errors (commit 8056783e)
- `docs/mcp/tools/core/find_longest_repeat.md` / `.mcp.json` example claimed `ATGATGATG` → `ATG`@[0,3,6], length 3 (a hand-written example error: the LRS is `ATGATG`@[0,3], length 6; the unit test already asserted it). `docs/mcp/tools/analysis/find_repeats.md` claimed O(n²) adjacent-suffix enumeration (the implementation is one tree traversal, O(n + output)). Both corrected; source anchors, traceability lines (`GenomicAnalyzer.cs#L29` / `#L65`), READMEs, tool descriptions (`find_repeats`, `find_longest_repeat`) and the generated skill catalog refreshed.

### F32 — GENOMIC-SIMILARITY-001 (B06 request): private k-mer Jaccard copy
- Defect: `GenomicAnalyzer.CalculateSimilarity` (+ private `GetKmers`, a `Substring` per window into a `HashSet`, and its own intersection/union loop) was a second implementation of the k-mer Jaccard index (DUP_MAP §16; B06 `KmerAnalyzer.JaccardSimilarity` is the canonical). Value-identical, so no repro of a wrong number; duplicate only.
- Source: Jaccard 1901 / Ondov et al. 2016 (earlier fetch, Wikipedia/PMC blocked now); Mash `Sketch.cpp` + `sketches.rst` (raw.githubusercontent.com, opened), scipy 1.18.1 and sourmash 4.9.4 (installed, behaviour run).
- Cross-check: facade == canonical x 100 bit-for-bit on 600 oracle rows in both modes; the existing 13 unit tests, fuzz/property/metamorphic similarity tests (63) and the MCP tests (6) unchanged and green.
- Fix: both overloads keep the null / `kmerSize < 1` checks and return `KmerAnalyzer.JaccardSimilarity(a.Sequence, b.Sequence, k, new KmerCountingOptions(Canonical: canonical)) * PercentScale`; `GetKmers` deleted (no other caller). Verified request (B06.md lines ~108/370/536/826): **done**.
- Tests: `GenomicAnalyzer_CalculateSimilarity_Oracle_Tests` (facade == canonical x 100). Commit: 5bd3db56 (F32-F34).

### F33 — GENOMIC-SIMILARITY-001: strand-agnostic (Mash/sourmash) k-mers were unavailable
- Gap: the unit cites Mash, whose default is canonical k-mers ("ignore strandedness by using canonical k-mers ... preserved with -n"; `Sketch.cpp`: `kmer = (noncanonical || fwd <= rev) ? fwd : rev`), but only the literal mode existed: AACCCGGG vs its reverse complement CCCGGGTT, k = 3 gave 50 where Mash/sourmash give 100. The unit never stated which mode it implements (Stage A note).
- Fix (additive, backward compatible): overload `CalculateSimilarity(a, b, kmerSize, bool canonical)`; the existing 3-argument form is literal (= Python `set` = scipy `jaccard` on presence vectors = `mash -n`), unchanged. MCP `calculate_similarity` gets optional `canonical` (default false). `AcgtOnly` is not exposed: `DnaSequence` is upper-case ACGT, so the rule holds by construction. Case: folded by `DnaSequence`.
- Oracle: `scripts/analysis/generate_similarity_oracle.py` -> `TestData/Analysis/similarity_oracle.tsv`: 164 pairs (random, mutated, reverse-complement, low-complexity alphabets, 2 kbp, edge cases) x k in {1,2,3,4,5,8,21} = 2086 rows; asserts Python set == scipy `1 - jaccard(presence vectors)` (literal) and Python canonical set (Biopython `reverse_complement`) == sourmash `MinHash(n=0, scaled=1).jaccard` (canonical); 924 rows differ between modes.
- Tests: oracle literal + canonical (O1, O2), RC property (50 / 100), k larger than both (also `int.MaxValue`), lower-case input, overload validation, 300 kbp smoke; MCP `Canonical_DelegatesToGenomicAnalyzer`.
- Empty union: scipy (`jaccard([], [])` = 0 distance) and Mash (`shared == union` -> distance 0) imply J = 1, sourmash `jaccard` = 0.0; no source fixes it, library keeps 0 (documented ASM-1 / algorithm doc §5.4 #3, consistent with `KmerAnalyzer.JaccardSimilarity`).

### F34 — GENOMIC-SIMILARITY-001: MCP / skill documentation claimed a 0-1 scale
- Defect: `docs/mcp/tools/core/calculate_similarity.md` / `.mcp.json` and the `bio-alignment` skill (SKILL.md, tool-map.md, pipelines.md) stated output "0-1 scale" with example `ATGCATGC` vs `ATGCATTT` -> `0.75`; the tool returns a percentage (real value for k = 5: 2/6 -> 33.333333333333336) and its own `[Description]` said 0-100. Wrong source links (`#L238`).
- Fix: docs corrected (percent, `canonical`, example, schema max 100, line link); `hamming_distance` / `edit_distance` in the same MCP class were checked: both delegate to `ApproximateMatcher` (no re-implementation; B05 canonical). GenomicAnalyzer has no other similarity / identity / Hamming / edit helper (grep `Similarity|Hamming|Distance|Identity` finds only `CalculateSimilarity`).

### F35 — GENOMIC-TANDEM-001 (B04 request, DUP_MAP §17): `FindTandemRepeats` reported non-primitive units and was a separate O(n²·m) scan
- Defect: `GenomicAnalyzer.FindTandemRepeats(new DnaSequence("ATATATAT"), 1, 2)` returned `AT`×4@0 **and** `ATAT`×2@0 (`AAAAA`, 1, 2 → `A`×5 and `AA`×2), where the canonical `RepeatFinder.FindMicrosatellites` and every microsatellite tool (MISA "reject false type motifs [e.g. (TT)6 or (ACAC)5]", pytrf) report the primitive run once. The method was its own substring scan (`FindTandemRepeatsCore`), a third copy of the exact tandem engine next to `RepeatFinder` and `GenomeAnnotator` (DUP_MAP §17). The 2026-06 unit description ("each period interpretation is a valid tandem repeat") was refuted by the sources.
- Sources actually opened: Bannai, I, Inenaga, Nakashima, Takeda, Tsuruta, "The Runs Theorem" (arXiv:1406.0263 abstract + full PDF: Definition 1 of a run, Lemmas 3/6/7/8, Theorem 9, §4.1–4.2); MISA `misa.pl` via raw.githubusercontent.com (line 110 "reject false type motifs"); Kolpakov & Kucherov FOCS 1999 and Benson 1999 via search snippets only (publisher pages 403). Fine–Wilf: a period-p interval of length ≥ 2p with a primitive unit has smallest period p, so "maximal run with primitive unit" = "Kolpakov–Kucherov run with smallest period p".
- Reference cross-check: new `scripts/analysis/generate_tandem_oracle.py` — three independent Python methods (brute-force Kolpakov–Kucherov runs from the definition; boolean-array runs + primitivity; regex lookahead at left-maximal starts), asserted equal on 1156 rows. The old code (Python port) differed on **276/1156 rows, all by extra non-primitive units** (487 extra hits; 26 747 extras on 20 000 further random strings; 0 missing and 0 non-maximal primitive hits — so the DUP_MAP remark "not left-maximal" does not hold for the primitive hits; the defect is primitivity). New code = oracle on 1156/1156 rows = `RepeatFinder.FindMicrosatellites` on every row = in-test brute force on 1500 random strings.
- Fix: `FindTandemRepeats` validates (eager `ArgumentNullException` / `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`) and delegates to `RepeatFinder.FindMicrosatellites(seq, minUnit, maxUnit, minReps)`, mapping `MicrosatelliteResult` → `TandemRepeat`; `FindTandemRepeatsCore` removed. XML doc states the contract: maximal runs, primitive unit at the left-end phase, complete copies only, order (unit length, start), runs of different periods overlap, case-insensitive A/C/G/T. MCP `find_tandem_repeats` description + `docs/mcp/tools/analysis/find_tandem_repeats.{md,mcp.json}` (the `AA`×2 example removed) + MCP README updated.
- Tests: `GenomicAnalyzer_TandemRepeat_Oracle_Tests` (new, 22 tests: oracle 1156 rows, facade = `RepeatFinder` on every row, brute force 1500 random strings bounded/unbounded, `ATATATAT`/`AAAAAA`/partial-copy/left-end-phase/overlap/order/validation cases); `GenomicTandemFuzzTests` oracle replaced by the run definition (it replayed the old greedy scan) + primitivity added to the well-formedness check + seeded-tandem test seeds primitive roots; MCP `FindTandemRepeatsTests` (`AAAAA` -> `A`×5 only, `ATATATAT` -> `AT`×4).
- Commit: e0218cef (F35-F38).

### F36 — GENOMIC-TANDEM-001: no unit-length bound and quadratic-to-cubic complexity (1 Mbp)
- Defect: the old scan was O(n²·m) with `m` up to n/minReps (no `maxUnitLength` existed): a 2 kb sequence took seconds, 1 Mbp hours. Delegating alone gives O(n) per unit length, still O(n²/k) ≈ 5·10¹¹ comparisons for the default "no upper bound" on 1 Mbp (500 000 unit lengths × 1 Mbp).
- Source: runs theorem (Bannai et al. 2017, opened; §3 Lemma 7, §4.1 stack computation of the longest Lyndon words, §4.2 LCE extension, Theorem 16 LCE in O(1) from suffix array + RMQ): all runs of a string (< n of them) from two Lyndon arrays.
- Fix: additive `FindTandemRepeats(sequence, minUnit, minReps, maxUnit)` (+ optional MCP `maxUnitLength`); `Analysis/MaximalRepetitions.cs` (internal): both orders (≺1 with the end marker larger, as in the paper), next-smaller-suffix monotonic stack, O(1) LCE from the existing `SequenceComplexity.BuildSuffixArray`/`BuildLcpArray` (suffix array + Kasai LCP + block sparse-table RMQ, of the text and its reverse), candidate kept when it spans ≥ 2p, de-duplicated, sorted by (period, start). `FindTandemRepeats` switches to it when more than 256 unit lengths are admissible (break-even ≈ 175 unit lengths at 1 Mbp: scan ≈ 17 ms per unit length, run enumeration ≈ 3 s). Results are identical in both regimes (differential tests).
- Reference cross-check: run enumeration = oracle on 1156/1156 rows; = brute force (every interval, its smallest period by definition) on **all** 62 000 strings over {A,C} ≤ 14 and {A,C,G} ≤ 9, 1200 random/structured strings (Fibonacci, Thue–Morse, periodic powers), and = the per-unit-length scan on 60 low-entropy 2.5 kb strings with planted repeats of unit length 1–40. 1 Mbp (Debug build): units 2–60 ≥ 5 copies 1 s; unbounded unit length 3 s; homopolymer + period-8 string (worst case for the prefix doubling) 6 s.
- Tests: `RunsEngine_*` ×5, `WideUnitRange_SwitchesToRunsEngine_WithIdenticalResult`, `OneMegabase_*` ×3, `MaxUnitLength_*` ×2 (+ MCP `FindTandemRepeats_MaxUnitLength_RestrictsUnitLength`).

### F37 — GENOMIC-TANDEM-001 (B04 request): algorithm docs sent approximate tandem repeats to external tools
- Defect: `docs/algorithms/Genomic_Analysis/Tandem_Repeat_Detection.md` (§5.3 "Not implemented: … users should rely on external tools (e.g. Tandem Repeats Finder)") and the REP-TANDEM-001 doc (§5.2/5.3/5.4 "brute-force", "delegation is a cross-batch request") were stale: approximate tandem repeats are implemented in-repo (`RepeatFinder.FindApproximateTandemRepeats`, TRF 4.10.0 model, B04 F14–F18/F40–F46; MCP `find_approximate_tandem_repeats`, `mask_approximate_tandem_repeats`, names verified by grep in `AnalysisTools.cs`).
- Decision on a `GenomicAnalyzer` pass-through for approximate repeats: not added. The unit's reference (Benson 1999) and contract describe the exact detector; the approximate detector is a canonical public API with a different result type (consensus, copies, % matches/indels, score) and parameter set, so a wrapper would be pure duplication.
- Fix: both algorithm docs rewritten (`Genomic_Analysis/Tandem_Repeat_Detection.md` fully: definition, runs, engines, complexity, oracles, approximate-repeat pointer; `Repeat_Analysis/Tandem_Repeat_Detection.md` §1/3/4/5/6 corrected, edited under the "finished batch" doc rule), TestSpec, Evidence, `docs/Validation/reports/GENOMIC-TANDEM-001.md` and `docs/mcp/tools/analysis/find_tandem_repeats.{md,mcp.json}` (approximate-repeat see-also, complexity).

### F38 — GENOMIC-TANDEM-001: independent oracle for the contract (tests)
- The unit's previous tests only locked examples with a primitive unit (they could not see the defect) and the fuzz oracle replayed the implementation's own greedy scan. Added: `scripts/analysis/generate_tandem_oracle.py` + `TestData/Analysis/tandem_oracle.tsv` (embedded resource, 1156 rows), in-test brute force on the run definition, exhaustive small-string comparison of the two engines. EMBOSS `etandem` 6.6.0 (installed) was run on 400 random cases as an informational third reference: 297 agree, the 103 differences are etandem's own diagonal-score model (global score threshold, rotation phase), so it is not an exact-run oracle (recorded in the Evidence file). `pip install pytrf` was declined by the sandbox; the pytrf/MISA comparison of the canonical scan stays B04 F5 (3099/3132 and 2752/3132, differences = two documented conventions).

### F39 — ALIGN-GLOBAL-001 / ALIGN-LOCAL-001 (audit A1-1): empty/null contract of the string overloads
- Defect: `GlobalAlign(string,string)` and its cancellable twin returned `AlignmentResult.Empty` (score 0, no rows) for an empty side and for null, while the `DnaSequence` overload, `GlobalAlignAffine(string)` and the Hirschberg base case return the Needleman-Wunsch all-gap alignment; `LocalAlign(string)` returned the Global-tagged `Empty` (coordinates 0) instead of the documented tagged-Local result with coordinates -1; `GlobalAlignAffine("", "")` returned `Empty` (End 0) while every other path gives End = length - 1 = -1; linear string overloads silently accepted null while the affine ones threw. Repro: `GlobalAlign("", "ACGT")` -> score 0 / no rows (correct: `----`/`ACGT`, 4·d); `CallVariants(empty, ACGT)` therefore reported no variants.
- Sources actually used: Needleman & Wunsch 1970 (border initialization F(0,j) = d·j; matrix definition), Durbin et al. 1998 §2.3 (WebSearch snippets); **tool oracle impossible**: Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner.score("", "ACGT")` raises `ValueError: sequence has zero length`, parasail 1.3.4 `nw_trace` raises `_s1Len must be > 0`, EMBOSS `needle` 6.6.0 ("Sequence ... has zero length, ignored"), scikit-bio 0.7.4 `pair_align` ("Sequence 1 has a length of zero"), `Bio.pairwise2` returns `[]` (all executed 2026-11-18) - the audit statement "Biopython score = -8 for CCAC/''" is not reproducible in 1.88; the contract is defined by the recurrence. Non-empty behaviour of the same code paths: differential run vs Biopython 1.88 + parasail 1.3.4 on 4090 random/edge rows (6 aligner kinds, 10 schemes, alphabets A/AC/ACGT, lengths 0..30): **0 score mismatches**; empty rows checked against the closed form (`d·len`, affine `o + len·e`, both empty 0) and row/coordinate shape.
- Fix: one contract for every overload (string, `DnaSequence`, cancellable, linear, affine, local): null -> `ArgumentNullException`; empty is not special-cased (global: all-gap NW alignment, both empty score 0 / empty rows / coordinates 0, 0, -1, -1; local: `("", "", 0, Local, -1, -1, -1, -1)`); `AlignmentResult.Empty` is no longer returned by any aligner (still the default for statistics/format helpers). Additive `SemiGlobalAlign(string, string, ...)` overload with the same contract. XML docs (`SequenceAligner`, `AlignmentResult`), `Global_Alignment_Needleman_Wunsch.md` §3.1/3.3/4/6.1 (two contradicting rows removed), `Local_...` §3.1-3.3/6.1, TestSpecs ALIGN-GLOBAL-001 (A1/A2/G6), ALIGN-LOCAL-001 (M4), Evidence (ALIGN-GLOBAL-001, honest oracle statement), the three unit reports (round-1 correction notes).
- Callers: `VariantCaller.CallVariants` (B21, via `GlobalAlign(string)`) now reports the insertions/deletions of an empty-vs-non-empty comparison instead of silently nothing (correct per the alignment; B21 tests green); `ChromosomeAnalyzer` monomer identity and `ComparativeGenomics.BestGappedFragmentMatch` (guards empty before the call) and `ProbeDesigner` (local, non-empty) are unaffected. MCP `global_align`/`local_align` keep their own `ArgumentException` for empty input (schema validation layer, tests unchanged).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_GlobalAlign_Tests` `EmptyInput_AllOverloads_AreTheAllGapNeedlemanWunschAlignment` x4 (all four overloads), `EmptyInput_LinearScore_IsGapTimesLength` x3 (incl. CCAC/"" gap -2 = -8, affine GapOpen 0 equal), `NullString_ThrowsArgumentNullException_OnEveryStringOverload`, `Affine_EmptyInputs_BorderValues` (both-empty coordinates = linear); `SequenceAligner_LocalAlign_Tests` `LocalAlign_EmptyInput_AllOverloads_ReturnTaggedLocalEmptyResult` x3, `LocalAlign_NullString_ThrowsArgumentNullException`; fuzz (`GlobalAlign_RawStringEmptyOrNull_FollowsTheTypedContract`, `LocalAlign_RawStringEmptyOrNull_FollowsTheTypedContract`) and mutation test re-locked. Also fixed a stale Metamorphic test (`AlignmentStatistics_AreSymmetric`, deferred tier): it compared `GapOpens` (a gap-RUN count of the one co-optimal alignment) between (a,b) and (b,a), but the fixed diagonal > up > left tie order is not transpose-symmetric (8 vs 9 runs on a 40/10 nt pair with equal scores and tallies) - `GapOpens` excluded with the reason in the test.
- Commit: see worklist entry A1-1.

### F40 — ALIGN-SEMI-001 (audit A1-4): semi-global `Start/EndPosition2` were the full-input bounds, not the fitted interval
- Defect: `SemiGlobalAlign`/`SemiGlobalAlignAffine` returned `StartPosition2 = 0`, `EndPosition2 = reference.Length - 1` whatever was fitted, so callers had to recount gaps in the rows to find where the query landed (`Semi_Global_Alignment.md` §5.3 "Intentionally simplified", MCP `semi_global_align` startPosition2/endPosition2 carried no information). Repro: `SemiGlobalAlign("GATTACA", "CCGATTTACAGG", (2,-3,..,-2))` -> `--GA-TTACA--`/`CCGATTTACAGG`, coordinates (0,6,0,11); the fitted interval is ref[2..9].
- Sources: Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner(mode='global', end_insertion_score=0)` alignments (`align` enumeration, leading/trailing gap-in-target columns = span of `aligned`/`coordinates`), parasail 1.3.4 `sg_dx_trace` (`end_ref`; its `cigar.beg_ref` includes the free prefix and is not comparable), Rosalind SIMS definition (fitting alignment) - README of parasail already opened in F3.
- Oracle numbers (2026-11-18): 4090 random/edge rows, 1382 semi-global (linear + affine, 10 schemes incl. GapOpen 0 / GapExtend 0, alphabets A/AC/ACGT, lengths 0..30): reported interval in the set of intervals of ALL Biopython co-optimal alignments in 823/823 fully enumerated rows (32 capped at 3000 alignments, score equal), parasail score equal in every row, `Global(query, ref[Start2..End2]).Score` = fitting score in every row, interval = span between the query row's leading/trailing gap columns; 0 failures.
- Fix: the traceback records the DP row-0 column where the path starts (linear `Traceback`, `AffineAlignCore`); `StartPosition2` = that column, `EndPosition2` = chosen end column - 1, `StartPosition1/EndPosition1` = 0 / m - 1 unchanged; an empty fitted interval (empty query or query aligned entirely against gaps) has `End2 = Start2 - 1`. Ties unchanged (first maximal last-row column, diagonal > up > left, M > X > Y), so rows are unchanged. XML docs (`AlignmentResult`, both aligners), `Semi_Global_Alignment.md` (§3.2/3.3/5.2/5.3/6.1/6.2), TestSpec ALIGN-SEMI-001 (reference equivalents, REF, COORD-1..4), Evidence INV-6, MCP descriptions/outputs/examples of `semi_global_align(_affine)` (`docs/mcp/tools/alignment/*`, tool `[Description]`, `gen-catalog.py` regenerated, `--check` fresh), unit report note.
- Tests: `SemiGlobalAlign_FittedInterval_MatchesBiopythonCoordinatesAndParasailEndRef` x8 (unique-interval cases, linear + affine), `SemiGlobalAlign_TiedFittings_ReportOneCoOptimalInterval` x3 (Biopython sets (0,-1)...(4,3) / (0..4,8) / (1,2)(3,4)(5,6)), `SemiGlobalAlign_EmptyFittedInterval_Coordinates`, `SemiGlobalAlign_FittedInterval_IsBruteForceOptimalSubstring` (600 pairs, independent brute-force over all substrings, linear + affine), the string-overload contract test; reference tests now lock Start2/End2 (2/9, 2/5, 0/3, 0/3, 2/9); fuzz S-INV-05 re-derived (interval from the gap columns).
- Commit: see worklist entry A1-4.

### F41 — ALIGN-GLOBAL/LOCAL/SEMI-001 (audit A1-2): no substitution-matrix alignment, no fractional gap scores
- Defect: all six aligners scored with the scalar `ScoringMatrix(Match, Mismatch, GapOpen, GapExtend)` (ints); `SubstitutionMatrix` (BLOSUM62/PAM250/EDNAFULL) only fed `CalculateStatistics`/`FormatAlignment`, so EMBOSS needle/water, Biopython `PairwiseAligner(substitution_matrix=...)` and parasail protein alignment with `-gapopen 10 -gapextend 0.5` could not be reproduced (the `ScoringMatrix` XML even said "not representable with integers"). Minimal repro: `GlobalAlignAffine("MVLSPADKTNVKAAWGKVG...", "MVHLTPEEKSAVTALWGKVN...")` with BLOSUM62 + gapopen 10 / gapextend 0.5 had no API.
- Sources actually opened: EMBOSS 6.6 `nucleus/embaln.c` (`embAlignPathCalcWithEndGapPenalties`, raw.githubusercontent.com), needle/water 6.6.0 binaries (`-gapopen G` = cost of a gap of length 1, G + (k-1)E for length k; needle default `-endweight N` = end gaps FREE on both sequences, so the global oracle uses `-endweight Y -endopen G -endextend E`), Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner`, parasail 1.3.4 matrices `blosum62`/`pam250`/`nuc44`.
- Reference cross-check (`scripts/alignment/generate_matrix_align_oracle.py`, 331 random protein/DNA/IUPAC-DNA pairs x global|local|semi x linear|affine = 1 986 rows, integer and fractional gap scores 0.5/0.1/0.25/0.3/0.75/1.5/2.5...): Biopython = Seqeron on 1 986/1 986 (1e-6); parasail on 616/616 integer-gap rows; EMBOSS needle/water on 1 103/1 121 (18 EMBOSS-internal disagreements: 14 needle rows lower than the optimum because embaln.c opens a last-row/column end gap only from the match matrix, never higher; water 3 lower + 1 higher on EDNAFULL, not analysed further; Biopython/parasail agree with Seqeron on all). HBA_HUMAN/HBB_HUMAN, EBLOSUM62, gapopen 10 / gapextend 0.5: needle `-endweight Y` 292.5, water 293.5, linear 0.5: 366.5.
- Fix (additive; every existing signature, behaviour and tie order kept): `MatrixScoring(SubstitutionMatrix, double GapOpen, double GapExtend)` + `MatrixScoring.Emboss(matrix, gapopen = 10, gapextend = 0.5)`; `GlobalAlign`/`GlobalAlignAffine`/`LocalAlign`/`LocalAlignAffine`/`SemiGlobalAlign`/`SemiGlobalAlignAffine` overloads for `string`, `ProteinSequence`, `DnaSequence`; `AlignmentResult.ExactScore` (real score; `Score` = rounded). No second DP: a private `PairScoring` (exact integer model "score = k/scale", scale = smallest integer making the gaps integral, 2 for 0.5) replaces `ScoringMatrix` inside `LinearAlignCore`/`LocalAlignCore`/`AffineAlignCore`/`Traceback`/`TracebackLocal` (6 `c1 == c2 ? Match : Mismatch` -> `scoring.Sub(c1, c2)`), the scalar path keeps scale 1. Validation: residue outside the matrix alphabet, gap > 0 / NaN / finer than 1e-6, integer-range overflow -> `ArgumentException`. MCP: `matrix` + fractional `gapOpen`/`gapExtend` on the six tools, `exactScore` in the DTO (additive); docs/Evidence/TestSpecs/algorithm docs/MCP tool docs/catalog updated. `SubstitutionMatrix` got `Contains`, `MaxAbsScore`, `IndexOf`, `ScoreAt`.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_MatrixScoring_Tests` (14: oracle scores vs Biopython/parasail on all rows, EMBOSS agreement with the documented quirk, independent re-scoring + coordinates, HBA/HBB, gap-open convention, 6 aligners x 400 pairs identical to the scalar aligners row for row, overloads, validation, empty inputs), `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests.MatrixAlignTests` (9).

### F42 — ALIGN-STATS-001 (audit A1-9): BLAST integer-rounded percent identity and BLAST positives were declared "Not implemented"
- Defect: `AlignmentStatistics` only had full-precision percentages; BLAST's pairwise report prints integer percentages (`Identities = 12/30 (40%)`) with a rule (round half up, never 100 unless every column matches) that a plain `Math.Round(Identity)` violates (199/200 -> BLAST 99, rounding 100), and BLAST "Positives" count identical X/X pairs that the EMBOSS similarity count does not.
- Sources actually opened: NCBI C++ toolkit `align_format_util.cpp` (`GetPercentMatch`), `showalign.cpp` (`s_DisplayIdentityInfo`, `x_FillIdentityInfo`), local BLAST+ 2.12.0 `blastn`/`blastp` (tabular `pident` = 3 decimals).
- Reference cross-check (`scripts/alignment/generate_blast_stats_oracle.py`): 320 random best HSPs (160 blastn, 160 blastp, 24 with 99.5 <= pident < 100 shown as 99%) as tabular `qseq sseq length nident mismatch gapopen gaps positive pident` and pairwise text: all counts, `positive`, the three integer percentages, `pident` text and the whole report line equal.
- Fix: `AlignmentStatistics.BlastPercent`, `BlastPercentIdentity/Positives/Gaps`, `BlastTabularPidentText`, `ToBlastString(protein)` and the new `BlastPositives` count (additive trailing record member), `CalculateStatisticsCore` fills it; MCP `alignment_statistics` returns the BLAST fields.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_BlastStatistics_Tests` (oracle 320 rows, `GetPercentMatch` table, report format, X/X positives), `MatrixAlignTests.AlignmentStatistics_BlastReportFields`.
- Commit: see worklist entry A1-9.

### F43 — ALIGN-STATS-001 (audit A1-9): no statistics of an alignment of more than two sequences
- Defect: `MultipleAlignmentResult` had rows, consensus and sum-of-pairs score only; no per-sequence identity/similarity/gap statistics, pairwise identity matrix, per-column conservation or gap fraction ("EMBOSS's multi-sequence (>2) consensus statistics: Not implemented").
- Sources actually opened: EMBOSS 6.6 `emboss/infoalign.c` (`infoalign_Compare`), `emboss/distmat.c` (`distmat_calc_match`, `distmat_uncorrected`, `distmat_JukesCantor`, output x100), 6.6.0 binaries `infoalign`/`distmat`; ClustalW 2.1 `-convert -output=clustal` (conservation line; strong/weak groups); Biopython 1.88 `DistanceCalculator._pairwise` (source inspected; in 1.88 `SummaryInfo` only has `get_column`, the old `pos_specific_score_matrix`/`information_content` are gone).
- Reference cross-check (`scripts/alignment/generate_msa_stats_oracle.py`, 150 random gapped DNA/IUPAC and protein MSAs): infoalign 3 268 per-row records (explicit reference and consensus), distmat 600 matrices (uncorrected + Jukes-Cantor, gap weight 0/0.5; two decimals, EMBOSS single precision), ClustalW 151 conservation lines (DNA only `*`; strong/weak groups reproduced), Biopython `DistanceCalculator('identity')` 77 matrices (it compares characters with `skip_letters = ()`, so both-gap columns count as identities): all equal. ClustalW's own `-type=auto` guess was not a simple fraction (4/151 DNA MSAs typed protein): the residue type is explicit or the EMBOSS first-row rule.
- Fix: new `MultipleAlignmentStatistics` (Analysis project because the reference of `infoalign` is the EMBOSS-`cons` consensus, taken by calling `MotifFinder.GenerateEmbossConsensus`, not re-implemented; `MotifFinder.IsEmbossNucleotideSequence` made `internal` for the type rule, B05 file, one word): `CalculateSequenceStatistics` (infoalign), `CalculateDistanceMatrix` (distmat), `CalculatePairwiseIdentityMatrix`, `ClustalConservationLine`, `Calculate` (columns, gap percent, fully conserved/gap-free columns, mean pairwise identity); MCP `multiple_alignment_statistics`, `alignment_distance_matrix` (Analysis server, 495 tools).
- Tests: `MultipleAlignmentStatistics_Tests` (oracle: infoalign, distmat, ClustalW, Biopython identity + hand examples + validation), `Seqeron.Mcp.Analysis.Tests.MultipleAlignmentStatisticsToolTests` (4) + the tool-registration contract.
- Not implemented (documented): distmat Kimura/Tamura/Tajima-Nei/Jin-Nei and `-ambiguous`/`-position`.
