### F44 — ALIGN-SEMI-001 (audit A1-3): overlap and all-ends-free semi-global variants (`EndGaps`)
- Defect: only the fitting member of the family existed (`Semi_Global_Alignment.md` §5.3 "Implementation Status: Simplified", "Not implemented: overlap and fully ends-free"; TestSpec "No other variant implementations"); overlap (suffix-prefix, either orientation), sequence 2 fitted in sequence 1, all four ends free (EMBOSS needle default `-endweight N`, Rosalind SMGB) and every other combination of the four end gaps could not be computed. Repro: overlap of `GGGGACGTAC` and `ACGTACTTTT` (match 1 / mismatch −1 / gap −1) had no API (`SemiGlobalAlign` forces a free sequence-2 flank only: score 1 instead of 6).
- Sources actually used: Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner` (`left/right_insertion_score`, `left/right_deletion_score`, `end_gap_score`), parasail 1.3.4 (`nw`, `sg`, `sg_qb/qe/db/de/qx/dx`, `sg_qb_db/qb_de/qe_db/qe_de`; flag mapping found by exhaustive search of the flag set reproducing each variant: `_qb` = FreeStart1, `_qe` = FreeEnd1, `_db` = FreeStart2, `_de` = FreeEnd2), EMBOSS needle 6.6.0 default `-endweight N`, Durbin et al. 1998 §2.3 / Gusfield 1997 (WebSearch snippets), Rosalind SIMS/SMGB (as in F3).
- Reference cross-check (`scripts/alignment/generate_endgaps_oracle.py`, 3 590 rows = 70 random/dovetail/containment DNA pairs x 16 policies x linear/affine x random schemes (2 240 scalar incl. GapOpen 0/GapExtend 0, alphabets A/AC/ACGT) + 1 350 BLOSUM62/EDNAFULL rows with fractional gaps): Biopython `align().score` = Seqeron 3 590/3 590; returned alignment in Biopython's co-optimal set 2 050/2 050 (rows with <= 40 co-optimal alignments, adjacent opposite gap columns order-normalised); independent rescoring + exhaustive enumeration of every alignment (120 pairs x 16 policies x linear/affine) equal. parasail: the 6 exact variants (`nw`, `sg_qb`, `sg_db`, `sg_qx`, `sg_dx`, `sg_qb_db`) equal on all of 1 680 integer rows; the 6 variants with a free END flag (`sg`, `sg_qe`, `sg_de`, `sg_qb_de`, `sg_qe_db`, `sg_qe_de`) are lower on 23 rows, never higher — parasail does not consider the border end cells (m,0)/(0,n) (an exhaustive reference skipping them reproduces parasail 300/300). EMBOSS needle default (= `EndGaps.All`, 287 rows): 277 equal, 10 lower, 0 higher (embaln.c end-gap quirk, F41). **Biopython 1.88 quirk found:** `PairwiseAligner.score()` disagrees with its own `align().score` on 25 degenerate rows (one sequence entirely against gaps; `score('AC','T')` = −2 with `left_deletion_score = right_insertion_score = 0` while the alignment `AC-`/`--T` it returns scores 0); the oracle is `align().score`, which equals the exhaustive optimum on every row.
- Fix (additive): `[Flags] enum EndGaps { None, FreeStart1, FreeEnd1, FreeStart2, FreeEnd2, Fitting, FittingSequence2, OverlapSuffix1Prefix2, OverlapSuffix2Prefix1, All }` (Infrastructure/AlignmentTypes.cs); overloads `SemiGlobalAlign`/`SemiGlobalAlignAffine`(string | DnaSequence, ..., `ScoringMatrix?`, `EndGaps`) and (string | ProteinSequence | DnaSequence, ..., `MatrixScoring`, `EndGaps`). No second DP: `LinearAlignCore(fitting: bool)` -> `LinearAlignCore(EndGaps)`, `AffineAlignCore(type, EndGaps)`, `Traceback(EndGaps)`; a free start sets the matching border to 0 (`F(i,0)` / `F(0,j)`, affine `M(i,0)` / `M(0,j)`), the end cell is the first maximum of the free last row (smallest j) then of the free last column (smallest i), traceback stops at row 0 / column 0 when that start is free and the unconsumed flanks are emitted against gaps. `EndGaps.Fitting` is the previous behaviour bit-for-bit (rows, coordinates, tie order), `EndGaps.None` equals `GlobalAlign`/`GlobalAlignAffine` (tagged Global). Coordinates generalised: Start/End of both sequences = consumed intervals (free flanks excluded; fitting results unchanged). MCP: additive `endGaps` parameter on `semi_global_align` / `semi_global_align_affine` (preset names or comma list of the four flags; default = Fitting), descriptions/docs/catalog updated. Docs: `Semi_Global_Alignment.md` (status Simplified -> Production, §2.2/2.5/3/4/5.3/5.4/6), `Global_Alignment_Needleman_Wunsch.md` §5.3, TestSpec ALIGN-SEMI-001 (EG-1..10), Evidence, reports.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_EndGaps_Tests` (14: oracle score/parasail/EMBOSS/co-optimal membership/rescoring+coordinates on 3 590 rows, brute-force over every alignment x 16 policies, Fitting = classic and None = global row for row incl. DNA/protein overloads, mirror symmetry, monotonicity in the free flags, overlap example with Biopython rows, zero-length overlap, empty/null), `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests.EndGapsAlignTests` (8).
- Commit: see worklist entry A1-3.

### F45 — ALIGN-GLOBAL/LOCAL/SEMI-001 (audit A1-5): arbitrary gap-cost functions (Waterman-Smith-Beyer)
- Defect: only linear and affine gap models existed (`Local_Alignment_Smith_Waterman.md` §5.3 "Not implemented: arbitrary (non-affine) W_k"); concave (logarithmic, square-root), convex, capped or step-wise gap-cost functions could not be used. Repro: `w(k) = -(2 + 3 ln k)` global alignment of `AAAATTTT`/`AAAACCCCTTTT` had no API.
- Sources actually used: Waterman, Smith & Beyer (1976) and Gusfield 1997 §11.2 (WebSearch snippets), Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner` with a callable `gap_score(position, length)` (selects "Waterman-Smith-Beyer global/local alignment algorithm"; end-gap properties are ignored for a callable: identical scores with `end_insertion_score = 0`).
- Reference cross-check (`scripts/alignment/generate_general_gap_oracle.py`, 1 440 rows): 960 random global/local pairs (8 families: linear, affine, log, sqrt, quadratic, capped, step, two-slope; scalar / BLOSUM62 / EDNAFULL; lengths <= 26 with planted long indels): Biopython (`align().score` and `score()` agree) = an independent three-state implementation = Seqeron general program and public API on 960/960 (1e-8); 480 ends-free rows vs the independent implementation; an exhaustive enumeration of every alignment (60 pairs, 6 gap functions incl. a non-monotone one, 16 policies; local on 15 pairs over all substring pairs) equals the program. **Model fixed by the oracle:** every maximal gap run is charged w(k) once; the textbook single-matrix recurrence max_k H(i-k,j) + w(k) lets a gap be split into cheaper adjacent pieces and disagrees with Biopython on 12 % (log) / 57 % (quadratic) / 27 % (step) of 200 random global pairs per family.
- Fix (additive): `GlobalAlignGeneralGap` / `LocalAlignGeneralGap` / `SemiGlobalAlignGeneralGap(..., EndGaps)` (`SequenceAligner.GeneralGap.cs`, `SequenceAligner` made `partial`) with `Func<int,double> gapScore` (score of a gap of length k, negative = penalty) and `ScoringMatrix` or `SubstitutionMatrix` scoring, string/DnaSequence/ProteinSequence overloads; three-state WSB program with back-pointers, O(m n (m+n)) time, O(m n) memory, `w` evaluated once per k = 1..max(m,n) (NaN/+inf rejected, -inf allowed); linear/affine functions (w(k) = w(1) + (k-1)e, e <= 0, w(1) - e <= 0, representable) are detected and run on the existing O(m n) linear/affine/local programs (same score and rows as calling them directly). Docs: `Global_Alignment_Needleman_Wunsch.md` §5.5, `Local_Alignment_Smith_Waterman.md` §5.3 ("Not implemented" removed), TestSpecs, Evidence.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_GeneralGap_Tests` (10: oracle score general program + API on 1 440 rows, rescoring/coordinates, affine/linear fast path row for row incl. EndGaps, fractional BLOSUM62 affine function = `MatrixScoring.Emboss`, brute force, convex-not-split, evaluation count/validation, empty contract, overload equality).
- Commit: see worklist entry A1-5.

### F46 — ALIGN-GLOBAL/LOCAL/SEMI-001 (audit A1-6): O(m n) memory, no guard; exact linear-space alignment
- Defect: `LinearAlignCore` / `AffineAlignCore` / `LocalAlignCore` allocate `new int[m+1, n+1]` (1 matrix linear, 3 matrices affine = 12 bytes per cell) with no size check: 20 kb x 20 kb affine = 4.8 GB, raw `OutOfMemoryException` at about 46 k x 46 k; the algorithm docs declared `O(mn)` and "does not implement the linear-space refinements". Linear-space Hirschberg existed only internally for the linear global model (`GlobalAlignLinearSpace`, MSA / anchors) with different ties from `GlobalAlign`.
- Sources actually used: Hirschberg 1975, Myers & Miller 1988 (CABIOS 4:11; PMID 3382986 record), Huang & Miller 1991 (Adv Appl Math 12:337; DOI record), SIM/Huang-Hardison-Miller 1990 descriptions (WebSearch snippets; the papers were not opened); oracles: EMBOSS 6.6.0 `stretcher` (Myers-Miller global, end gaps penalised, gap = gapopen + (k-1) gapextend) and `matcher` (SIM local, first alternative) run locally; Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner`, parasail 1.3.4.
- Fix (additive; every existing output unchanged): (1) `SequenceAligner.LinearSpaceCellThreshold` = 25 000 000 cells (same limit as `AnchorBasedAligner`): above it every pairwise aligner — `GlobalAlign`, `GlobalAlignAffine`, `LocalAlign`, `LocalAlignAffine`, `SemiGlobalAlign`, `SemiGlobalAlignAffine`, all 16 `EndGaps` policies, scalar and `MatrixScoring` overloads, the linear/affine fast path of the general-gap aligners, the cancellation overload — switches automatically to the linear-space program; (2) explicit public `GlobalAlignLinearSpace`, `GlobalAlignAffineLinearSpace`, `LocalAlignLinearSpace`, `LocalAlignAffineLinearSpace`, `SemiGlobalAlignLinearSpace(…, EndGaps)`, `SemiGlobalAlignAffineLinearSpace(…, EndGaps)` (string / DnaSequence scalar, string / ProteinSequence / DnaSequence `MatrixScoring`, optional `maxFullMatrixCells`); (3) **the result is identical to the full-matrix aligner — score, rows, coordinates, type, tie behaviour**: the midpoint of Hirschberg / Myers-Miller is found by carrying, through the forward pass, the vertex (column and Gotoh state) at which the full-matrix traceback crosses the middle row, so the divide-and-conquer reproduces the very path the full-matrix program returns (lemma + proof sketch in `Linear_Space_Alignment.md` §2.3); sub-problems are "global alignment from arrival state s0 to forced state s1" (Myers-Miller type-1/type-2 midpoints), solved at the leaves by the existing `AffineAlignCore` / `LinearAlignCore` (new optional `startState` / `endState` / `forceFullMatrix`, no second DP); local and ends-free: an `O(n)`-memory locate pass (end cell by the core's own rules + carried traceback start vertex) then the global solve of the rectangle (Huang-Miller / SIM scheme); the old private Hirschberg (`HirschbergAlign`, `LinearLastRow`) is replaced by this one engine (MSA center-star and `AnchorBasedAligner` keep their `GlobalAlignLinearSpace(string, string, ScoringMatrix, maxFullMatrixCells)` call; MSA pair scores use `LinearScoreOnly`). (4) Memory guards: `GeneralGapMaxCells` = 4 M cells (non-affine `w(k)` needs 33 bytes per cell and O(m n (m+n)) time; clear `ArgumentException`; linear/affine `w` are delegated and unlimited), `ProfileMatrixMaxCells` = 50 M cells for the progressive / iterative / consistency profile DP (clear `ArgumentException`; the exact center-star has no matrix). (5) MCP: automatic (no schema change), memory / limit text in the six pairwise tool descriptions and `general_gap_align`, tool docs + README.
- Measured (Release, 1 core, random DNA with 10 % edits, BLAST DNA scoring): 20 000 x 19 994 affine global 16.0 s / 111 MB peak (full matrices 4.8 GB), local 27.8 s / 118 MB, fitting 28.3 s / 113 MB, linear global 6.0 s / 57 MB; **100 000 x 100 000 (10^10 cells) affine global 391.7 s / 185 MB peak (full 120 GB), linear global 153.9 s / 73 MB**; 10 000 x 10 000: full-matrix 2.6 s (1.18 GB) vs linear-space 3.3 s (103 MB) affine global, local 3.1 s vs 6.4 s, linear 1.0 s vs 1.5 s.
- Reference cross-check: linear-space == full-matrix core row for row (score, both rows, coordinates, type) on 1 530 + 2 800 + 420 random / tie-rich (alphabets A, AC, ACGT; zero gap scores) / long-indel / BLOSUM62 + EDNAFULL fractional-gap cases with base cells 1…100 (all 16 end-gap policies, local, linear + affine), on 2.6-3 kb pairs with planted indels (default base and 50 000-cell base), and through the automatic switch on a 5 300 x 5 200 pair (all six aligners, protein BLOSUM62); Biopython 1.88 scores on 1 280 pairs (global / local / fitting / 9 other policies, linear + affine, ties, long indels) = linear-space scores, parasail 1.3.4 equal where a variant exists (≥ 500 rows), every alignment re-scores independently and is one of Biopython's co-optimal alignments (≥ 400 rows with ≤ 40 co-optimal); EMBOSS `stretcher` 160 rows: score equal, rows equal wherever Biopython finds a unique optimum (49); EMBOSS `matcher` 160 rows: score equal, rows and 1-based coordinates equal where unique (93); large pairs (DNA, planted indels, EDNAFULL, gapopen 16 / gapextend 4): `stretcher` = `GlobalAlignAffine` and `matcher` = `LocalAlignAffine` scores equal for 100, 500, 1 000, 2 000, 5 000, 20 000 and 50 000 nt (14/14; 50 kb: 191 903 / 192 525).
- Tie behaviour: identical to the full-matrix aligner (diagonal > up > left; M > X > Y; first maximal end cell); EMBOSS stretcher/matcher break ties differently (identical rows in 55 / 160 and 99 / 160 oracle rows, always the same score and always where the optimum is unique).
- Not done / not applicable (with reason): a linear-space `*GeneralGap` (a non-affine `w(k)` has no Hirschberg split: the gap state is not Markov); the profile-profile MSA DP (no published linear-space variant keeping the T-Coffee/sum-of-pairs column scores) - both get a documented limit + clear error instead of OOM; SIM's k-best non-intersecting alignments (EMBOSS matcher `-alternatives`) are a different feature (top-k local alignments), not memory.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_LinearSpace_Tests` ×20 (forced-recursion differential over all families/policies, matrix scoring, kb pairs, automatic switch above 25 M cells, memory (allocation < 1/8 of the matrices), cancellation, Biopython/parasail/stretcher/matcher oracle `TestData/Alignment/linear_space_oracle.tsv`, `scripts/alignment/generate_linear_space_oracle.py`, validation, empty inputs), `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests.LinearSpaceAlignTests` ×3; existing `AnchorBasedAligner_Tests.GlobalAlignLinearSpace_*` now also assert the rows (identical to NW).
- Commit: `fdc6c83a`.

### F47 — ALIGN-LOCAL-001 (audit A1-7): one local alignment only; no Waterman-Eggert suboptimal alignments, no co-optimal enumeration
- Defect: `LocalAlign` / `LocalAlignAffine` return the single optimal alignment; `Local_Alignment_Smith_Waterman.md` §6.2 ("not the full set of equally optimal tracebacks"; "SIM's top-k non-intersecting alignments are not reproduced"), `Global_...` §6.2 and `Multiple_Sequence_Alignment.md` §5.3 ("single best SW alignment per pair instead of Lalign's top-N non-intersecting alignments") declared it. Repro: 3 x `ACGTAC` in sequence 1 vs 1 x `ACGTAC` in sequence 2 (+2/-3, gap -2): Smith-Waterman reports 1 copy; EMBOSS `matcher -alternatives 3` and FASTA `lalign` report 3 (12, 12, 12).
- Sources actually used: Waterman & Eggert 1987 (J Mol Biol 197:723; WebSearch record, paper not opened); **EMBOSS 6.6.0 `emboss/matcher.c`** (raw.githubusercontent.com `kimrutherford/EMBOSS` mirror, opened: `matcher_Sim` "Smith Waterman Eggert local alignment", `MATCHERDIAG`, `matcher_Diff`) - fixes the definition: only the diagonal move into a pair used by an earlier alignment is removed, gap moves are not; **Biopython 1.88 `_pairwisealigner.c`** (opened: ENDPOINT / STARTPOINT / "we don't allow zero-score extensions") for the co-optimal set; installed `matcher`, `water` 6.6.0 (water reports only the best alignment).
- Fix (additive, `SequenceAligner.TopN.cs` + 3 small refactors of the existing cores): (1) `LocalAlignTopN` / `LocalAlignAffineTopN(seq1, seq2, scoring, maxAlignments, minScore)` - Waterman-Eggert declumping, definition "alignment k = maximum-score local alignment among those that contain none of the aligned residue pairs (diagonal columns) of alignments 1..k-1" (`AlignedPairs`); the original recomputation scheme (matrices kept, after each report only the cells that can change - new forbidden pair or a changed diagonal/upper/left predecessor - are recomputed row by row) on the very same cell recurrences as the plain aligners: `LocalAlignCore`'s inline fill was extracted into `LocalLinearCell`, `AffineAlignCore`'s into `AffineCell` (+ `forbidden` flag), the affine traceback into `AffineTraceback`, `TracebackLocal` got an optional forbidden set - no second DP; alignment 1 = `LocalAlign` / `LocalAlignAffine` (identical), scores non-increasing, ties = the `LocalAlign` convention applied to the declumped matrix (documented: a later round depends on the tie choice); ScoringMatrix (string / Dna) and MatrixScoring (string / Protein / Dna, fractional gaps, real `minScore`, `ExactScore`) overloads; (2) `LocalAlignCoOptimal` / `LocalAlignAffineCoOptimal(..., maxAlignments)`: every distinct optimal local alignment (depth-first tracebacks from every maximal diagonal-reached cell, Biopython's rules: no zero-score extension through another end cell), capped, deterministic order, first = `LocalAlign`; (3) memory guard `TopNMaxCells` = 25 000 000 cells (`O(m n)` as in the 1987 algorithm); (4) MCP `local_align_top_n` (`maxAlignments`, `minScore`, `affine`, `matrix`) and `local_align_co_optimal`, docs/README/MCP_STATUS/traceability/tool-map, catalog regenerated (498 tools, `EXPECTED_COUNTS` alignment 37).
- Reference cross-check: independent Python Waterman-Eggert (full recompute, written from the definition) identical on 1 350 oracle rows + 300 long pairs (6 405 alignments); exhaustive enumeration of all local alignments of tiny inputs: every round is the exhaustive optimum (198 + 600 pairs); **EMBOSS matcher** `-alternatives k`: 397 rows / 1 695 alignments (+ 1 207 in a first 300-case check) each re-scored, pair-disjoint and optimal for the forbidden set -> matcher implements exactly this definition; Seqeron's score list equals matcher's on 390 / 397 rows (7 tie divergences, round 1 equal on 397); Biopython `align()` co-optimal set identical on 1 345 rows (<= 60 alignments). 1.9 kb x 1.3 kb, 50 alignments: 1.2-1.4 s.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TopN_Tests` x16 (oracle `TestData/Alignment/topn_oracle.tsv` 1 350 rows via `scripts/alignment/generate_topn_oracle.py`; matcher/Biopython/independent-WE comparisons; exhaustive optimum of every round on 600 tiny pairs; first = `LocalAlign`; prefix stability; tandem-repeat 12/12/12; co-optimal gap placements; overloads; validation; memory guard), `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests.TopNAlignTests` x4, registration contract updated.
- Commit: `bc2394dc`.

- Defect: `AlignmentTools.MultipleAlignProgressive` `[Description]` and four comments / XML docs in `SequenceAligner.cs` said "distances 1 − fractional identity (identical columns / alignment length)" while the code (`PairwiseIdentityDistance`, F8) and the algorithm doc / TestSpec / Evidence use the ClustalW distance 1 − identities / min(len1, len2) (`pairalign.c` `tmat = (100 − 100·count/MIN(len1,len2))/100`, opened in F8); the per-tool `.mcp.json`/`.md` had already been corrected, so the source attribute (the real tool description) and the docs disagreed.

### F49 — GENOMIC-MOTIFS-001 (audit A1-20): mismatch-tolerant known-motif search was "not implemented"
- Defect (gap): the unit declared fuzznuc `-pmismatch > 0` out of scope ("= `ApproximateMatcher` / PWM"), so a caller of `FindKnownMotifHits` / MCP `find_known_motifs` had no way to ask for near matches of a known motif (TATA box with 1 substitution) on both strands with IUPAC codes; `ApproximateMatcher.FindWithMismatches` is literal (pattern IUPAC symbols compare as characters) and strand-blind.
- Source/oracle: EMBOSS fuzznuc 6.6.0 (run locally) `-pmismatch 1..3 -complement Y -rformat gff` (hit positions and the `*mismatch n` notes) == independent Python Hamming scan (position agrees when the base is in the NC-IUB set of the pattern code; reverse = same scan with the IUPAC reverse-complement pattern) on **6576** (sequence, pattern, k) rows (20 patterns incl. 10 IUPAC; 3932 rows with forward hits, 3785 with reverse hits, 4183 with mismatching hits; sequences include planted 0-3-substitution copies on both strands), asserted inside `scripts/analysis/generate_known_motif_oracle.py --mismatches` (byte-reproducible; the exact-mode TSV is unchanged). fuzznuc quirks recorded, outside the oracle: hits overhanging a sequence shorter than the pattern (28 of 3330 probe cases, all of that kind), k >= pattern length refused for some patterns.
- Fix: `GenomicAnalyzer.FindKnownMotifHits(…, maxMismatches = 0)` (additive optional parameter; negative -> `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`); k > 0 = one canonical `ApproximateMatcher.FindWithMismatches` scan per motif and strand (no new scan loop); `KnownMotifHit.Mismatches` (default 0) carries the per-hit distance (= fuzznuc note; a non-palindromic motif can hit its two strands with different distances). IUPAC pattern support was missing in the canonical matcher, so (B09 for B05, small additive overload, no behaviour change of existing members) `ApproximateMatcher.FindWithMismatches(sequence, pattern, k, iupacPattern[, token])`: per-position agreement = `IupacHelper.MatchesIupac`, eager validation of the pattern, sequence side literal (a sequence N is always a mismatch, = `MotifFinder.FindDegenerateMotif`); the existing scan now counts with early exit before allocating the position list (identical results, one list allocation per hit instead of per window). MCP `find_known_motifs` gains `mismatches` and the additive result fields `matchMismatches` / `reverseMismatches` (omitted for the exact search).
- Tests: `GenomicAnalyzer_KnownMotifMismatch_Tests` (6576-row oracle with strands + counts, literal==IUPAC mode on ACGT patterns, forward-only, 3 fuzznuc worked examples, k=0 == exact, monotone in k, literal-N costs one mismatch, k >= m, shorter sequence, contracts, 3 `ApproximateMatcher` IUPAC-overload tests); MCP `FindKnownMotifs_Mismatches_MatchFuzznuc_WithDistances`, `…DefaultKeepsExactContract_AndRejectsNegative`.
- Docs: `Known_Motif_Search.md` (§5.2 mismatches, §5.3 "not implemented" replaced by the gapped variant, §6.2), TestSpec INV-10 / O8-O10, Evidence (fuzznuc mismatch observations), report, MCP `find_known_motifs.md` / `.mcp.json` / README / generated catalog.

### F50 — GENOMIC-TANDEM/REPEAT/ORF-001 (audit A1-21): `GenomicAnalyzer` tandem / ORF / palindrome search were `DnaSequence`-only (no N / IUPAC)
- Defect (gap): `DnaSequence` is A/C/G/T only, so an assembly with N runs or any IUPAC code could not be searched through `FindTandemRepeats`, `FindOpenReadingFrames` or `FindPalindromes`; the docs sent users to other classes and MCP `find_tandem_repeats` / `find_open_reading_frames` answered "Invalid DNA sequence" for `ACACNACAC` / `ATGNNNTAA` (`find_palindromes` already took raw strings).
- Source: the canonical string engines' contracts — `RepeatFinder.FindMicrosatellites(string)` (MISA `[acgt]`: a unit must be A/C/G/T only; pytrf skips N; Kolpakov-Kucherov runs), `RepeatFinder.FindPalindromes(string)` (REVP alphabet, EMBOSS einverted: only A/C/G/T pair), `OrfScanner` / `Translator.FindOrfs(string)` (EMBOSS getorf + NCBI COrf ambiguity rules: an ambiguous codon is a stop only if every concrete codon is a stop, never a start). Windows containing non-ACGT symbols therefore never form a repeat / palindrome, and an N codon neither opens nor ends an ORF.
- Reference cross-check (the existing generators in `--ambiguous` mode, existing TSVs byte-identical): tandem 892 rows (three independent Python methods + the `[acgt]` unit rule; 235 with hits), palindromes 861 rows (Biopython `reverse_complement` on A/C/G/T-only windows; 340 with hits), ORFs 2844 rows (Biopython definition for all; EMBOSS getorf 6.6.0 `-find 3` on the 184 A/C/G/T/N rows it resolves exactly — getorf mis-resolves OR-ed IUPAC codons such as TRA, the documented `Translator.FindOrfs(string)` difference, so rows with other IUPAC symbols rest on the Biopython definition; 2363 rows with ORFs).
- Fix: additive string overloads `FindTandemRepeats(string, min, minReps[, maxUnit])`, `FindPalindromes(string, min, max)`, `FindOpenReadingFrames(string, minLength[, OrfSearchOptions])` (null -> `ArgumentNullException`, empty -> none, ORF input normalised to upper-case DNA with `U` -> `T`, other non-IUPAC symbol -> `ArgumentException` naming symbol and position via the canonical `IndexOfInvalidIupacDna`); tandem: the canonical scan or, for wide unit ranges, the run engine now in a string form that keeps only runs whose left-end unit is A/C/G/T-only; ORF: the core takes the string. MCP `find_tandem_repeats` and `find_open_reading_frames` accept IUPAC input (`RequireIupacDna`), `find_palindromes` description states the N rule. Existing `null!` call sites in tests were disambiguated with `(DnaSequence)null!` (compile-only).
- Tests: `GenomicAnalyzer_IupacInput_Tests` (3 oracle tests over 892 + 861 + 2844 rows, worked examples, A/C/G/T string == `DnaSequence` overload for the 3 methods incl. the run-engine regime, run engine == canonical per-unit scan on N/IUPAC strings, contracts); MCP `FindTandemRepeats_IupacInput_…`, `FindOpenReadingFrames_IupacInput_…`.
- Docs: tandem / ORF / palindrome algorithm docs, TestSpecs, Evidence change histories, reports, MCP md/json (+ catalog).
- Commit: da9d4a17.

### F51 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-15): guide-tree distance was never ClustalW's own pairwise stage
- Defect: `PairwiseIdentityDistance` (progressive / iterative / consistency guide tree) took the identity count from the repository's linear-gap global NW alignment, while ClustalW's distance (`1 − identities / MIN(len1, len2)`) is the identity count of its own pairwise stage. Reading `FullPairwiseAlign.cpp` (clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7, sources.debian.org, opened with `SubMatrix.cpp`, `matrices.h`, `UserParameters.cpp`, `Clustal.cpp`) shows that stage is **not a global alignment**: `forwardPass` = affine Smith-Waterman-Gotoh (end = first row-major maximum), `reversePass` finds the start, `diff` = Myers-Miller with ClustalW's own midpoint tie rule on that local region; DNA pairwise matrix IUB ×100 (match +1000, every A/C/G/T mismatch −900; the 2.1 transition-weight lines address residue codes A-E / C-M / C-N, so no ACGT pair changes), gap 3000 + 666·k (PWGAPOPEN 15 → 2·15·100, PWGAPEXT 6.66 → 666). Percent identity = 100·identities/MIN(len1,len2); `-ALIGN` prints its truncation as "Score:". Minimal repro: the three sequences in `ClustalWPairwise_HandRunExamples_PrintedScore` (35/36/30 nt) give identities 32 / 12 / 8 = Score 91 / 40 / 26 in the binary; the old linear-NW distance counts different identities and for 65 of 140 random sets built a different guide tree and different progressive rows (independent Python, `clustalw_msa_oracle.tsv`).
- Sources: ClustalW 2.1 source files above (raw text, Debian source mirror); Thompson, Higgins & Gibson 1994 (NAR 22:4673) for the distance; the installed `clustalw` 2.1 binary as the oracle (`-TYPE=DNA -ALIGN`: "Sequences (i:j) Aligned. Score: N"; `-TREE -PIM` is a different quantity — it counts identities column-wise on the input rows, not of the pairwise alignment).
- Reference cross-check: `generate_clustalw_pairwise_oracle.py`: **3 600 pairs** (2 000+ random / two-letter / tandem / mutated ACGT pairs, 500+ IUB-ambiguity pairs, 8 pairs up to 320 nt) — the printed Score, the identity count and the DP score of the C# `ClustalWPairwiseAlign` equal the binary / the Python line-by-line port on every row (0 mismatches; for min length ≤ 100 the integer Score fixes the identity count uniquely). The MSA rows of `MultipleAlignProgressive` / `MultipleAlignIterative` with `Distance = ClustalWAffine` equal an independent Python implementation (NW + profile DP + exact-rational UPGMA + tree-partition refinement driven by the port) on 140 sets × 4 scoring schemes; 65 of them differ from the `Linear` mode, so the oracle discriminates.
- Fix: additive `MsaDistanceMode { Linear (default, unchanged), ClustalWAffine }` and `MsaOptions` (Infrastructure), new overloads `MultipleAlignProgressive/Iterative/Consistency(seqs, scoring, MsaOptions)` (the existing signatures delegate with defaults), `SequenceAligner.ClustalWPairwiseAlign(string|DnaSequence, …, gapOpen, gapExtend)` returning `ClustalWPairwiseResult` (score, identities, percent identity, distance, the integer ClustalW prints, local alignment rows/coordinates) — a faithful port, because the Myers-Miller tie rule decides the identity count among co-optimal alignments (LocalAlignAffine's tie rule is different); the three duplicated distance-matrix loops became one `ComputeGuideDistances`; MCP `multiple_align_progressive|iterative|consistency` get `distance = linear|clustalw`. Exactness statement: the full-DP ("slow") mode is reproduced exactly; ClustalW's fast `-QUICKTREE` k-tuple mode is a different estimate and is registered as A1-15b; ClustalW's protein pairwise stage (Gonnet/BLOSUM with length-dependent gap open) belongs to A1-14.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWDistance_Tests` ×17 (3 600-row clustalw oracle; hand-run Scores 91/40/26; rows spell the local region, rescore to the DP score with ClustalW's matrix and gaps; empty → distance 1; gap penalties honoured; MSA rows vs independent Python; default unchanged; invalid mode), `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests.MultipleAlignTests` ×2 (distance parameter).
- Commit: 625eba59.

### F52 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10): affine-gap MSA (GapOpen was unused by every MSA method)
- Defect: all five multiple aligners used the linear model only (`GapOpen` ignored, `TotalScore` = column sum of pairs), so a MSA of sequences that differ by long indels was scored per gap position; the declared reason ("no exact polynomial profile-profile DP for the natural affine SP") is true for the optimisation but not for the objective, the pairwise stages or the profile approximations all published MSA programs use. Minimal repro (`LongIndel_AffineChargesOneOpening_LinearDoesNot`): s0 = 33 nt, s1 = s0 without a 10-nt A-run, s2 = s0; match 2, mismatch −3, GapOpen −8, GapExtend −1: linear TotalScore 138 counts the 10-nt gap as −10, affine 122 charges it −8 − 10 = −18 once per pair.
- Sources: Altschul 1989 (natural gap costs; WebSearch record of the abstract only, quasi-natural costs not retrievable and therefore neither implemented nor claimed), Gotoh 1982 / Flouri et al. 2015 (F1), Edgar 2004 and Thompson et al. 1994 for position-specific profile penalties; ClustalW 2.1 `FullPairwiseAlign.cpp` (F51) for the gap convention. Installed `clustalw`, `muscle` 5.2, `mafft`, `kalign` 3.3.5, `clustalo`, `t_coffee` used for an informational comparison only.
- Fix: new `MsaGapModel { Linear (default), Affine }` + `MsaOptions.GapModel` (Infrastructure, additive) and overloads `MultipleAlign / MultipleAlignAnchored / MultipleAlignProgressive / MultipleAlignIterative / MultipleAlignConsistency(seqs, scoring, MsaOptions)` (old signatures delegate unchanged). (1) **Objective, exact:** public `SequenceAligner.SumOfPairsScore(rows, scoring, gapModel)`: natural affine sum of pairs (pair projection, gap-gap columns skipped, every maximal gap run opens once); it is `TotalScore` under the affine model. (2) Star: exact affine pairwise alignments to the center (`GlobalAlignAffineLinearSpace`, = `GlobalAlignAffine`), center = best total affine pair score (`SelectCenterByPairwiseScore` generalised to a pair-score delegate); anchored: affine segments between anchors (`AnchorBasedAligner.AlignWithAnchors(affineGaps)`). (3) Progressive / iterative: `AlignProfilesAffine`, a Gotoh three-state DP over profile columns with exact integer arithmetic, byte traceback, Flouri border and M > own > other tie order = `GlobalAlignAffine`, column score Σ cross pairs, gap runs `r_i·|B|·(GapOpen + GapExtend)` at the first column and `r_i·|B|·GapExtend` afterwards (ClustalW/MUSCLE occupancy scaling); the iterative method accepts a re-alignment only on a strict improvement of the EXACT natural objective; guide-tree identities come from `GlobalAlignAffine` under `Linear` distance. (4) Consistency: T-Coffee primary library from Gotoh global + Smith-Waterman-Gotoh local alignments; progressive DP keeps T-Coffee's zero gap penalty. (5) MCP `gapModel = linear|affine` on all five `multiple_align*` tools, descriptions/docs/catalog truthful (gapOpen "used only with gapModel=affine").
- Optimal vs heuristic (documented in the algorithm doc §4.2/§5.3): only k = 2 is optimal (rows = `GlobalAlignAffine`); for k ≥ 3 every method is a heuristic because the natural cost of a pair depends on the full column history; the exact 3-sequence optimum is computed by the test oracle (DP over (i, j, l, run state per pair) = exhaustive enumeration) and exposed as audit item A1-11.
- Reference cross-check: independent Python re-score on 453 random MSAs (+ affine SP ≤ Σ Biopython 1.88 pairwise optima on all); exact optimum of 260 tiny sets (k = 2: = Biopython; k = 3 DP = exhaustive enumeration for Σ length ≤ 9): no C# method exceeds it, k = 2 equal for star/progressive/iterative; on 227 three-sequence sets the optimum is reached by star 170, progressive 189, iterative 189; star/progressive/iterative/consistency rows = independent Python on 150 sets (0 differences). Informational (same objective, 60 sets, k 4-7): mean natural affine SP Seqeron iterative −94.7 / progressive −102.6 / star −144.4 vs linear-model progressive −278.3, muscle −240.9, mafft −299.1, clustalw −372.2, kalign −640.1, clustalo −636.0, t_coffee −148.1 (tools optimise their own objectives).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_MultipleAlignAffine_Tests` ×16, `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests.MultipleAlignTests` ×2. Targeted tier: 1053 (Genomics: SequenceAligner|MultipleAlign|Alignment|MSA|ALIGN|AnchorBased|Assembl classes) + 129 (Mcp.Alignment) passed / 0 failed (A1-15: 775 + 105 before the MCP additions).
- Commit: 5d2df121.

### F53 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-12): duplicated UPGMA guide tree, no neighbour-joining guide tree
- Defect: `SequenceAligner.BuildProgressiveGuideTree` was a private copy of `PhylogeneticAnalyzer.BuildUPGMA` ("reimplemented because Phylogenetics already depends on Alignment, so a reverse reference would be circular"), and no MSA method could use the neighbour-joining tree that ClustalW (default), T-Coffee and MUSCLE v3 use. Verified: `Seqeron.Genomics.Phylogenetics.csproj` referenced Alignment but no `Phylogenetics/*.cs` used an Alignment type (only a global `using`); removing the project reference and the `using` leaves `dotnet build Seqeron.sln` at 0 errors and `ArchitectureTests` (incl. `Modules_ShouldBeFreeOfCycles`) green — the circularity argument was not real.
- Sources actually opened: clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7 sources (sources.debian.org): `tree/NJTree.cpp`, `tree/ClusterTreeOutput.cpp`, `tree/Tree.cpp`, `tree/TreeInterface.cpp`, `tree/UnRootedClusterTree.cpp`, `pairwise/FullPairwiseAlign.cpp`, `general/clustalw.h`, `tree/UPGMA/UPGMAAlgorithm.cpp` (read only); Biopython 1.88 `TreeConstruction.upgma/nj` source (its `upgma` averages without cluster sizes = WPGMA, so it is NOT the UPGMA oracle); Saitou & Nei 1987 / Sokal & Michener 1958 / Thompson et al. 1994 as before.
- Reference cross-check: installed `clustalw` 2.1 `-TYPE=DNA -ALIGN -MAXDIV=0 -NEWTREE` on 420 DNA sets (`scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_guidetree_oracle.py`): the unrooted NJ tree (5-decimal lengths, |b| < 1e-4 → 0) equals the `.dnd` edge for edge, and the sequence of merge sizes of the rooted tree equals the "Group n: Sequences: s" lines — 0 mismatches (the lexicographic tie order of the canonical NJ fails 21 sets: ClustalW's `NJTree` loops jj-outer / ii-inner). Canonical NJ = Biopython `nj` and canonical UPGMA = scipy `linkage('average')` on 400 random matrices each (≤ 1e-6); the guide tree of the three aligners equals the removed private UPGMA on 900 random and tie-heavy matrices (kept in the test as the oracle). ClustalW's NJ update `(d_ik+d_jk)/2` is Saitou-Nei up to a per-step constant shift of Q, so topology and lengths are standard NJ; the ClustalW-specific parts are the tie order, the printing/re-reading (single precision), `Tree::reRoot` and the post-order merge list.
- Fix: `Phylogenetics.csproj` loses the unused `Alignment` reference/`using`; `Alignment.csproj` references `Phylogenetics`; additive canonical API `PhylogeneticAnalyzer.BuildTreeFromMatrix(taxa, matrix, method, TreeBuildOptions?)` with `TreeBuildOptions { TieTolerance, TieOrder = PairScanOrder.FirstIndexMajor | SecondIndexMajor }` (B16 file, additive only, defaults reproduce the old behaviour byte for byte); new `SequenceAligner.GuideTree.cs`: the UPGMA guide tree calls the canonical builder (`TieTolerance` 1e-9, lowest pair), `MsaGuideTree.NeighborJoining` = canonical NJ (`SecondIndexMajor`) + line-by-line port of ClustalW's `createTree/insertNode/reRoot/calcMean/insertRoot/groupSeqs` (single precision) with ClustalW's single-precision distances under `ClustalWAffine`; additive `MsaOptions.GuideTree` (`Upgma` default = unchanged behaviour, `NeighborJoining`) for progressive / iterative (refinement edges follow the tree) / consistency; MCP `guideTree = upgma|nj` on `multiple_align_progressive/iterative/consistency` (+ docs md/json, catalog `--check` fresh).
- Not claimed: final MSA rows equal to `clustalw -USETREE` — ClustalW's profile stage (sequence weights from the tree, residue-specific/hydrophilic gap penalties, delayed divergent sequences) is A1-10b, which this tree unblocks; ClustalW's own `-CLUSTERING=UPGMA` is a separate implementation (own tie rules) and is not claimed either.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_GuideTree_Tests` ×18 (UPGMA vs removed code on 900 matrices, Biopython/scipy 2 × 400, clustalw 420 sets, hand-run ties, option semantics, valid MSAs on 80 sets, layering), MCP `MultipleAlignTests` ×2 (`guideTree`).
- Commit: 5f886ce6.

### F54 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-16): O(a²) anchor chaining, dead baseline in production code
- Defect: `AnchorBasedAligner.ChainAnchors` compared every anchor with every predecessor (O(a²): 10⁵ anchors ≈ 5·10⁹ tests); `SequenceAligner.MultipleAlignClassic` (`internal`, no public surface) existed only for the explicit benchmark fixture.
- Source: Abouelhoda & Ohlebusch 2005 (J Discrete Algorithms 3:321; opened in F5) sparse dynamic programming with range-maximum queries; the recurrence of the code (trim of the later anchor by the earlier anchor's ends) is unchanged.
- Fix: with δ = query − ref, a predecessor leaves a positive remainder iff both of its ends are strictly below the new anchor's ends, and its trim is `(endRef(j) − refStart(i))⁺` for δⱼ ≤ δᵢ and `(endQuery(j) − queryStart(i))⁺` otherwise — a function of one end coordinate per half, so each half is two orthogonal range-maximum queries (prefix on the diagonal rank × end ≤ start / end in (start, end)); implemented as a Fenwick tree over diagonal ranks whose nodes hold max segment trees over the end coordinate, O(a log² a) time, O(a log a) memory, ties (earliest predecessor / anchor) preserved. Honest note: the task asked for O(a log a); the overlap-trimming recurrence is 2-D per half, so the exact equivalent is O(a log² a) (O(log a) per query only for the non-overlapping regime). `MultipleAlignClassic` removed from production; the benchmark fixture (`[Explicit]`) now compares the public exact `MultipleAlign` (the "classic NW star") with `MultipleAlignAnchored`.
- Reference cross-check: the former O(a²) scan is kept verbatim in the test as the oracle — equal chains on 1 500 random sets (coordinate ranges 6-600, lengths 4-80), 400 MEM-like sets, 12 adversarial shapes (identical anchors, same start, nested, anti-diagonal, staircase, shifted diagonals, one-sided overlaps, duplicates, empty, single, invalid entries) and 2 × 3 000 anchors; 10⁵ anchors chain in under a second.
- Tests: `AnchorBasedAligner_ChainEquivalence_Tests` ×5.
- Commit: 5f886ce6.

### F55 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-15b): ClustalW's fast `-QUICKTREE` pairwise stage was not implemented
- Defect (gap): `MsaDistanceMode.ClustalWAffine` (F51) reproduced only ClustalW's full-DP pairwise stage; the fast k-tuple stage that `clustalw -QUICKTREE` (and the interactive "fast" menu) uses gives different distances, hence different guide trees, and was declared "a different estimate".
- Sources actually opened: clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7 (sources.debian.org) `pairwise/FastPairwiseAlign.cpp` (all of it), `general/UserParameters.cpp` (DNA defaults), `interface/CommandLineParser.cpp` (`-KTUPLE` sets the pair gap to ktuple + 4; `-WINDOW/-TOPDIAGS/-PAIRGAP` accepted only above the k-tuple; `-SCORE`), `alignment/Alignment.cpp` (`calculateMaxLengths`: maxAlnLength = 2 · longest sequence of the whole input); Wilbur & Lipman 1983.
- Fix: `SequenceAligner.ClustalWQuickPairwiseAlign(a, b, ClustalWQuickOptions?, maxSequenceLength?)` = exact port of `pairAlign` (+ `makeNPtrs`, `putFrag`, `fragRelPos`, the unstable `desQuickSort` that leaves the last diagonal outside its range, the "(Partial alignment)" exit at 2·maxAlnLength fragments; A=0 C=1 G=2 T=U=3, IUB codes break k-mers), score = 100·S/MIN(len1, len2) (`-SCORE=ABSOLUTE` = S), distance (100 − score)/100 in double; `ClustalWQuickOptions` (KTuple 2 (1-4), Window 4, TopDiagonals 4, PairGap 5, Percent) in `MsaOptions.QuickPairwise`; `MsaDistanceMode.ClustalWQuick` through the single `ComputeGuideDistances`; MCP `distance = linear | clustalw | clustalw-quick` + `ktuple / window / topDiagonals / pairGap` on `multiple_align_progressive/iterative/consistency` (pair gap = ktuple + 4 when only ktuple is given, as the clustalw command line does).
- Reference cross-check: `generate_clustalw_quick_oracle.py` — installed `clustalw` 2.1 `-TYPE=DNA -QUICKTREE -ALIGN` "Score:" (`%lg` of the double percent) / "Not Aligned" on **8 257 pairs of 605 sets** committed (10 733 pairs in the development run) with k-tuple 1-4, `-WINDOW`, `-TOPDIAGS`, `-PAIRGAP`, `-SCORE=ABSOLUTE`, IUB-N sets and repeat-rich sets that reach the partial-alignment exit: 0 mismatches. Bug found by the second oracle (the MSA one, F56): the first version defaulted the pair gap to ktuple + 4 = 6; ClustalW's default without `-KTUPLE` is 5 (a hand-run pair prints 27.5862 with 5 and 25.8621 with 6); the first oracle had only recorded explicit pair gaps and could not see it — default-option rows are now asserted separately.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWQuick_Tests` ×10, `MultipleAlignTests` ×1 (MCP delegation / validation).
- Commit: f4315761 (one commit with F56).

### F56 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10b): ClustalW's multiple-alignment stage (weights, delayed sequences, position-specific gaps, NJ arithmetic) is not reproduced
- Defect (gap): the profile aligners (`MultipleAlignProgressive/Iterative`) are Seqeron's own occupancy-scaled models (F52); nothing reproduced `clustalw -ALIGN` rows, so a caller could not obtain ClustalW's alignment, and the F52 comparison with the binary stayed informational.
- Sources actually opened: clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7 (sources.debian.org) `Clustal.cpp`, `multipleAlign/MSA.cpp`, `MyersMillerProfileAlign.cpp`, `ProfileBase.cpp`, `ProfileStandard.cpp`, `ProfileWithSub.cpp`, `tree/Tree.cpp` (`calcSeqWeights`, `calcWeight`, `calcSimilarities`, `createSets`, `groupSeqs`), `tree/NJTree.cpp`, `tree/TreeInterface.cpp`, `tree/UnRootedClusterTree.cpp`, `substitutionMatrix/SubMatrix.cpp` (`getProfileAlignMatrix`, `getMatrix`), `interface/CommandLineParser.cpp`; Thompson, Higgins & Gibson 1994; Myers & Miller 1988. What the source says (details in `Multiple_Sequence_Alignment.md` §4.6): weights = Σ branch length / leaves below along the leaf→root path (single precision, normalised to INT_SCALE_FACTOR 1000), tree-derived similarities replace the pairwise identities, sequences with best identity ≤ `-MAXDIV` (30) are delayed, gap penalties are position-specific (columns that hold gaps get a reduced opening penalty and half the extension penalty, end gaps free), the IUB ×100 matrix is shifted by +900 and A-G / C-T / C-U := ⌊TRANSWEIGHT·1900⌋, `-GAPEXT` is a float (665, not 666); the residue-specific / hydrophilic / variable-region gap penalties and `-GAPDIST` are guarded by `!_DNAFlag` (no effect for DNA, so `-NOPGAP/-NOHGAP/-GAPDIST` are not options here); `-ENDGAPS` on the command line only writes its default false.
- Fix: `SequenceAligner.MultipleAlignClustalW(IEnumerable<DnaSequence> | IEnumerable<string>, ClustalWOptions?, ScoringMatrix?)` (`SequenceAligner.ClustalWMultiple.cs`) = step-by-step port (pairwise stage full / fast → ClustalW NJ → `Tree::reRoot` → weights → similarities → `multiSeqAlign` passes → `profileAlign`: profiles with/without matrix, `calcGapCoeff`, Myers-Miller `progDiff`, `addGGaps`); additive `ClustalWOptions` (GapOpen 15, GapExtend 6.66, PairwiseMode Full/Quick, PairwiseGapOpen/Extend, Quick, TransitionWeight 0.5, DivergenceCutoff 30, NoWeights, NegativeMatrix); rows in input order, consensus, `TotalScore` = natural affine sum of pairs under the caller's scoring. Two supporting corrections: (1) `ClustalWNeighborJoining` = port of `NJTree::generateTree` (height-corrected matrix, `(d_ik + d_jk)/2`, `fnseqs2·d_ij + 2·sumd − diq − djq`, jj-outer scan, strict `<`) now builds the ClustalW guide tree (`MsaGuideTree.NeighborJoining`, F53's tree builder): the canonical `PhylogeneticAnalyzer` NJ differs on exact ties by rounding noise and failed 2 of 700 oracle sets once fast-mode distances (many ties) were used — the canonical builder is unchanged and stays the Biopython-checked Saitou-Nei implementation (this is not a duplicate: the arithmetic is the point); (2) `-TRANSWEIGHT` reaches the pairwise matrix (C-M / C-N code pairs), `ClustalWPairwiseAlignCore(…, transitionWeight)`; MCP tool `multiple_align_clustalw` (additive; tool count 38) with `gapOpen gapExtend distance pwGapOpen pwGapExtend transitionWeight maxDiv noWeights negative ktuple window topDiagonals pairGap match mismatch scoreGapOpen scoreGapExtend`.
- Reference cross-check: `generate_clustalw_msa_full_oracle.py` — installed `clustalw` 2.1 `-TYPE=DNA -ALIGN -OUTPUT=FASTA -OUTORDER=INPUT` (the binary computes its own pairwise stage, tree, weights and profile alignments): **1 000 committed sets** (2-25 sequences up to 600 nt; mutated families, low-complexity, duplicates, tiny and IUB-ambiguity sets; `-GAPOPEN -GAPEXT -TRANSWEIGHT -MAXDIV -NOWEIGHTS -NEGATIVE -QUICKTREE -KTUPLE -WINDOW -TOPDIAGS -PAIRGAP -PWGAPOPEN -PWGAPEXT` variants): **every row identical, 0 differences**. Development history: 148/150 sets equal at the first run (the 2 misses were the fast-mode pair-gap default, F55), 698/700 after that (the 2 misses: tied fast-mode distances → the NJ arithmetic port), 2 496/2 500 (4 sets with IUB codes and `-TRANSWEIGHT` ≠ 0.5 → the pairwise-matrix transition weight), then 2 500/2 500 and the 1 000 committed sets. Discrimination: switching off weights / delayed sequences / transition weight / negative matrix / the fast stage / changing the gap penalties changes the rows on many oracle sets (CM3). `-USETREE` with our tree was not run: the binary builds the same tree itself (F53, 420 sets) and the end-to-end comparison covers tree reading, weights and similarities.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWMultiple_Tests` ×9, `SequenceAligner_GuideTree_Tests` ×18 (unchanged, green on the ported NJ), `MultipleAlignTests` ×1 (MCP), `ToolRegistrationContractTests` (new tool). Targeted tier: 1 833 (Genomics) + MCP.Alignment passed / 0 failed.
- Commit: f4315761

### F57 — GENOMIC-ORF-001 (audit A1-18 / A1-19): NCBI ORFfinder 0.4.3 standalone semantics (`-n`, `-b/-e`, `-c`, `-ml` clamp, `-strand`, partial flags, tables 27/28/31) measured on the real binary
- Defect (gap): the cross-frame "ignore nested ORFs" filter, the search region, the `-c` option, the `-ml < 30 -> 30` clamp and ORFfinder's partial flags are defined nowhere in the open-source `COrf` (F23/F25) and had been declared BLOCKED/"not part of the method"; the audit (A1-18) refuted the "no binary" proof (the NCBI FTP binary is reachable) but the sandbox refuses to run it, so the user ran the hand-off package.
- Sources actually opened: the unmodified NCBI program `https://ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/genomes/TOOLS/ORFfinder/linux-i64/ORFfinder.gz` (0.4.3, sha256 `f30a0806…09f2`, Build-Date May 4 2021) + `USAGE.txt` / `CHANGELOG.txt`, **run by the user in Docker** (linux/amd64, Debian 12, x86_64): the returned archive `handoff/A1-18-orffinder/returned/A1-18-orffinder-out.zip` (1750 invocations, 1738 rc 0 / 4 rc 1 / 3 rc 254 / 5 rc 255; `ENV.txt` `ORFfinder -version` = 0.4.3), parsed with `python3 -I` as untrusted text; nothing executed.
- Reference cross-check: `scripts/analysis/generate_orffinder_oracle.py` converts the archive (formats observed: `-outfmt 0` header `>lcl|ORF<n>_<id>:<from>:<to> unnamed protein product[, partial]` with 0-BASED inclusive coordinates incl. the stop, `-outfmt 1` 1-based, `-outfmt 2` fuzz flags, `-outfmt 3` 0-based without `<`/`>`; the converter asserts the four formats agree, numbering 1..n, partial vs fuzz flags, 4 134 duplicate measurements) into `orffinder_cli_oracle.tsv` (4.9 MB; 620 sequences, 260 option sets, **54 820 results = 232 162 ORFs + 360 rejected regions**). Measured rules (all rows): `-n true` = drop an ORF whose forward span lies completely inside another ORF of any frame/strand (non-strict; 67 373 ORFs dropped: 58 732 strictly inside, 3 373 sharing the start, 3 539 sharing the end, 1 729 identical spans across strands: **the plus-strand ORF is dropped, minus kept**; 23 231 contained only by the other strand; containers = ORFs left by `-ml`/`-strand`); `-ml` = `max(ml,30)` then whole codons (`3·⌊ml/3⌋` coding nt; `-ml 31` reports 30 nt; the plain rule is refuted on 384 rows); `-b/-e` 1-based inclusive, `-b 0` = 1, `-e 0`/beyond = end, `-b<0` and `-b>-e` errors, **cut-then-scan** in input coordinates (7 080 region rows); **`-c true` has no effect** (byte-identical output on 36 circular + 20 region + 12 no-stop pairs: nothing to implement); partial: 5' <=> strand offset < 3 (also with ATG), 3' <=> no stop; protein = table translation, `M` first unless 5'-partial (254 010 ORFs); tables 27/28/31 accepted with the table's stops, 0/7/8/17-20 rejected; `-s 0/1/2` and all 30 tables equal the shipped scanner (30 224 sequence rows); order = plus then minus, each by frame then start; lower/mixed case accepted, `N`/IUPAC as `OrfScanner`, `U`/gap/protein rejected by the reader (27 records without result).
- Fix: additive `OrfSearchOptions.IgnoreNestedOrfs` (`OrfScanner.FindNestedSpans`, O(m log m) sweep), `RegionStart/RegionEnd` (+ `OrfInfo.RegionStart/RegionLength`, region-aware `GetForwardStrandRange()`), `Strands` (`OrfStrands`; `SearchBothStrands` kept as a view), `OrfFinderMinimumLength`, `DualCodingStopsTerminate` (`OrfScanner.FindRegions(..., allowDualCodingStops)`), `OrfInfo.IsStartPartial`, profile `OrfSearchOptions.OrfFinderCompatible`; no circular option (the binary ignores `-c`). MCP `find_open_reading_frames` 1.2.0: `ignoreNested`, `strand`, `regionStart`, `regionEnd`, `orfFinderMinLength`, `allowDualCodingStopTables`, result `isStartPartial`; defaults unchanged (F21–F26 contracts and the 1746-row `orf_oracle.tsv` test untouched and green).
- Residual: **none** — the library equals the binary on all 54 820 results (strand, coordinates, order, partial flags, protein sequences). Not measured (documented): `-b` beyond the sequence length with `-e 0` (treated as the start>end error), RNA/gap input (the binary's FASTA reader rejects it; the string overloads keep accepting `U`), multi-record id collisions.
- Tests: `GenomicAnalyzer_FindOpenReadingFrames_OrfFinderCli_Tests` ×23 (`…_EqualsNcbiOrfFinderBinary_OnEveryOracleRow` over all rows, option-dimension coverage, `-c` no-op proof, worked examples for each option), MCP `FindOpenReadingFramesTests` +2.

### F58 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-13): `MultipleAlignConsistency` did not reproduce the T-Coffee program (F7 "binary cross-check": 105 of 120 sets differ)
- Defect: the unit is documented as T-Coffee; its reference program (installed, `t_coffee` 13.41) produced different rows in most sets, and F7 had attributed this to the paper-vs-program "rescale of extended pair scores" (`evaluate.c` L5245-5250) and `-extend_mode slow_triplet`. Reading the program's pipeline shows that explanation was wrong: `-extend_mode` has **no effect** on the default `dp_mode linked_pair_wise` (0 of 60 sets differ for `slow_triplet` / `triplet` / `fast_triplet`); the rows are produced by (i) library RELAXATION (`-relax_lib 1`, `fork_relax_constraint_list`, `util_constraints_list.c`: per directed edge `trunc((w_back + Σ min(w1, w2)) / min(deg1, deg2))` in double arithmetic, loop bound `r1 < len` so the last residue of each sequence keeps its weight, then `filter_constraint_list` keeps weight **> 10**) and (ii) the sparse "linked pair-wise" DP (`util_dp_gotoh_nw.c`: `cl2pair_list_ext` scores every column pair in float from the primary library, 0.01 filter, last row/column excluded, value round-tripped through `%f`; `cl2diag_cap` joins segments with zero caps, `cl2list_borders`, `list2nodup_list`, then a three-state maximum-weight chain with gap open = gap extend = 0, M >= I,J / I >= J tie order, smaller profile first). The plain `t_coffee seqs.fa` default method is `Mproba_pair` (`t_coffee.c` L4375: "new default"; the paper's `lalign_id_pair` + `slow_pair` are commented out), `-mode=regular` gives identical rows.
- Sources actually opened: Debian source package t-coffee 13.45.0.4846264+really13.41.0.28bdc39+dfsg-1 (the installed version) via sources.debian.org: `t_coffee_source/dp_lib/{evaluate.c, util_dp_gotoh_nw.c, util_dp_drivers.c, util_dp_mm_nw.c, util_dp_sim.c}`, `util_lib/{util_constraints_list.c, util.c, aln_convertion_util.c}`, `t_coffee_lib/t_coffee.c`, `coffee_defines.h` (SCORE_K 10, NORM_F 1000), `matrices.h` (`dna_idmat`).
- Fix: `SequenceAligner.MultipleAlignTCoffee(sequences, TCoffeeLibrary, TCoffeeTreeNode, relaxLibrary)` (new `SequenceAligner.TCoffee.cs`; additive types `TCoffeeLibrary`, `TCoffeeTreeNode`) = exact port of (i) + (ii) for an explicit primary library (weights of a `TC_LIB_FORMAT_01` file; 10 x percent identity for the program's own methods) and explicit binary guide tree; additive `MsaOptions.ConsistencyEngine` (`PaperExtension` default = unchanged published formula, `TCoffeeLinked` = the program's progressive stage on this repository's library, weights x 10, and the configured guide tree), MCP `multiple_align_consistency` `consistencyEngine = paper|tcoffee`. `scripts/alignment/generate_tcoffee_oracle.py`.
- Reference cross-check: 400 random DNA sets (3-6 sequences of 3-12 nt, half related), each run with `-relax_lib 1` and `-relax_lib 0` against `t_coffee -seq -lib -usetree ... -n_core 1`: **800 / 800 row sets identical**, libraries from this repository's NW+SW ("rand") and from the program's own `slow_pair,lalign_id_pair` ("binary"). Development: Python port 0 / 100 mismatches at relax 0 for k = 3-6; at relax 1 the strict `> 10` filter (instead of `>=`) removed the last 8 / 100 k = 5 mismatches.
- Residual (measured, not ported): (a) primary library - the program's `slow_pair` is Myers-Miller (`util_dp_mm_nw.c`) global alignment with `dna_idmat` (+10/-1, scaled by SCORE_K = 10), gap penalties `gop = get_avg_matrix_mm(M, AA_ALPHABET)*10` (an average over amino-acid letter indices of a DNA matrix), `gep = -1`, TG_MODE 1 (terminal gaps pay `gep` only), `lalign_id_pair` is SIM top-10 (`-lalign_n_top 10`) local alignments, weights 10 x percent identity, and the plain default is `Mproba_pair` (pair-HMM); the gap model fitted on 60 random pairs (M = 10, X = -1, gep = -1, terminal gep only) makes the binary's alignment optimal for 54/60 pairs at gop = -11 (43/60 at -10, 39/60 at -12), so it is not yet an exact model of the Myers-Miller midpoint rule; this repository's NW (+1/-1/-2) + single SW library equals the program's `slow_pair,lalign_id_pair` library in 0/150 sets (pair sets 0/150); (b) the program's default guide tree (`-tree_mode nj` on k-tuple distances, trifurcating root converted to a binary order) is not ported. End to end on 150 related-sequence sets, `MsaConsistencyEngine.TCoffeeLinked` (own library, NJ tree) equals `t_coffee -method slow_pair,lalign_id_pair` in 21 sets (the paper-formula engine in 19) and a plain `t_coffee` in 20 (paper 21): that is the size of the remaining library/tree gap, split off as A1-13b.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TCoffee_Tests` x6 (oracle complete, every row equals the program on 800 sets, rows spell the inputs, contract errors, default engine unchanged, `TCoffeeLinked` valid), MCP `MultipleAlignTests` +1 (`consistencyEngine`). Targeted tier 449 + MCP.Alignment 134 passed.

### F59 — ALIGN-GLOBAL-001 (audit A1-7b): no enumeration of co-optimal GLOBAL / ends-free alignments
- Defect (gap): `GlobalAlign` / `GlobalAlignAffine` / `SemiGlobalAlign*` return one optimal alignment; Biopython `PairwiseAligner(mode='global').align()` iterates all of them (doc §6.2 "returns one optimal alignment, not all optimal alignments").
- Fix: `GlobalAlignCoOptimal` / `GlobalAlignAffineCoOptimal` / `SemiGlobalAlignCoOptimal` / `SemiGlobalAlignAffineCoOptimal` (`SequenceAligner.GlobalCoOptimal.cs`; capped `maxAlignments`; `ScoringMatrix` string + `DnaSequence`, `MatrixScoring` string / `ProteinSequence` / `DnaSequence`; all 16 `EndGaps` policies) + MCP `global_align_co_optimal`. No second DP: `LinearAlignCore` / `AffineAlignCore` were split into the shared `LinearFillMatrix`, `AffineNewMatrices` / `AffineInitBorders` / `AffineFill`, `EndCellCandidates` (the local top-N enumerator calls `AffineInitBorders` as well); only the traceback differs (depth-first over every predecessor reproducing a cell's value). Distinct rows are listed once (set of rows; with zero gap costs a free flank is also a zero-cost gap run of the path); coordinates = consumed intervals of the rows.
- Resume note: the work package was resumed from a stopped agent's commit 78f07d4a (core + oracle + tests, never built); the first build failed on an ambiguous `null` overload in a test (cast added) and the oracle test found one real defect - the "dominated end cell" shortcut listed a different first alignment than `SemiGlobalAlign` on a zero-gap-cost row with all four ends free; replaced by the row set.
- Reference cross-check: `generate_global_cooptimal_oracle.py` (Biopython 1.88, 1 920 pairs, all 16 policies, linear/affine, scalar + BLOSUM62 fractional gaps, up to 150 alignments compared): identical sets on 1 859 / 1 859 comparable rows (935 with several alignments), the other 61 rows (> 150 alignments) give distinct optimal prefixes; exhaustive enumeration of every column sequence of pairs <= 5 residues equal for all 16 policies, linear + affine.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_GlobalCoOptimal_Tests` x13, MCP `GlobalCoOptimalAndExactMsaTests` x3 (+ registration list). Targeted tier 2412 + MCP.Alignment 140 passed.

### F60 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-11): exact sum-of-pairs optimal MSA was "not implemented (NP-complete)"
- Defect (gap): every MSA method is a heuristic for k >= 3 and nothing computed or bounded the optimum; the documented reason ("NP-complete") does not justify omitting the exact algorithms that exist for small k.
- Fix: `SequenceAligner.MultipleAlignExact` / `MultipleAlignExactWithStatistics` (`SequenceAligner.MultipleExact.cs`; additive `MsaOptions.ExactMaxStateCells`, `ExactCarrilloLipman`, record `ExactMsaResult`) + MCP `multiple_align_exact`. k = 2 = `GlobalAlign` / `GlobalAlignAffine`; k >= 3: k-dimensional dynamic program over the 2^k - 1 neighbours (k = 3: the 7-neighbour 3-D recurrence); natural affine gaps with one value per gap-history state of every cell (weak order of the last-move times: 13 / 75 / 541 states for k = 3 / 4 / 5) = the exact state-per-pair program of the tests; Carrillo-Lipman (1988) pruning (sum over pairs of the through-cell pairwise optimum, forward + backward Gotoh tables, against the best heuristic score of star / progressive / iterative) plus the A*-style suffix-bound state test; complexity guard (`ArgumentException` above `ExactMaxStateCells` surviving values, never OOM; <= 8 sequences linear / 6 affine). Lower-bound seeding = the existing heuristics, no duplicate aligner.
- Sources actually opened: Shamir lecture notes on the Carrillo-Lipman bound (`cs.tau.ac.il/~rshamir/algmb/98/scribe/html/lec03/node11.html`, pairwise-budget form; the implemented sum test implies it). Carrillo & Lipman 1988, Lipman-Altschul-Kececioglu 1989, Altschul-Lipman 1989 only as WebSearch abstracts (full text not retrievable); correctness is proved in the code comment (every alignment through a cell is bounded by the sum of its pairwise projections) and measured below.
- Reference cross-check: `generate_msa_exact_oracle.py` - 804 random sets (k = 2..5, both objectives): two independent Python exact programs (lattice DP; DP over positions x run state of every pair) agree with each other, with exhaustive enumeration of every alignment (tiny sets) and with Biopython (k = 2); C# optimum equal on 804 / 804 with and without the bound, rows score to it; in-test exhaustive check of 120 tiny sets; pruned = full lattice on 12 medium sets. Heuristics never exceed the optimum; reached on (k = 3..4, 309 sets / objective) linear: star 218, progressive 276, iterative 276, consistency 186, anchored 187, best of five 285; affine: 212, 227, 228, 164, 178, 265.
- Speed (Debug): k = 3 x 300 nt 0.18 s (1 300 of 27 M cells); k = 4 x 100 nt 30 ms (160-226 of 100 M cells); k = 5 x 50 nt < 70 ms; k = 6 x 40 nt affine 0.6 s.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_MultipleAlignExact_Tests` x11, MCP `GlobalCoOptimalAndExactMsaTests` x3. Targeted tier 2412 + MCP.Alignment 140 passed.
- Commit: see worklist A1-11.

### F61 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-14): MSA was DnaSequence-only (no protein / substitution-matrix multiple alignment)
- Defect (gap): every multiple aligner took `DnaSequence` and the scalar `ScoringMatrix` (match / mismatch / integer gaps); a protein family could not be aligned with BLOSUM62 / PAM250 and gapopen 10 / gapextend 0.5 (`Multiple_Sequence_Alignment.md` §6.2 "documented and tested for DnaSequence inputs only"); the MCP tools used `DnaSequence.TryCreate` and integer gap scores; `BuildConsensus` knew only A, C, G, T.
- Sources actually opened: F41's pairwise matrix model (EMBOSS needle / water numbers, Biopython 1.88 `PairwiseAligner(substitution_matrix=...)`), T-Coffee 13.41 (installed program), no new algorithm: the same published methods on a different residue-pair score. Informational programs run locally: muscle, mafft, kalign, clustalo, clustalw, t_coffee.
- Fix (additive; every existing signature, behaviour and DNA row unchanged): `MultipleAlign / MultipleAlignAnchored / MultipleAlignProgressive / MultipleAlignIterative / MultipleAlignConsistency / MultipleAlignExact(+WithStatistics)(IEnumerable<ProteinSequence> | IEnumerable<string>, MatrixScoring, MsaOptions?)`, `MatrixSumOfPairsScore`, `MultipleAlignmentResult.ExactTotalScore`, `ExactMsaResult.ScoreScale`. No second implementation: the internals of all methods (`StarAlignCore`, `AnchoredStarAlignCore`, `ProgressiveAlignCore`, `IterativeAlignCore`, `ConsistencyAlignCore`, `ExactAlignCore`, profile DPs, SP objective, T-Coffee library, `AnchorBasedAligner`) were switched from `ScoringMatrix` to the exact-integer `PairScoring` of F41 (`Sub(a, b)` instead of `Match`/`Mismatch`; the scalar scoring is its scale-1 case), `PairScoring.Create(MatrixScoring, IReadOnlyList<string>)` validates a whole set; `BuildConsensus` counts every residue letter in matrix runs. MCP: `type = dna | protein`, `matrix` on `multiple_align*` (not `_clustalw`), `gapOpen / gapExtend` numbers, `exactTotalScore` (+ `scoreScale` for the exact tool); catalog regenerated and `--check` fresh, tool docs / README / tool-map updated. A `SumOfPairsScore(rows, MatrixScoring)` overload was NOT added under that name because the existing test `SumOfPairsScore(rows, null, model)` would become ambiguous; the matrix objective is `MatrixSumOfPairsScore`; likewise no `DnaSequence + MatrixScoring` overload (a `null` scoring literal would be ambiguous, tests pass one): use the string overload with EDNAFULL.
- Reference cross-check: `generate_msa_matrix_oracle.py` (independent Python, exact rationals): 238 sets x star / anchored / progressive / iterative / consistency (k = 2..6; BLOSUM62, PAM250, EDNAFULL; linear / affine; 15 gap schemes incl. 9.5/0.5, 4.9/0.1, 0.25/0.75): **every row equal**; 308 exact sets (k = 2 = Biopython, k = 3, 4 lattice DP, tiny sets exhaustive): C# optimum equal with and without Carrillo-Lipman; 420 random MSAs re-scored; `t_coffee` 13.41 on 160 protein sets (320 rows): `MultipleAlignTCoffee` equal on all. Informational SP comparison (10 families, BLOSUM62 10/0.5): mafft 1072.5, seqeron-consistency 1067.4, muscle 1054.1, kalign 1024.4, seqeron-star 1021.5, seqeron-iterative 1013.3, t_coffee 1009.9, seqeron-progressive 995.8, clustalw 910.7, clustalo 753.4 (upper bound 1257.2).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_MultipleAlignMatrix_Tests` x15, `SequenceAligner_TCoffee_Tests` +1, `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests.MultipleAlignTests` +3. Targeted tier 2 131 (Genomics: MultipleAlign|AnchorBased|SequenceAligner|ClustalW|TCoffee|Align|Exact|MultipleAlignmentStatistics) + MCP.Alignment 143 passed / 0 failed.

- Reference cross-check: `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_protein_msa_oracle.py` - installed `clustalw` 2.1 `-TYPE=PROTEIN -ALIGN -OUTPUT=FASTA -OUTORDER=INPUT`: **1 000 committed sets** (2-25 sequences, <= 365 residues; family / hydrophilic-block / low-complexity / repeat / tiny / duplicate sets, B Z X O U) with `-MATRIX -PWMATRIX -GAPOPEN -GAPEXT -PWGAPOPEN -PWGAPEXT -MAXDIV -NOWEIGHTS -NEGATIVE -QUICKTREE -KTUPLE -WINDOW -TOPDIAGS -PAIRGAP -NOPGAP -NOHGAP -NOVGAP -GAPDIST -HGAPRESIDUES -ENDGAPS` variants (333 default runs): **998 sets row-identical, the 2 others (both `-NOVGAP`) identical with the forced `gdist` below, 0 other differences**. The binary's `-DEBUG=5` dump (opening / extension penalty and every profile column of every profile alignment) localised every divergence during development. Development history: 584/700 equal at the first run (116 misses: `-NOVGAP` read inverted - clustalw's `-NOVGAP` enables the variable-region penalties), 683/700 after that (17: unassigned `gdist`), 697/700 with the heap model, 699/700 after `MAXHYDRESIDUES` = 9, then the single-precision gap opening (`PrfScaleValues.scale` is a float: 174 -> 175); two development panels (700 + 3 000 sets, other seeds): all equal except 5 sets, all `-NOVGAP`. `clustalw_protein_series_reference.tsv`: the series tables equal EMBOSS on 8 x 529 cells. **Residual (documented, not eliminable):** with `-NOVGAP` and a two-sequence profile of > 60 % identity `ProfileBase::calcGapCoeff` reads the member `gdist`, which `ProfileBase` never initialises - undefined behaviour; the value is whatever the recycled heap chunk held (found: 0, the user's `-GAPDIST`, or a stale residue code 5 / 16 / 20 / 30). The port emulates the usual value (0 for the first profile pair, afterwards `GapSeparationDistance`, LIFO order of the two freed profile objects); `ClustalWProteinOptions.UninitialisedGapDistances` forces the values of a given run (sets 368: 0,4,0,0 and 915: 5,0,20 of the committed oracle are reproduced row for row this way; the forced values come from a per-profile scan against the `-DEBUG=5` dump). Rate: 7 of 4 700 sets (0.15 %), all with `-NOVGAP`, none without. `-ENDGAPS` on the command line is a no-op (it writes `useEndGaps = false`): 54 oracle sets carry it and equal the runs without it.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWProteinMultiple_Tests` x13 (oracle rows on 1 000 sets, residual accounting, coverage of every option, mechanism discrimination, `MAXHYDRESIDUES`, invariants, overload equality, contracts, defaults, EMBOSS series cells x 4 232, GONNET symmetry / anchors), `MultipleAlignTests` (MCP) +1. Targeted tier 1 541 (Genomics: ClustalW|MultipleAlign|SequenceAligner|SubstitutionMatrix|Align, no Fuzzing / Properties / Metamorphic) + MCP.Alignment 145 passed / 0 failed; solution build 0 errors.

### F64 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10d, file part): ClustalW user-defined matrix files (`-MATRIX=file`, `-PWMATRIX=file`, series files) were not supported
- Defect (gap): `ClustalWProteinOptions` / `ClustalWProteinPairwiseOptions` knew only the built-in matrices; the program's `readUserMatrix` / `readMatrixSeries` (NCBI / EMBOSS-style and ClustalW matrix files, `CLUSTAL_SERIES` files with percent-identity ranges) had no counterpart.
- Sources actually opened (sources.debian.org, clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7 `src/`): `substitutionMatrix/SubMatrix.cpp` (`readUserMatrix`, `readMatrixSeries`, `getUserMatFromFile`, `getUserMatSeriesFromFile`, `getMatrix`, `getProfileAlignMatrix` user branches, `getPairwiseMatrix`, `commentline`, `setCurrentNameAndNum`), `interface/CommandLineParser.cpp` (`-MATRIX`, `-PWMATRIX`, `-DNAMATRIX`, `-PWDNAMATRIX` handlers), `general/{UserParameters.cpp,Utility.cpp,clustalw.h}` (`aminoAcidCodes`, `maxAA`, `NUMRES`, `MAXMAT`, `lineType`, `SeriesMat`, `PrfScaleValues`).
- Fix (additive): `ClustalWUserMatrix.Parse / ReadFile / FromSubstitutionMatrix` (+ `Cells()`, `ToSubstitutionMatrix()` = the canonical `SubstitutionMatrix`), `ClustalWMatrixFile.Parse / ReadFile` (single matrix or series, injected file reader), `ClustalWMatrixSeriesEntry` (`ClustalWUserMatrix.cs`, Infrastructure); `ClustalWProteinOptions.UserMatrix / UserMatrixSeries`, `ClustalWProteinPairwiseOptions.UserMatrix`; the built-in tables now flow through the same generic `ClustalWGetMatrix` (port of `getMatrix`: cells scattered through `Xref`, `matrixAvgScore` with the x86 cast, shift) - `BuildClustalWProteinMatrix` / `BuildClustalWProfileMatrix` / the profile choice `ClustalWChooseProfileMatrix` (user matrix: intScale 100, scale 0.75; series: first entry with `llimit <= pcid <= ulimit`, else the first, scale `0.5 + (pcid - llimit) / ((ulimit - llimit) * 2)` as a float). MCP `multiple_align_clustalw`: `matrixFile` (text of the file / series), `matrixFiles` (name -> text for the series entries), `pwMatrixFile`.
- Reference cross-check: `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_user_matrix_oracle.py` - installed `clustalw` 2.1 `-TYPE=PROTEIN -ALIGN -OUTPUT=FASTA -OUTORDER=INPUT -MATRIX=file / -PWMATRIX=file`: **240 committed sets** (matrix files embedded; single, series (2-4 entries, tiled / gapped / overlapping limits), pairwise, both; integer / `%.1f` / `%g` rows, labelled / unlabelled, tab separated, header subsets, shuffled residue order, junk above the diagonal, trailers, J / lower-case / 2-3 residue quirk matrices; the option variants of F63): **every output row and 2 000+ printed pairwise `Score:` lines equal**; development run (seed 31337, 800 sets): 2 differences, both `-NOVGAP` (the documented uninitialised `gdist`). Found on the way: the decode of the profile matrix with unique cell ids (`-DEBUG=5`) confirmed the shifted reading of a header with an unknown letter (a first version of the port forgot the `Xref != -1` test of the storing loop and failed 7 / 240 sets, all with a J inside the header - fixed before the commit); a file without the extra `*` row is rejected by the binary ("wrong format in matrix", reproduced as `FormatException`).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWUserMatrix_Tests` x15 (240-set rows, pairwise scores, coverage, discrimination, NCBI layout = BLOSUM62, label / float / triangle / comment / malformed / unknown-letter / series rules, `FromSubstitutionMatrix`), `MultipleAlignTests` (MCP) +1, contract test +1 tool name.
- Left: `-DNAMATRIX=file` / `-PWDNAMATRIX=file` and the end-gap options: done in F68 / F67 (A1-10d parts 3 / 2).

### F66 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-13b): the T-Coffee program's own primary libraries, default guide tree and default method (`proba_pair`) were not ported (F58 residual)
- Defect (gap): `MultipleAlignTCoffee` / `TCoffeeLinked` reproduced the program only for an explicit library and tree; `t_coffee seqs.fa` and `t_coffee -method slow_pair,lalign_id_pair` differed in most sets (21 / 150 end to end). F58's model of `slow_pair` (+10 / -1, "gop -11") was wrong in two places (the matrix, the gap formula).
- Sources actually opened (sources.debian.org t-coffee 13.45.0.4846264+really13.41.0.28bdc39+dfsg-1, `t_coffee_source/`, downloaded as text into an isolated directory, never compiled): `t_coffee_lib/t_coffee.c`, `util_lib/util_constraints_list.c`, `dp_lib/util_dp_drivers.c`, `util_dp_mm_nw.c`, `util_dp_sim.c`, `util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c`, `util_dp_fasta_nw.c`, `evaluate.c`, `evaluate_dirichlet.c`, `util_lib/aln_convertion_util.c`, `util.c`, `util_make_tree.c`, `util_job_handling.c`, `io_lib/io_func.c`, `io_lib/tree_util.c`, `matrices.h`, `coffee_defines.h`.
- Findings (all measured against the installed program): `read_matrice("dna_idmat")` = array `idmat` (+10 / 0), GOP -10 / GEP -1 from the method file (protein `slow_pair`: GOP `(int)(10 x mean mismatch of blosum62mt)`, `lalign_id_pair`: BLOSUM50, -10 / -4), the fitted "gop -11" is `g + h k`; the Myers-Miller routine is not optimal for its own model (TTTCAACTC / TTTTCAACTC -> `T-TTCAACTC`); SIM residues are mapped to the sequence by a second Myers-Miller alignment (`fix_seq_seq`, identity matrix, -4 / -1, also for proteins), which puts pairs outside the local alignments into the library; several methods are merged by forked processes that return list POSITIONS (`freeze_constraint_list`), so the merge loses and duplicates entries and the in-memory list is asymmetric (independent of `-n_core`); type decision > 98 % `AGCTUNRYMKSWHBVD`; `proba_pair` = ProbCons 1.1 pair-HMM in float arithmetic (the DNA model is built with the protein initial distribution; residues outside the 20-letter code have an all-zero profile); default tree = `ktup_dist_mat` + `slow_nj_tree` / `fast_nj_tree` (>= 100 sequences) + `reroot`; names that are prefixes of one another (s1 / s10) change the program's tree (oracle names q000...).
- Fix (additive): `SequenceAligner.MultipleAlignTCoffeeProgram(IEnumerable<string>, TCoffeeProgramOptions?, MatrixScoring?)`, `TCoffeePrimaryLibraryEntries`, `TCoffeeGuideTree`, `TCoffeeKtupSimilarity`, `TCoffeeSlowPairAlign`, `TCoffeeLalignTopAlignments`, `TCoffeePairMethod`, `TCoffeeProgramOptions` (`SequenceAligner.TCoffeeLibrary.cs`, `.TCoffeePrimary.cs`, `.TCoffeeProba.cs`, `.TCoffeeProbaTables.cs`, `.TCoffeeMatrices.cs`, `.TCoffeeTree.cs`; the rooting code of the ClustalW port is shared, `TcIndex.FromConstraintList` feeds the existing progressive stage), MCP `multiple_align_tcoffee`. `scripts/alignment/generate_tcoffee_program_oracle.py`.
- Reference cross-check (installed `t_coffee`, `-n_core 1`): stage a `slow_pair` / b `lalign_id_pair` / d `proba_pair` libraries + merges: **820 / 820 committed library sets equal** (every pair, weight, count; DNA + protein, both merge orders, 3 methods); stage c + end to end: **1 480 / 1 480 committed sets equal in guide tree and rows** (DNA / protein plain default 260 + 260, `slow_pair,lalign_id_pair` 260 + 260, single methods, reverse order, 3 methods; 2-8 sequences) + 100 / 101 sequences (fast NJ). Development runs (a few thousand further sets incl. 1-6 residue, near-identical, two-letter alphabets, `-n_core` 2 / 4 / 8): all equal.
- Residual: registered as new DOABLE items A1-13c (RNA `proba_pair`: `get_tot_prob`, 5-state RNA model) and A1-13d (other `-method`s, `-tree_mode upgma`, `-quicktree`, `-seq_weight`, `-relax_lib` > 1); the name-prefix quirk and a rooting tie that crashes the program are not reproduced.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeProgram_Tests` x13, MCP `MultipleAlignTests` +1, registration list +1. Targeted tier (`TCoffee|Consistency|MultipleAlign|SequenceAligner`, Fuzzing/Properties/Metamorphic excluded) 564 passed / 0 failed; `Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests` 148 passed; solution build 0 errors / 0 warnings; `gen-catalog.py --check` fresh (503 tools), `check-links.py` OK.
