### F67 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10d part 2): ClustalW's end-gap options of the profile stage and the program's abort were not reachable
- Defect (gap): `UserParameters::useEndGaps` / `endGapPenalties` were ported (F56 / F63) but hard-wired to `false` in `CwConfig.FromDna / FromProtein`; ClustalW's behaviour when a first-pass profile alignment scores below zero (silent exit with no output) had no counterpart in the port.
- Sources actually opened (sources.debian.org, clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7 `src/`; the user's returned archive): `multipleAlign/ProfileBase.cpp` (`calcGapCoeff`), `multipleAlign/MyersMillerProfileAlign.cpp` (`profileAlign` returns `score / 100`, `progDiff`, `openPenalty / extPenalty / gapPenalty`), `multipleAlign/MSA.cpp` (`multiSeqAlign`: first pass `if (score < 0) return -1`, second pass ignores the score), `Clustal.cpp` (`align`: `count <= 0 -> return`), `interface/CommandLineParser.cpp` (`-ENDGAPS` writes `false`), `interface/InteractiveMenu.cpp`.
- Oracle: the user ran the Docker package `handoff/A1-10d` (archive committed: `returned/A1-10d-out.zip`, "B09 A1-10d: returned ClustalW end-gap oracle archive (user-run)"): clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7 built from the Debian source with the single edit that the `-ENDGAPS` handler reads `CW_USEENDGAPS` / `CW_ENDGAPPEN`; `control_check.txt`: control rows = installed binary on 340 / 340 sets. 340 sets (120 DNA + 220 protein) x 4 modes -> `clustalw_end_gaps_oracle.tsv` (`scripts/alignment/build_clustalw_end_gaps_oracle.py`). In the modes `endgappen` / `both` the program wrote no output (rc 0) for 34 sets (31 DNA + 3 protein): the diagnosis (scratch copy of the port with a score hook; one example re-verified in the repo) is `MSA.cpp` 150-155 `score < 0 -> return -1` + `Clustal.cpp` 205-208; a negative optimum needs end-gap penalties (the all-end-gap alignment costs 0), the score is `optimum / 100` with truncating `int` division (so (-100, 0) does not abort) and only the first pass checks. The five sets D113 / P116 / P221 / P61 / P68 (all `-QUICKTREE`) that differed from the oracle in the diagnostician's scratch harness (also in control mode) are equal in the repo in all four modes: the scratch harness mapped the options differently (the repo's oracle tests use the `-KTUPLE` => pair gap = ktuple + 3 / + 4 mapping); no option-mapping bug in the port.
- Fix (additive): `ClustalWOptions.UseEndGaps / EndGapPenalties` and `ClustalWProteinOptions.UseEndGaps / EndGapPenalties` (`AlignmentTypes.cs`) feed `CwConfig`; `ProgDiff` returns the optimum (as C++ `progDiff`), `ProfileAlign` returns `score / 100`, `MultiSeqAlign` throws **`ClustalWAbortedException`** (`InvalidOperationException`; `Group` = number of the first-pass profile alignment, `Score` < 0) for a negative first-pass score - the documented contract replacing "no output file"; MCP `multiple_align_clustalw` `useEndGaps`, `endGapPenalties` (the abort is a tool error "ClustalW 2.1 abandons the alignment ...").
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWEndGaps_Tests` x9 (340 control + 340 `useendgaps` rows, 306 + 306 rows and 34 + 34 aborts of `endgappen` / `both` = all 1 360 oracle rows, discrimination, abort contract), MCP `MultipleAlignTests` +1. Targeted tier see WP18 below.
- Commit: a29446c5.

### F68 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10d part 3): ClustalW nucleotide matrices `-DNAMATRIX=IUB | CLUSTALW | file`, `-PWDNAMATRIX=...`, `-TRANSWEIGHT` rewrite of a user matrix were not supported
- Defect (gap): `MultipleAlignClustalW` (DNA) knew only the built-in IUB matrix; `-DNAMATRIX=CLUSTALW`, `-PWDNAMATRIX=CLUSTALW` (clustalvdnamt with the gap scales 0.66 / 0.6667 / 0.751) and the user-file branches of `getProfileAlignMatrix` / `getPairwiseMatrix` (userDNAMat / DNAXref, transition weights through the matrix' own Xref) had no counterpart; the DNA pairwise stage had no options overload (`-TRANSWEIGHT`, `-PWDNAMATRIX`).
- Sources actually opened (sources.debian.org, clustalw 2.1+lgpl-7 `src/`): `substitutionMatrix/SubMatrix.cpp` (`getPairwiseMatrix`, `getProfileAlignMatrix` DNA branches, `getMatrix`, `getUserMatFromFile`, `readUserMatrix`, `setCurrentNameAndNum`, `getUserMatAddress / getUserXrefAddress`), `substitutionMatrix/matrices.h` (`swgapdnamt`, `clustalvdnamt`), `interface/CommandLineParser.cpp` (the `-DNAMATRIX` / `-PWDNAMATRIX` handlers), `interface/InteractiveMenu.cpp` (`readMatrix`, `multiMenu`, pairwise menu), `pairwise/FullPairwiseAlign.cpp`, `multipleAlign/MyersMillerProfileAlign.cpp`. **Findings:** (1) `-DNAMATRIX=file` / `-PWDNAMATRIX=file` read and validate the file but then call `setCurrentNameAndNum(name, 3, Protein, ...)` - the PROTEIN slot - so the DNA stages keep `iub`: the command-line option has NO effect on a DNA alignment (a defect of the program, confirmed against the binary: identical scores and rows); the interactive menu registers the file for DNA, so there the matrix is used; (2) the menu's built-in items register an empty name (`menu.opt[i - 1].string` after the loop) and are unusable, but `-DNAMATRIX=CLUSTALW` / `-PWDNAMATRIX=CLUSTALW` on the command line work; (3) the profile stage indexes the matrix with `(*_matXref)[0 / 4 / 2 / 11 / 12]` (header positions), the pairwise stage with the residue codes 0 / 4 / 2 / 11 / 12 (so only ambiguity codes move there).
- Fix (additive): `ClustalWOptions.DnaMatrix / PairwiseDnaMatrix` (`ClustalWDnaMatrix` = `Iub` | `Clustalw`), `UserDnaMatrix / PairwiseUserDnaMatrix` (`ClustalWUserMatrix`, the F64 reader) and `UserDnaMatrixUse` (`Applied` = the menu behaviour, default; `CommandLine` = `clustalw -DNAMATRIX=file` exactly: read, validated, ignored), `ClustalWDnaPairwiseOptions` + `SequenceAligner.ClustalWPairwiseAlign(string | DnaSequence, ..., ClustalWDnaPairwiseOptions)`; the built-in DNA tables now flow through the same generic `ClustalWGetMatrix` / `BuildClustalWProfileMatrix` as the protein tables (`ClustalWDnaTableCells`, `ClustalWProfileDnaMatrixSource`, `ClustalWDnaPairwiseSetup`; `clustalvdnamt` = +10 on the A, C, G, T, U diagonal); MCP `multiple_align_clustalw` `dnaMatrix`, `pwDnaMatrix`, `dnaMatrixFile`, `pwDnaMatrixFile`, `userDnaMatrixUse`.
- Reference cross-check: `scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_dna_matrix_oracle.py` - the installed `clustalw` 2.1, **360 committed sets** (125 command-line runs `-TYPE=DNA -ALIGN -OUTPUT=FASTA -OUTORDER=INPUT -DNAMATRIX / -PWDNAMATRIX`: IUB / CLUSTALW (35), files (75; layouts: NCBI / EMBOSS with comments / labelled rows / `*` row, unlabelled, integer / floating-point rows, tab separated, junk above the diagonal, trailers, header orders and 4-17 letters), malformed files (15: no output); 235 interactive-menu runs `clustalw -INTERACTIVE -TYPE=DNA` through stdin with a user profile and / or pairwise matrix and the menu's gap / transition / delay / negative / fast items): **every output row (345 sets), all 3 485 printed pairwise `Score:` lines (full stage) and all 1 393 printed first-pass `Group g: Sequences: n Score: s` / `Delayed` lines equal the binary**; exception = undefined behaviour of the C++: a user profile matrix whose header has fewer than 13 residues leaves `Xref[0 / 2 / 4 / 11 / 12]` at -1 and clustalw writes outside its `matrix[NUMRES][NUMRES]` array (8 of 37 such menu runs crashed with SIGSEGV and are not in the oracle; 28 of the 29 others equal the port, which drops the writes). Findings: the command-line options with a FILE have no effect on a DNA alignment (PROTEIN-slot defect, rows and scores identical to the run without them); the menu applies the file; `-DNAMATRIX=CLUSTALW` changes the group scores (865 -> 450 on one set); the menu classifies a set with >= 15 % non-ACGTUN letters as protein unless `-TYPE=DNA` is given (first oracle run: 3 pairwise scores of such a set differed - an oracle-generation defect found and fixed by starting the menu with `-INTERACTIVE -TYPE=DNA`).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWDnaMatrix_Tests` x9 (rows / group scores / pairwise scores on the oracle, malformed files, coverage, discrimination, command-line vs applied, IUB-equivalent user file, option contracts), MCP `MultipleAlignTests` +1. Targeted tier see WP18 below.
- Commit: a29446c5.

### F69 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10c part a): the MUSCLE 3.8.1551 oracle (user-run Docker package) is equal to the port on 420 / 420 sets
- Defect: none found. F65 was "implemented and checked against definitions"; the binary was not available (installed muscle = 5.x; the sandbox refused to run the compiled v3 source), so the user ran the Docker hand-off package `handoff/A1-10c-muscle` on their Mac (`ENV.txt`: MUSCLE v3.8.1551 by Robert C. Edgar, built unmodified from the Debian `muscle3` 3.8.1551-2 source, x86_64, Debian 12.7; binary sha256 `ab1a833e93c972e627c9e1802d1d62cb345d746caff26ef763b49b0b05e08b67`; `run.log`: 420 sets run, 0 failed). The returned archive was committed alone (`returned/A1-10c-muscle-out.zip`, 5888980d), untrusted data parsed with `python3 -I` and never executed.
- Oracle: `scripts/alignment/generate_muscle3_oracle.py` -> `TestData/Alignment/muscle3_oracle.tsv` (embedded; 420 sets: 270 protein, 110 DNA, 40 RNA; options `-maxiters 1|2`, `-le/-sp/-sv`, `-gapopen/-gapextend/-center`, `-distance1 kmer6_6`, `-cluster1/-cluster2` upgma / upgmb / upgmamin / upgmamax, `-sueff`, `-maxtrees 0`; leading M, wildcards, lower case, duplicates, sequences shorter than 5 residues, two-sequence sets). Test `SequenceAligner_MuscleOracle_Tests`: for every set the rows of `MultipleAlignMuscle` (input order) AND the order MUSCLE wrote them (`TreeOrder`, depth-first leaf order of the final tree) equal the binary's: **420 / 420 rows, 420 / 420 orders (841 test cases), 0 differences** - no stage had to be corrected, so no scratch re-derivation of intermediate quantities was needed.
- Platform caveat: the single-precision logf of the LE score and the C++ float / double mixing could differ by 1 ulp between libm / compiler versions; the oracle was produced by g++ on Debian 12 / x86_64 and no set differs in this implementation (.NET `MathF.Log`); a future libm difference would show up as an isolated column difference in `SequenceAligner_MuscleOracle_Tests`, not as a systematic one.
- Commits: 5888980d (archive), d48f0ff5 (oracle TSV + test).

### F70 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10c part a2): the other options and command lines of MUSCLE 3.8.1551 (iterations >= 3, anchors, objective scores, weights, distances, neighbour joining, roots, ...) implemented from the source (verified against the binary in F76: 630 / 630 recorded sets)
- Defect (gap): `MultipleAlignMuscle` ported `-maxiters 1 | 2` only (`MaxIterations > 2` threw); the program's default `-maxiters` is 8 (`params.cpp`: `g_uMaxIters = 8`; the usage text says 16), so the program's default behaviour - restricted-partition refinement inside anchor blocks - was not reproducible; no sequence-weight, distance, clustering, rooting, objective-score, `-matrix`, `-usetree` or output-file option; none of the command lines that work on existing alignments.
- Sources actually opened (sources.debian.org muscle3 3.8.1551-2, the tree fetched in F65, text only, never executed - the sandbox refuses to run it, so the verification is the Docker round 2): `options.cpp` (the complete option list), `params.cpp`, `muscle.cpp`, `domuscle.cpp`, `refinehoriz.cpp`, `refinevert.cpp`, `scorehistory.cpp`, `aligntwomsas.cpp`, `anchors.cpp`, `objscore.cpp`, `objscore2.cpp`, `spfast.cpp`, `diffobjscore.cpp`, `msa2.cpp`, `henikoffweight.cpp`, `henikoffweightpb.cpp`, `setgscweights.cpp`, `setblosumweights.cpp`, `cluster.cpp`, `threewaywt.cpp`, `clwwt.cpp`, `progressivealign.cpp`, `progalign.cpp`, `refinetree.cpp`, `difftrees.cpp`, `realigndiffs.cpp`, `profilefrommsa.cpp`, `fastdist.cpp` / `fastdistkmer.cpp` / `fastdistjones.cpp` / `fastdistkbit.cpp`, `distfunc.cpp`, `distcalc.cpp`, `distpwkimura.cpp`, `scoredist.cpp`, `msadist.h`, `clust.cpp`, `fastclust.cpp`, `treefrommsa.cpp`, `phy.cpp`, `phy2.cpp`, `phy3.cpp`, `phy4.cpp`, `phyfromclust.cpp`, `phyfromfile.cpp`, `phytofile.cpp`, `upgma2.cpp`, `termgaps.cpp`, `makerootmsab.cpp`, `outweights.cpp`, `readmx.cpp`, `profile.cpp`, `refine.cpp`, `dosp.cpp`, `ppscore.cpp`, `local.cpp`, `sw.cpp`, `tracebacksw.cpp`, `aligngivenpathsw.cpp`, `profdb.cpp`, `refinew.cpp`, `maketree.cpp`, `mhack.cpp`, `muscleout.cpp`, `glbalign.cpp`, `glbalign352.cpp`, `muscle.h`, `types.h`, `enums.h`, `alpha.cpp`, `blosum62.cpp`.
- Source findings that decide behaviour (each needs the round-2 confirmation): (1) `muscle.h` has `VER_3_52 = 0`: `GlobalAlign` is always `NWSmall`; the diagonal dynamic program (`-diags`, `-diags1`, `-diags2`, `-diaglength`, `-diagbreak`, `-diagmargin`) sits in the `#else` branch of `glbalign.cpp` and in `glbalign352.cpp` under `#if VER_3_52`, so **these options have no effect**; (2) `#if HYDRO` blocks (`-hydro`, `-hydrofactor`, `-tomhydro`) and `DOUBLE_AFFINE = 0` (`-gapopen2`, `-gapextend2`) are compiled out: no effect; `-gapambig` only fills `g_scoreGapAmbig`, never read; (3) `SetMuscleTree` computes `g_MuscleWeights` by LEAF INDEX (`CalcClustalWWeights`) but `SetClustalWWeightsMuscle` / `ProgressiveAlignE` read it by sequence ID: after the tree refinement (leaf order = row order, ids permuted) and for `-usetree` the weights are therefore attached to the wrong sequences - reproduced; (4) `ReadMx` reads `HeadingCount - 1` columns: a matrix file with a trailing `*` column is read completely, one without it loses its last residue column; (5) `DistKbit20_3` increments the position twice per loop (every second residue is skipped) and `CommonBitCount` counts the bits set in either vector (`b & 0x101`; **corrected by the oracle in F76: union + the second vector again**), `DistKmer20_3` stores the shared triples per length (a similarity) as the distance; (6) `RootByMinAvgLeafDist` computes `x = (ADR2 - ADL1 + L) / 2` with `ADL1` taken from the far end of the edge (its comments say the opposite) - the program's arithmetic is reproduced; (7) `SetPPScore` silently replaces a protein score by SPN for nucleotides and SPN by LE for proteins (the F65 port threw); (8) `-ppscore`, `-sw`, `-refinew` call `SetPPScore(PPSCORE_SPN)` only for nucleotides: for a protein `g_ptrScoreMatrix` stays null and the program crashes; `-profdb` never sets the alphabet; `-sw` builds the profile trees with `-cluster1/-distance1` (the default nucleotide k-mer distance is undefined for aligned sequences and stops the program unless a profile has one sequence); (9) `MUSCLE()` (used by `-refinew`) forces `-distance1 kmer4_6` for nucleotides; (10) `ScoreHistory` stops the refinement of an edge when its best score repeats a score of an earlier iteration (with `TryRealign` returning 0 / 0 for an unchanged path, an edge that did not change for two iterations ends the whole refinement).
- Fix (additive, backward compatible; defaults of the old API unchanged: `MaxIterations` default stays 2, the program's 8 must be asked for): `MuscleOptions` + `MuscleSeqWeight`, `MuscleObjectiveScore`, `MuscleTermGaps`, `MuscleRoot`, `MuscleClusterMethod.NeighborJoining`, `MuscleDistance` (kmer20_3, kmer20_4, kbit20_3, pctid_kimura, pctid_log, pwkimura, pwscoredist, scoredist, edit), `MuscleRunResult`; `SequenceAligner.MuscleRun` (files: `-tree1 -tree2 -distmx1 -distmx2 -computeweights`, `-clusteronly`), `MuscleProfileAlign`, `MuscleRefine`, `MuscleScoreAlignment`, `MuscleProfileScore`, `MuscleGuideTree`, `MuscleRefineWindows`, `MuscleLocalAlign`; engine files `SequenceAligner.MuscleRefine.cs` (RefineHoriz / RefineVert, objective scores SP / DP / XP / PS / SPF / SPM, anchors, weights none / Henikoff / Henikoff-PB / ClustalW / three-way, non-low-complexity pipeline: `ProgressiveAlign` + `RefineTree` + `DiffTrees` + `RealignDiffs`), `SequenceAligner.MuscleWeights.cs` (GSC cluster tree, three-way weights), `SequenceAligner.MuscleTrees.cs` (distances, neighbour joining `Clust`, `FixRoot` / `FindRoot` / `UnrootByDeletingRoot` / `RootUnrootedTree` / `OrientParent`), `SequenceAligner.MuscleTreeRefine.cs`, `SequenceAligner.MuscleProgram.cs` (modes, `-matrix` reader, Newick reader / writer, `-brenner`). MCP: `multiple_align_muscle` gains all options (new parameters appended, result `MuscleRunResultDto` adds names, order, trees, distance matrices, weights) and the new tool `muscle_msa_command` (modes profile / refine / spscore / ppscore / maketree / refinew / sw).
- Reference cross-check: **no oracle for these options yet** (the binary only runs in the user's Docker). Locked in `SequenceAligner_MuscleOptions_Tests` (41 tests): Henikoff / Henikoff-PB / GSC / three-way weights on hand traces of the source (0.375 / 0.25 / 0.375; GSC 0.25 / 0.25 / 0.5; three-way 0.2 / 0.2 / 0.6), the SP objective and its terminal-gap rules (`-termgaps half / full / ext`) with a BLOSUM62 matrix file (3.25 / 0.75), XP / SPF = SP consistency, the anchor columns of a constant alignment (4, 12, 20, 28, 36), the k-mer definitions (kmer20_3 0.8, kmer20_4 0.25 / 0.99, kbit20_3 4/9 and 5/9 - now 8/9 and 1 after F76), neighbour joining reproducing an additive matrix, rooting (path lengths preserved, midpoint of the longest span, the program's min-average-distance relation), `DiffTrees` on the example of its source comment, `%g` formatting, tree / distance / weight file formats, the Newick reader (node numbering of the program, errors), the matrix reader incl. the lost last column, the options that must have no effect, validity of the refinement for every objective x weighting x anchors combination (72) and every clustering / distance / root combination, the MSA modes. **Round 2 package `handoff/A1-10c-muscle-r2` (638 deterministic sets: 449 align, 36 profile, 35 refine, 22 spscore, 23 maketree, 27 ppscore, 18 refinew, 23 sw, 5 profdb; the unmodified binary) was built and dry-run with a stub binary; the user must run `./run.sh` in that directory (Docker, `--platform linux/amd64` is set by the script) and return `returned/A1-10c-muscle-r2-out.zip`.**
- Marked "implemented from the source, awaiting oracle" at the time (NOT ticked): everything above except what F69 verified. **Superseded by F76: the round-2 oracle verified these options (630 / 630 recorded sets); the residual is A1-10c(a3).**
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_MuscleOptions_Tests` x41 (new), `SequenceAligner_MuscleOracle_Tests` x841, `SequenceAligner_Muscle_Tests` 20 (option validation updated: `MaxIterations 3` supported, wrong-alphabet score replaced like the program), MCP `MultipleAlignTests` +2 (`Seqeron.Mcp.Alignment.Tests` 152 passed), contract test +1 tool; `gen-catalog.py --check` fresh (503 tools).
- Left: the oracle comparison (round 2), see the package README; mode `-profdb` and sequences shorter than 4 (kmer20_4) / 2 (kmer20_3, kbit20_3) residues are undefined in the program (it reads outside its buffers) and rejected / handled conservatively.
- Commit: b58172e6.

### F71 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-13c): `proba_pair` of RNA input (a U) was not ported (`NotSupportedException`)
- Defect (gap): for more than 98 % nucleotide letters with at least one U the program's type is `RNA` and `proba_pair_wise` takes a separate branch (`util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c` L752-L811): 5 matrix types, 2 insert-state pairs, `RNAgapOpen2Default` / `RNAgapExtend2Default`, the six-letter table "ACGUTN" with 1e-5 / 1e-10 for every other letter, and `get_tot_prob` (L924) instead of `get_tot_prob2`: the residues themselves index the emission tables (case-insensitively) instead of the 20-letter dirichlet profile (in which U, X, B ... have an all-zero profile). The call passes `initDistrib2Default` (the protein initial distribution), not `RNAinitDistrib2Default`.
- Sources actually opened: sources.debian.org t-coffee 13.45.0.4846264+really13.41.0.28bdc39+dfsg-1 `t_coffee_source/dp_lib/util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c` (`proba_pair_wise`, `get_tot_prob`, `get_tot_prob2`), `io_lib/io_func.c` (`get_string_type`: `dna > 98 && rna > 0` -> RNA), `util_lib/util.c` (`is_rna` = U / u).
- Fix: `TcSeqKind` (Dna / Rna / Protein; `TcSeqType`), `TcProbaModel.For(kind)` with the RNA model (shared forward / backward), `TcProbaPairEntries` with the `get_tot_prob` residue lookup; no new public API (RNA input now works in `MultipleAlignTCoffeeProgram` and the MCP tool). The old `NotSupportedException` assertion is replaced by a positive one.
- Reference cross-check (installed `t_coffee` 13.41, `-n_core 1`): **430 / 430** committed RNA sets equal (260 plain `proba_pair`, 60 `-method proba_pair`, 40 `slow_pair,lalign_id_pair`, 40 `proba_pair,slow_pair,lalign_id_pair`, 30 `lalign_id_pair,proba_pair`; 2-8 sequences of 3-36 nt); development runs (short sets, 100 sets of 8-40 nt, all method mixes) all equal.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeOptions_Tests.Rna_EveryRowEqualsTheProgram` (430 sets), `MultipleAlignTCoffee_...` (MCP) RNA example.
- Commit: 8d215cc1.

### F72 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-13d part 1): `-relax_lib N`, `-filter_lib`, `-gapopen`, `-gapext`, `-cosmetic_penalty`, `-nomatch`, `-do_normalise`, `-matrix` and `method@EP@GOP|GEP|MATRIX@value` of the progressive stage and the pair methods
- Defect (gap): only `-relax_lib 0 | 1`, zero gap penalties and the methods' own matrices were reproduced; every other flag changed the program's rows (measured: relax_lib 0 / 2: 14-16 of 24 sets differ, `-filter_lib 20` 4-5, `-gapopen -50` 14-15, `-gapext -2` 7-13, `-cosmetic_penalty 5` 7-18, `-matrix blosum50mt` 19-20, `-dp_mode ...` 15-19 of 24).
- Sources actually opened: `t_coffee.c` (L5035-5050 penalties, L5109-5172 order of the library operations), `util_constraints_list.c` (`relax_constraint_list`, `filter_constraint_list`, `shrink_constraint_list`, `evaluate_constraint_list_reference` L1483, method-file parameters `@EP@`, L1096-1125), `util_dp_gotoh_nw.c` (`list2linked_pair_wise` L2363, `cl2list_borders` L1790), `util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c` (`get_emitPairs` L2005), `util_dp_drivers.c` (`method2pw_cl`, matrix / GOP / GEP selection), `io_func.c` (`read_matrice`), `matrices.h` (all 38 matrices, transcribed by `scripts/alignment/generate_tcoffee_matrix_tables.py`).
- Findings: (1) `-gapopen` / `-gapext` are the `gop` / `gep` of the sparse DP; `gop` is 0 for a cell in the last row or column; (2) **the border cells of `cl2list_borders` carry the score `gep x SCORE_K x position`** - this is why a gap extension penalty acts ten times stronger at the borders than a per-step cost would (found by comparing the penalty at which `t_coffee` flips the alignment of one pair with ours: gap open flips at the same value, gap extension 10 x earlier); (3) `-cosmetic_penalty` is the opening penalty only when `-gapopen` and `-gapext` are both 0; (4) `-nomatch` is rescaled by `-do_normalise` (1000) / the largest sum of the weights of the entries of a residue (`evaluate_constraint_list_reference`, which runs after the relaxation and again after the re-weighting); (5) `-filter_lib T` keeps weights > T before the relaxations, `-relax_lib N` relaxes N times (each relaxation includes the filter > 10); (6) `-matrix` reaches `proba_pair` through `get_emitPairs` (p = s x s x exp(score x ln 2 / 2) normalised, single precision) and the PROTEIN `slow_pair` (matrix and `GOP = (int)(10 x mean mismatch)` of that matrix); DNA `slow_pair` and `lalign_id_pair` carry their own `MATRIX` line (no effect); (7) `-extend_mode`, `-extend`, `-evaluate_mode`, `-shrink_lib` (recounts only), `-tg_mode`, `-ktuple`, `-n_core` have no effect on the rows (0 / 24 sets, DNA and protein).
- Fix (additive): `TCoffeeProgramOptions.RelaxLibIterations`, `FilterLib`, `GapOpen`, `GapExtend`, `CosmeticPenalty`, `NoMatch`, `DoNormalise`, `Matrix`, `MethodParameters` (`TCoffeeMethodParameters`: GOP / GEP / MATRIX), `TCoffeeProgramOptions.FromCommandLine / WithCommandLine` (t_coffee flag strings; unknown flags are rejected by name), `TCoffeeSlowPairAlign / TCoffeeLalignTopAlignments(..., matrix / parameters)`, `TcPenalties` in the progressive DP, `TcMatrixCatalog` (generated), MCP `multiple_align_tcoffee` `options` / `names`.
- Reference cross-check (`t_coffee` 13.41): relax / filter 500 sets, gap model 560 sets, `-matrix` 675 sets (DNA, protein, RNA; blosum30/50/62/80, pam120/250, md_250, idmat, est_idmat; with `slow_pair`, with all three methods), method parameters 390 sets (3 of them crash the program: F75): all equal except one set (protein `-nomatch 50 -gapext -1`, 7 sequences: the `nomatch` rescaling differs in one case of 1 030 - not found, listed as a known difference).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeOptions_Tests` (relax / filter, gap model, matrix, method parameters), MCP `MultipleAlignTCoffee_Options_AndNames_...`.
- Commit: 8d215cc1.

### F73 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-13d part 2): guide tree and sequence weights - `-tree_mode upgma`, `-distance_matrix_mode ktup | fast | idscore | no`, `-distance_matrix_sim_mode`, `-seq_weight t_coffee | file`, `-weight no`
- Defect (gap): measured against the default: `-tree_mode upgma` 7-9 of 24 sets differ, `-distance_matrix_mode fast` 6-8, `slow` 8-12, `-quicktree` 5-7, `-seq_weight t_coffee` 3-4.
- Sources actually opened: `util_constraints_list.c` (`seq2distance_matrix` L4930-5240, `weight_constraint_list`, `compute_t_coffee_weight`, `re_weight_constraint_list`), `util_dp_drivers.c` (`make_tree`), `util_make_tree.c` (`int_dist2upgma_tree`, `upgma_merge`), `aln_convertion_util.c` (`get_seq_sim`, `is_in_same_group_aa`, `make_group_aa`, `sim_array2dist_array`, `normalize_array`), `util_dp_gotoh_nw.c` (`idscore_pairseq`), `reformat.c` (`read_seq_weight`: file format `SINGLE_SEQ_WEIGHT_FORMAT_01`).
- Findings: (1) the distances are `normalize_array(sim_array2dist_array(score, 1000), 1000, 100)` of the `score` matrix: ktup / fast score = identity x 10, **idscore score = identity (not scaled: all distances ~ 90-100)**, no = 1000; (2) UPGMA merges the smallest integer distance (strict `<`, first on ties) and the new row is the integer mean; `read_tree` delivers the two children in the opposite order, so the higher-index subtree is aligned first (found by the 4 / 100 mismatches of the natural order); (3) `fast` = the Myers-Miller alignment of `slow_pair` (idmat -10 / -1 or blosum62mt) + `get_seq_sim` with `idmat_sim1 | 2 | 3 | gap1 | logid`; the residue groups of `is_in_same_group_aa` are a process-static made by the FIRST caller (a library method that ran earlier uses the matrix `idmat`: T and U together, J / O never equal; otherwise one group per letter); (4) `-seq_weight t_coffee`: `compute_t_coffee_weight` in single precision, the library entries are multiplied by MIN of the two sequence weights and truncated; a weight file needs the header `SINGLE_SEQ_WEIGHT_FORMAT_01` (an invalid file made the program stop: an earlier "test" of mine read a stale output); `-weight no` skips the weighting.
- Fix (additive): `TCoffeeTreeMode`, `TCoffeeDistanceMode`, `TCoffeeProgramOptions.TreeMode / DistanceMatrixMode / DistanceMatrixSimMode / TCoffeeSequenceWeights / SequenceWeights` (`SequenceAligner.TCoffeeDistance.cs`).
- Reference cross-check: tree modes + distance modes + sim modes 730 sets, sequence weights 470 sets (incl. 60 random weight files and `-seq_weight t_coffee` with idscore + UPGMA): all equal. Residual (1 committed set of 470 + 1 development set): protein, `-seq_weight t_coffee -distance_matrix_mode idscore -tree_mode upgma` (8 sequences; minimal reproduction 5 sequences) and `-distance_matrix_mode fast -seq_weight t_coffee -method slow_pair` (1 of ~1 100 development sets): the sequence weights equal the program's (2-decimal `-outseqweight` output; an explicit weight file gives the program the same rows as `-seq_weight t_coffee`) and the explicit-weight path equals the program on 40 random weight vectors, but with uniform weights 0.04 or 0.05 the rows differ (0.02, 0.03, 0.045, 0.055, 0.06 ... 0.4 equal); the tree, `evaluate`, rounding variants (double product, rounding) were excluded - not found; the committed set is listed in `KnownDifferences` of the test.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeOptions_Tests.TreeModeAndDistanceMatrixMode_...`, `SequenceWeights_...`.
- Commit: 8d215cc1.

### F74 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-13d part 3): the program sorts the sequences by name (`-inorder aligned`, its default) - this is the "name-prefix quirk" of F66; `-outorder`
- Defect: F66 recorded that names that are prefixes of each other (s1 / s10) "change the program's tree" and used q000... names. Reading `t_coffee.c` L4706 (`new_order = sort_string_array(new_order, S->nseq)`; `reorder_seq`) shows the actual mechanism: **with the default `-inorder aligned` the program sorts the sequences by name (`strcmp` byte order) before building the library, the distances and the tree, and restores the input order on output (`-outorder input`)**. For names s0 ... s12 the order is s0 s1 s10 s11 s12 s2 ...; ties are then broken differently. `name_is_in_list` itself is exact for names shorter than 100 characters (`strncmp(name, list[a], 100)`).
- Fix (additive): `TCoffeeProgramOptions.Names` (sorted with `StringComparer.Ordinal`, the pipeline runs on the sorted list and the rows are mapped back), `InOrderInput` (`-inorder input`), `OutOrderAligned` (`-outorder aligned` writes the internal order); without names the names are taken to sort in input order (as q000...). MCP `names`.
- Reference cross-check: 560 sets with shuffled names (4 flags x DNA / protein), prefix names (s1 / s10 mixes) and 11-13 sequences s0 ... s12: all equal.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TCoffeeOptions_Tests.NameOrder_...`.
- Commit: 8d215cc1.

### F75 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-13d part 4): `clustalw_pair`; the deterministic core dump of the program reproduced as `TCoffeeAbortedException`; items blocked / registered
- `clustalw_pair` (the program runs the external `clustalw -INFILE -OUTFILE -OUTORDER=INPUT -align` on every pair; `clustalw` 2.1 is installed): `TCoffeePairMethod.ClustalwPair` aligns every pair with this repository's ClustalW 2.1 ports (DNA and protein) and builds the library like the other methods (weight 10 x percent identity). **399 / 400** committed sets equal; the one difference is the protein pair YAGR / NSMYC (the installed clustalw gives `---YAGR / NSMYC--`, the protein port `-YAGR / NSMYC-`), listed in `KnownDifferences`.
- Deterministic crash: `-method lalign_id_pair@EP@GOP@-5` (and -3) makes `t_coffee` core-dump (`COREDUMP`, rc 1, no alignment) on some sets (3 of 25 committed GOP -5 sets; 1 of 240 random pairs; the default GOP -10 never did). Source (`util_dp_sim.c` L326, L386): the edit script is kept in `S = sim_vcalloc(min(M, N) x 5 / 4 + 1, sizeof(int))`, `sapp = S`; with a small gap open the script of an alignment can be longer. A heap overflow is harmless inside the slack of the glibc chunk and kills the program beyond it: the rule "script entries > ((4 x capacity + 8 + 15) / 16 x 16 - 8) / 4" predicts all 3 crashing sets and none of the 22 others (2 of them overflow the nominal array by 1 and survive; also the 240-pair scan: 1 crash predicted = 1 crash observed). `TCoffeeAbortedException` (`InvalidOperationException`, like `ClustalWAbortedException`) is raised by the SIM port for it; the 3 crashing sets of the oracle file are asserted to raise it.
- "Rooting tie that crashes the program" of F66: not found in 5 000+ development runs of the default pipeline (identical sequences, 1-8 residues, all-zero distances, `-distance_matrix_mode no`); the only deterministic crash found is the SIM overflow above.
- Commit: 8d215cc1.

### F76 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A1-10c part a2): the MUSCLE 3.8.1551 round-2 oracle (user-run, 638 sets) found six defects of the port; all fixed, 630 / 630 recorded sets equal
- Defect: first comparison of `SequenceAligner_MuscleOracleRound2_Tests` (rows in the written order, names, `-tree1/-tree2/-distmx1/-distmx2/-computeweights` files, `SP=%.4g`, `Score=%.4g`, the `-maketree` file, refusals): 602 of 639 test cases equal; the 37 failures are the 8 unrecorded `-matrix blosum62nostar` sets (see Residual) and 29 sets with six causes (the F70 "risks noted" list - leaf-index vs sequence-id weights, `ReadMx` last column, `RootByMinAvgLeafDist`, uninitialised refinement scores - held except the first; the others were new):
  1. **`DistKbit20_3` bit count** (3 sets: R167, R179, R188; nucleotides with `-distance1 kbit20_3` and a non-default `-cluster1`): `CommonBitCount` computes `b = *p1 | (*p2 << 8)` and tests `b & 0x101` in a `while (b)` loop; the shifts 0-7 count the bit positions set in either byte (union), the shifts 8-15 find the bits of `*p2` again at position 0 (position 8 is empty by then), so the count is `|A union B| + |B|` with B = the vector of the sequence with the SMALLER index (F70 had "bits set in either vector").
  2. **`-brenner` after the tree refinement** (10 sets R308-R326 with `-maxiters >= 2`; `-maxiters 1` was equal): `RealignDiffsE` (`realigndiffse.cpp`) makes the root MSA of iteration 2 with `MakeRootMSABrenner` again, i.e. the rows are the sequences in INPUT order (the port kept the depth-first leaf order).
  3. **leaf index vs sequence id in `ProgressiveAlignE`** (5 sets: `-usetree` R361, R366, R375, R382, R383): `CalcClustalWWeights` fills `Weights[]` by leaf INDEX, `progalign.cpp` reads `Weights[uId]` by sequence ID (`Node.m_Weight = Weights[uId]`); the port used the leaf node (equal only when leaf node = id, i.e. for trees built by the program, not for a tree read from a file). F70 reproduced the mix-up in `SetClustalWWeightsMuscle` (refinement) but not here.
  4. **negative zero** (6 sets: the files of R393, R399, R408 and the trees of R556, R557, R560; R547 / R551 / R554 also contain it): `printf("%g", -0.0)` prints `-0`; the distance of identical sequences is `-log(1.0)` = -0.0 (`pctid_kimura`, `pctid_log`), the node heights and edge lengths inherit the sign (`s2:-0`, `-0` in the distance matrices); `FormatG` printed `0`.
  5. **`-maketree` ignores `-distance2`** (3 sets: R547, R551, R554): `maketree.cpp` never calls `SetPPScore`, the only place where `-distance1/2` are parsed (`SetPPCommandLineParams`), so the global default `pctid_kimura` is always used (the same reason makes `-sw` on proteins ignore `-distance1`).
  6. **a one-sequence profile re-rooted** (2 sets: R462 `-profile` protein, R484 RNA, `-root1 midlongestspan`; exit status 139 = segmentation fault): `FixRoot` -> `Tree::UnrootByDeletingRoot` takes `GetLeft(root)` = `NULL_NEIGHBOR` (4294967295) of a one-leaf tree and writes through it. The port skipped the re-rooting; it now refuses (`NotSupportedException`) for every `-root1/-root2` other than `pseudo` on a one-sequence tree.
- Refusals recorded as program behaviour (23 of the 630 recorded sets, each asserted with the matching exception type): `-stable` ("not supported in this version of muscle": `MuscleOptions.Stable` -> `NotSupportedException`, MCP `stable`), `-distance2 edit` with neighbour joining ("MSADist::ComputeDist, invalid DISTANCE_11": `ArgumentException`; the message was misleading), `-sw` on proteins with a multi-sequence profile ("DistCalcMSA: Invalid DISTANCE_1": `ArgumentException`; with one-sequence profiles the program crashes: `NotSupportedException`), neighbour joining of one sequence ("Clust::SetLeafCount(1)", `InvalidOperationException`), `-weight2 henikoffpb` of `-profile` ("g_MuscleWeights = 0", `InvalidOperationException`), protein `-ppscore` / `-refinew` / `-sw` and every `-profdb` (segmentation fault: `NotSupportedException`; new `SequenceAligner.MuscleProfileDatabase`, MCP mode `profdb`, always refused).
- Intermediate quantities: the oracle gives final output only for the alignments, but `-tree1/-tree2/-distmx1/-distmx2/-computeweights` expose the first and second guide trees, the distance matrices and the sequence weights of the progressive stage; every difference above was traced to its stage from those files and the written order (no scratch Python re-implementation was needed: the stage of each difference follows from the file that differs first).
- Oracle: `TestData/Alignment/muscle3_r2_oracle.tsv` (630 rows; embedded) from `scripts/alignment/generate_muscle3_r2_oracle.py` and the archive `handoff/A1-10c-muscle-r2/returned/A1-10c-muscle-r2-out.zip` (committed alone, 10ac6c8b; `ENV.txt`: MUSCLE v3.8.1551, x86_64; parsed with `python3 -I`, never executed). **Residual (8 sets, cause documented): the 8 sets with `-matrix blosum62nostar` (R330, R335, R336, R342, R344, R347, R348, R350) are `RUNNER_ERROR` in the archive - the Docker harness decoded the program's console output as UTF-8 and the "Matrix is not symmetrical" warning of a matrix file without the `*` column prints the byte 0xff; the run itself is not recorded. They are re-run (same ids and data, harness fixed) in the new package `handoff/A1-10c-muscle-r3` (60 sets, together with `-matrix` for nucleotides, one- / two-sequence corners and the score flags `-le/-sp/-sv/-spn`): A1-10c(a3).**
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_MuscleOracleRound2_Tests` (new; 630 sets: 441 align + 189 command lines = 630 oracle cases + 1 inventory test), `SequenceAligner_MuscleOracle_Tests` 841 (still equal), `SequenceAligner_MuscleOptions_Tests` 41, MCP `MultipleAlignTests` +1 (`stable`) and `profdb` in the command test.
- Commits: 10ac6c8b (archive), 2c907943 (fixes, oracle TSV, test, docs, round-3 package).

### F77 — ALIGN-LOCAL-001 (audit A1-7c): Waterman-Eggert top-N above `TopNMaxCells` (25 M cells) was rejected; now exact linear space
- Defect: `LocalAlignTopN` / `LocalAlignAffineTopN` (F47) kept the O(m·n) matrices of the 1987 algorithm and threw `ArgumentException` for `(m+1)(n+1)` > 25 000 000 (two 5 kb sequences, `local_align_top_n`), although SIM / EMBOSS `matcher` produce the same alignments in O(m + n). Repro: `LocalAlignTopN(new string('A', 5001), new string('A', 5001))` -> "Waterman-Eggert declumping ... exceed the limit of 25,000,000 cells".
- Sources actually used: the definition and scheme of F47 (matcher source, Huang-Miller SIM = recomputation per alignment; Huang, Hardison & Miller 1990 / Huang & Miller 1991 records, WebSearch snippets, papers not opened - see A1-22); the exact linear-space local program of F46 (locate-then-solve).
- Fix (`SequenceAligner.TopNLinearSpace.cs` + threading in `SequenceAligner.LinearSpace.cs` / `SequenceAligner.cs`): an internal predicate `ForbiddenPairs` (absolute 1-based (row, column), sorted columns per row) with the forward cursor `ForbiddenRow`; a forbidden cell has no diagonal / M-state move (`ForbiddenPairs.NoDiagonal` / `AffineNegInf`) in `LocateLinearLocal`, `LocateAffine`, `LinearMidpoint`, `AffineMidpoint`, and in the full-matrix base cases of the divide and conquer (`LinearAlignCore` / `AffineAlignCore` with `LinearFillMatrix` / `AffineFill` / `Traceback`, each given the absolute row / column offset of the sub-problem); `LinearSolve` / `AffineSolve` / `LinearSpaceAlign` pass it on. Round k of `LocalTopNLinearSpaceCore` = one `LinearSpaceAlign(local: true)` with the pairs of rounds 1..k-1 forbidden (the SIM scheme: N + 1 runs of the exact linear program, O(m + n) memory per round plus the forbidden pairs). `LocalAlignTopN` / `LocalAlignAffineTopN` (all 10 overloads) switch to it above `TopNMaxCells` (internally `LocalTopNCore(..., fullMatrixCells, baseCells)`, lowered by the tests); new explicit API `LocalAlignTopNLinearSpace` / `LocalAlignAffineTopNLinearSpace` (string / `DnaSequence` x `ScoringMatrix`, string / `ProteinSequence` / `DnaSequence` x `MatrixScoring`, optional `maxFullMatrixCells`). Needs non-positive gap scores (clear `ArgumentException` above the limit otherwise). `LocalAlignCoOptimal` keeps the matrices (an exponential-size output has no linear-space form) and still rejects the input with the old message. MCP `local_align_top_n`: description (limit text replaced), `docs/mcp/tools/alignment/local_align_top_n.{md,mcp.json}`, MCP README.
- Why identical: removing diagonal edges only deletes edges of the DAG of Linear_Space_Alignment.md §2.3; the greedy-walk lemma does not depend on which edges exist (new §2.4).
- Cross-check: forced through the linear path (explicit API with base-case sizes 1 / 9 / 40 / 200, and `LocalTopNCore(fullMatrixCells: 10)`): **480 random tie-rich pairs** (320 scalar: 5 schemes incl. zero gap scores, linear + affine; 160 matrix: BLOSUM62 / EDNAFULL, fractional gaps; k <= 11, minScore) = the in-memory declumper row for row (score, coordinates, rows, order); **all 1 350 rows of `TestData/Alignment/topn_oracle.tsv`** (independent full-recompute Waterman-Eggert) equal, and the EMBOSS matcher 6.6.0 score lists on the same >= 90 % of its 397 rows as the in-memory path; large inputs above the limit (5 300 x 5 300 linear, 5 100 x 5 100 affine, planted similar segments) = the in-memory declumper forced with `fullMatrixCells = long.MaxValue`, alignment 1 = `LocalAlign` / `LocalAlignAffine`, no aligned pair reported twice. Memory (Release, one core, `LocalAlign(Affine)TopN` k = 10, random DNA with 40 planted similar segments, peak working set of the process incl. ~30 MB runtime): 5 kb 40 MB linear / 88 MB affine (matrix 95 / 286 MB), 10 kb 42 / 54 MB (381 MB / 1.1 GB), 20 kb 46 MB linear (1.5 GB) - time 2.8 s / 7.7 s, 10.9 s / 29.3 s, 41.5 s (linear, 20 kb).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_TopNLinearSpace_Tests` x10 (new), `SequenceAligner_TopN_Tests` memory-guard test replaced (top-N above the limit now works, co-optimal still rejects), MCP `TopNAlignTests` +1 (5 100 x 5 100). Docs: `Local_Alignment_Smith_Waterman.md` §5.5/§6.2, `Linear_Space_Alignment.md` §2.4/§4, MCP README, TestSpec ALIGN-LOCAL-001.
- Commit: 4dd26f80.

### F80 — ALIGN-GLOBAL/LOCAL/SEMI-001 (audit A2-37): the scalar `ScoringMatrix` wrapped `int` silently and accepted positive / `int.MinValue` gap scores
- Defect (exact arithmetic is the oracle): `GlobalAlign("A"x50, "A"x50, new ScoringMatrix(1_000_000_000, -1, -2, -1)).Score` = 1 999 999 904 (true 50 x 1e9 = 5e10), `LocalAlign` = 2 000 000 000, `GlobalAlignAffine` = 1 999 999 900, no exception; `GlobalAlignAffine(.., new ScoringMatrix(1, -1, int.MinValue, int.MinValue))` -> `IndexOutOfRangeException` (negation overflow; through `MultipleAlign(.., Affine)` an `AggregateException`); `GlobalAlign("ACGT","AC", new ScoringMatrix(1,-1,5,5))` = 30 (a positive gap "score"), while `MatrixScoring` rejected both the range and a positive gap. The multiple-alignment sum-of-pairs scorers accumulated in `int`.
- Source: the `MatrixScoring` contract already documented in `AlignmentTypes.cs` (gap scores are penalties <= 0, exact integer model, headroom 2^28 for the affine -inf sentinel `int.MinValue / 4`); EMBOSS / BLAST+ gap penalties are positive numbers = scores <= 0 here. No external oracle exists for "an overflowing score"; the reported `AlignmentResult.Score` is a 32-bit integer, so the only honest answer for a model that cannot be represented is a clear exception.
- Fix: `PairScoring(ScoringMatrix)` validates the gaps (<= 0, > `int.MinValue`, same message as the matrix path, ParamName `scoring`) and stores `max|step|`; the new `PairScoring.EnsureRange(m, n)` (one function for both models, same message "Score range too large for the integer dynamic program") runs at the top of every dynamic program (`LinearAlignCore`, `AffineAlignCore`, `LocalAlignCore`, `LinearSpaceAlign`, `LinearScoreOnly`, top-N, co-optimal); the general-gap fast path falls back to its double program when the integer model does not fit; sum-of-pairs accumulates in `long` (`SumOfPairsScoreLong`) and refuses a total beyond `int` with an `ArgumentException` (`MatrixSumOfPairsScore` returns the exact 64-bit value / scale). Dead code of the old "positive gap" special cases (`LinearSpaceApplies`, two `ArgumentException` branches) removed; XML docs (`ScoringMatrix`, affine remarks, top-N) state the contract.
- Contract change (tests updated, not bent): `LinearSpace_PositiveGapScore_IsRejectedAboveTheLimitWithAClearError` accepted `LocalAlignAffineTopN` with gaps (1, 1) below the limit; it now asserts the uniform rejection.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ScoringContract_Tests` x7 (the repro values through 14 aligners, 1e6-scale exact values 50 000 000, the 133 / 134 residue bound, int.MinValue, positive gaps scalar vs matrix, `MultipleAlign` k = 2/3/5, 64-bit sum of pairs 1.2e10 and BLOSUM62 24.0).
- Commit: 4e2f3ef7.

### F82 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-39): the empty-input contract differed per entry point, and the MCP pairwise tools contradicted the library
- Defect: library pairwise / star / progressive / iterative / consistency: an empty member -> all-gap row (F39); the three program ports refused it with three different messages that never said which methods accept it; MCP `global_align` / `local_align` / `*_affine` / `semi_global_align` / `local_align_top_n` / `*_co_optimal` / `general_gap_align` re-implemented the check ("Sequence cannot be null or empty", error 1001).
- Decision: the MCP tools DELEGATE (README rule 3): an empty string is passed to the library (all-gap alignment; no top-N / empty co-optimal list for the local tools); only a missing (null) value is rejected. The program ports keep refusing (the programs do: ClustalW 2.1 cannot read an empty sequence, MUSCLE 3.8 ignores empty FASTA records, `t_coffee` stops) with ONE message (`EmptyMemberMessage`) naming the program and the aligners that accept empty members.
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_InputContract_Tests` (`EmptyMember_ThreeProgramPorts_ShareOneMessage`, `EmptyMember_SeqeronAligners_ReturnAnAllGapRow`), MCP `EmptyInputContractTests` x4 (library equality for global / affine / matrix / co-optimal / local / semi-global / general-gap, null still rejected); the eight old "empty throws" assertions of the MCP tests removed. Docs: 7 `docs/mcp/tools/alignment/*.md` + `.mcp.json` error tables, MCP README, `Multiple_Sequence_Alignment.md` edge-case table.

### F83 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-41): int overflow / out-of-memory instead of a clear error in the T-Coffee proba pair-HMM and the MUSCLE profile DP
- Defect: `TcProbaPairEntries` allocated `new float[(I + 1) * (J + 1)]` and `l = (I + 1) * (J + 1) * nmt` in 32-bit `int` (wraps at 2^31 floats = about 27 000 x 27 000 protein / 36 000 x 36 000 DNA; 412 MB at 3 000 x 3 000 protein measured by the auditor); the MUSCLE profile DP (`NwSmall`) was unguarded (2 x 30 kb: 954 MB peak, one traceback byte per cell, then OOM).
- Source (opened): T-Coffee 13.41 `util_dp_suboptimal_nw.c` `forward_proba_pair_wise` / `backward_proba_pair_wise` (`int l = (len1 + 1) * (len2 + 1) * NumMatrixTypes`, `vcalloc`; no limit of its own); MUSCLE 3.8.1551 `nwsmall.cpp` (`char **TB`, one byte per cell; Debian `muscle3` 3.8.1551-2, grep of `muscle.h` / `globals.cpp` finds no `maxmb`): neither program has a limit, they wrap / abort. The port therefore refuses before allocating: `SequenceAligner.TCoffeeProbaMaxMatrixFloats` = 2^28 (1 GiB per matrix; about 7 300 x 7 300 protein / 9 400 x 9 400 DNA) and `MuscleProfileMatrixMaxCells` = 1e9 (30 kb x 30 kb still runs), `long` arithmetic (`checked` conversion of the forward / backward length), `ArgumentException` with the limit and the alternative (`slow_pair` / `lalign_id_pair`; ClustalW linear space). ClustalW has no guard: linear space, O(k^2 L^2) time - cancellable (F86). MCP README "Memory and length limits" and `Multiple_Sequence_Alignment.md` updated.
- Tests: `TCoffeeProba_RefusesAnOversizedPair_BeforeAllocating`, `MuscleProfileDp_RefusesAnOversizedProfilePair_WithAClearArgumentException`.
- Commit: 4e2f3ef7.

### F84 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-42): `Parallel.For` / `ForEach` leaked `AggregateException` (and ignored cancellation / thread limits)
- Defect: `SelectCenterByPairwiseScore` (always) and `AlignAllToCenter` (k >= 4) ran the pair DPs through `Parallel.ForEach` / `For`; any exception inside reached the caller wrapped (the sequential path of the same method, k = 2 with the old code, threw the bare exception).
- Fix: `SequenceAligner.ParallelFor` (`SequenceAligner.Cancellation.cs`): records the exception of the LOWEST failing index (the one the sequential path throws first), stops the loop and rethrows it with `ExceptionDispatchInfo` (original type and stack); honours the ambient cancellation token; new `MsaOptions.MaxDegreeOfParallelism` (-1 default = runtime, 1 = sequential; 0 / < -1 -> `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; the result never depends on it).
- Tests: `ExceptionInTheParallelPairStage_KeepsItsType_ForEveryK` (k = 2, 3, 5 x linear / affine; the 2^28 range guard fires inside a worker thread), `MaxDegreeOfParallelism_ChangesNothing_AndIsValidated`.
- Commit: 4e2f3ef7.

### F85 — ALIGN-GLOBAL-001 (audit A2-43): `null` for the scoring argument did not compile (CS0121)
- Defect: `LocalAlignTopN(a, b, null, 3, 1)` was ambiguous between `(string, string, ScoringMatrix?, int, int)` and `(string, string, MatrixScoring, int, double)`; the same for every overload pair created by F41.
- Source: C# 13 `OverloadResolutionPriorityAttribute` (Microsoft Learn "Overload resolution priority"; net10.0 / LangVersion latest): the highest priority wins among applicable candidates.
- Fix: `[OverloadResolutionPriority(1)]` on the 57 public `ScoringMatrix?` overloads that have a `MatrixScoring` twin (found by a signature scan; `GlobalAlign`, `LocalAlign`, `SemiGlobalAlign` + `Affine`, linear space, top-N, co-optimal, `MultipleAlign*`, `MultipleAlignClustalW`); calls with a `MatrixScoring` argument do not change (the `ScoringMatrix?` overload is not applicable). Docs: parameter table of `Global_Alignment_Needleman_Wunsch.md`.
- Tests: `NullScoring_Compiles_AndSelectsTheDefault_OnEveryPairwiseFamily` (17 `null` calls - this file does not compile without the fix), `NullScoring_TheTypedMatrixOverloadsStillBind`, reflection test `EveryScoringMatrixOverloadWithAMatrixScoringTwin_CarriesTheResolutionPriority` (guards future overloads).
- Commit: 4e2f3ef7.

### F86 — ALIGN-* / GENOMIC-* (audit A2-40): no cancellation on the long-running public APIs and the MCP tools
- Defect: only 5 `GlobalAlign` overloads and the linear-space core took a `CancellationToken`; none of `MultipleAlign*`, `MultipleAlignClustalW` (2 x 30 kb DNA = 258 s Debug), `MultipleAlignMuscle` (47 s), `MultipleAlignTCoffeeProgram`, `LocalAlignTopN` / `*CoOptimal`, `*GeneralGap`, `FindCommonRegions`, `FindOpenReadingFrames`, `FindKnownMotifHits`, `FindMaximalRepeats`; the MCP tools had no token.
- Design (documented, single mechanism): the dynamic programs poll an ambient `AsyncLocal<CancellationToken>` (`AlignmentCancellation`, flows into `Parallel.For` workers) at their row / iteration boundaries: affine / linear / local fills, linear-space passes, top-N declumper rows, co-optimal DFS, general-gap rows, profile DPs, ClustalW `ProgDiff` / pairwise rows / distance matrix, MUSCLE `NwSmall` rows, T-Coffee pair-HMM forward / backward / entry scan. The scope is entered by: the option records (`MsaOptions`, `ClustalWOptions`, `ClustalWProteinOptions`, `MuscleOptions`, `TCoffeeProgramOptions` get `CancellationToken`, the convention of `ParallelOptions.CancellationToken`; additive, ignored by the semantics), `SequenceAligner.WithCancellation(token, () => ...)` / `BeginCancellationScope(token)` (every other aligner, incl. the matrix overloads - no 100-overload explosion, no CS0121 against the existing optional-parameter overloads), and trailing-token overloads of `GenomicAnalyzer.FindRepeats` / `FindMaximalRepeats` / `FindCommonRegions` / `FindKnownMotifHits` / `FindOpenReadingFrames` (+ `OrfScanner.FindRegions(..., cancellationToken)`, polled every 65 536 codons / 4096 nodes / 1024 positions). A cancelled call throws `OperationCanceledException` and returns nothing (result objects are built after the last poll); the scope is restored on exit. MCP: the 20 alignment tools and `find_repeats`, `find_known_motifs`, `find_common_regions`, `find_open_reading_frames` take a trailing `CancellationToken` (bound by the MCP SDK, not in the input schema). `IProgress<double>` is not added (no natural measure for the MSA programs; the existing `GlobalAlign` progress overload is unchanged).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_Cancellation_Tests` x11 (pre-cancelled token on every family, 100 ms cancellation of calls that run seconds to minutes, identical results with a live token, no leak of the scope).
- Commit: 4e2f3ef7.

### F89 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-1 = A1-10c a3): MUSCLE round 3 run locally with the Debian `muscle3` package; `-matrix` for nucleotides, one-leaf `-maketree`
- **Defects (2).** (1) `muscle -matrix file` on DNA / RNA was refused by the port (`ArgumentException`), but the program runs it: `domuscle.cpp` calls `SetAlpha(DNA)`, reads the matrix with `ReadMx` under the nucleotide tables (only the A C G T/U rows and columns reach `Mx[0..3][0..3]`), then sets `g_Alpha = ALPHA_Amino` and `g_PPScore = PPSCORE_SP` - the sequences stay nucleotide letters while the score defaults (gap open -1439, `kmer6_6`), the methionine hack (`MHackStart` tests `g_Alpha`), the wildcard counts (`GetFractionalWeightedCounts` amino branch) and `-le/-spn` replacement rules follow the amino alphabet. (2) `-maketree` of a one-sequence alignment: the port wrote `s0;`, the program `(\ns0)\n;\n` (the leaf is the root, `ToFileNodeRooted` groups a root; oracle R651).
- **Source.** Debian `muscle3` 3.8.1551-2 sources from sources.debian.org (`fetch_src.py`, 184 files, User-Agent `Seqeron-review/1.0`): `domuscle.cpp` L56-80, `readmx.cpp`, `params.cpp` SetPPScore / SetPPDefaultParams, `alpha.cpp`, `msa2.cpp`, `mhack.cpp`, `phytofile.cpp`. Oracle binary: `apt-get install muscle3` (Debian 3.8.1551-3, x86_64; the package is a distro package from the Debian archive; `muscle3 -version` = MUSCLE v3.8.1551), the Docker package's harness `run_sets.py` (already fixed to decode console bytes with `backslashreplace`) run locally; the Docker run is no longer needed (README: INTEGRATED / NOT NEEDED).
- **Oracle.** `scripts/alignment/generate_muscle3_r3_oracle.py` -> `TestData/Alignment/muscle3_r3_oracle.tsv` (60 sets, `returned/ENV-local.txt`). First comparison 52 / 60; after the fixes 60 / 60 (8 `-matrix blosum62nostar`, 8 `-matrix` on DNA / RNA, 44 one- / two-sequence corners incl. 10 refusals: 8 segmentation faults of `-root1/-root2 midlongestspan | minavgleafdist` on one-sequence profiles = `NotSupportedException`, 2 `Clust::SetLeafCount(1)` = `InvalidOperationException`, 8 `-le -sp -sv -spn`).
- **Fresh re-verification of F69 / F76.** `scripts/alignment/muscle3_fresh_sample.py` (seeds 7, 11-15 = 1 800 random `align` sets over protein / DNA / RNA with random option mixtures incl. `-matrix`): 1 800 / 1 800 equal to the port (rows, written order, files, refusals). Two refusal classes of the program were not yet classified in the test mapping (`DistUnaligned, unsupported distance method` for pctid_* distances of unaligned sequences, `DistKmer4_6 requires nucleo alphabet`): both are the port's `ArgumentException`; no defect of the port. The claims of F69 / F76 hold.
- **Undefined behaviour of the reference (new finding).** With `-matrix` on nucleotides `ScoreProfPos2` (`scorepp.cpp`) loops over 20 sorted letters while `SortCounts` fills only `g_AlphaSize` = 4 entries of `m_uSortOrder`; a profile column holding all four nucleotides therefore reads `m_uSortOrder[4]`, never written. Debian's binary segfaulted on 8 of the 60 sampled DNA / RNA `-matrix` runs and on 70 of the 1 800 fresh runs overall (the crashes are the pre-existing one-sequence-profile cases plus these); a `-O3` build of the same source instrumented in the scratchpad read 0, 22 and 46 there and finished (ASan: 0xBEBEBEBE). The port computes with that memory zero, which equals the program on every run that finished, and reports it: `MuscleRunResult.ReferenceBehaviourUndefined` (MCP `multiple_align_muscle` output `referenceBehaviourUndefined`); the test asserts that every crash of a nucleotide `-matrix` run is flagged. Proposed LIMITATIONS entry: nucleotide `-matrix` reproduces MUSCLE 3.8.1551 only up to this undefined read.
- **Tests.** `SequenceAligner_MuscleOracleRound3_Tests` (62 cases: 37 align + 23 command-line sets + 2 coverage tests; R0..R637 covered by round 2 + round 3), `SequenceAligner_MuscleOptions_Tests.MuscleRun_UserMatrix_OnNucleotides_...` replaces the old `Throws<ArgumentException>` assertion; the Round2 test helpers were made shareable (`CheckAlign`, `CheckCommandLine`, `Load`); the fresh-sample comparison is an uncommitted temporary test (not skipped, not committed: its inputs are generated on demand).
- Files: `SequenceAligner.Muscle.cs` / `.MuscleProgram.cs` / `.MuscleTrees.cs`, `AlignmentTypes.cs` (`MuscleOptions.UserMatrix` doc, `MuscleRunResult.ReferenceBehaviourUndefined`), MCP `AlignmentTools` / `Results.cs` + tool docs, ALIGN-MULTI-001 TestSpec / Evidence / report / algorithm doc §4.9a, handoff README (INTEGRATED).

### F90 — ALIGN-STATS-001 (audit A2-15): EMBOSS `distmat` corrections / options and the full EMBOSS matrix set were not implemented
- **Gap.** `MsaDistanceMethod` had only uncorrected and Jukes-Cantor (double precision, no `-ambiguous` / `-position`); `SubstitutionMatrix` had BLOSUM62 / PAM250 / EDNAFULL (+ ClustalW's own tables) and the MCP tools resolved only those three names.
- **Source (opened, raw.githubusercontent.com/kimrutherford/EMBOSS, neutral User-Agent):** `emboss/distmat.c` in full, `ajax/core/ajbase.c` (`ajBaseAlphacharCompare`), `ajax/core/ajseq.c` (`ajSeqsetIsNuc`); installed EMBOSS 6.6.0 `distmat` and `/usr/share/EMBOSS/data/*`.
- **Implemented.** `MultipleAlignmentStatistics.CalculateDistanceMatrix(rows, MsaDistanceOptions)` (new file `MultipleAlignmentStatistics.Distmat.cs`; the class is partial now): Kimura (2-parameter and protein), Tamura, Tajima-Nei, Jin-Nei Gamma (`CalculateA`, `ParameterA`), `Ambiguous`, `Position` 123 / 12 / 1 / 2 / 3, `GapWeight`; a faithful single-precision port (float variables, double `log` / `pow` / `sqrt`, C promotion rules, the program's quirks: Jin-Nei `slen` carried across pairs, `strchr` matching NUL, NUL padding of position 12, the EMBOSS reader typing every row on its own and turning X into N in a nucleotide-looking row). The old overload delegates (no duplicate). `SubstitutionMatrix.Get / TryGet / BuiltInNames / IsNucleotide` (`SubstitutionMatrix.Registry.cs` + generated `SubstitutionMatrix.EmbossData.cs`, `scripts/alignment/generate_emboss_matrices.py`): the 70 matrices of the EMBOSS 6.6.0 data directory (EBLOSUM30-90, EBLOSUM62-12, EBLOSUMN, EPAM10-500, EDNAFULL, EDNAMAT, EDNASIMPLE, ENUC.4.4) by EMBOSS or NCBI name; EBLOSUM62 / EPAM250 / EDNAFULL are the existing static instances. MCP `alignment_distance_matrix` (methods, `ambiguous`, `position`, `calculateA`, `parameterA`), the Alignment tools' `matrix` parameters and `multiple_align_clustalw`'s `scoreMatrix` resolve through `SubstitutionMatrix.TryGet` (the nucleotide test is `IsNucleotide`, not reference equality with EDNAFULL).
- **Oracle numbers.** `scripts/alignment/generate_distmat_oracle.py` -> `distmat_oracle.tsv`: 1 100 runs of the installed `distmat` (every method / option; 50 are rejections or non-terminations of the program): printed strings equal on 1 100 / 1 100 after (a) the per-row EMBOSS reader typing (X -> N in a nucleotide-looking row; 1 case) and (b) refusing the undefined cases below. Matrices: 70 / 70 EMBOSS files cell for cell; NCBI aliases = Biopython 1.88 on all common symbols (0 differing cells).
- **Program defects reproduced / refused (documented in the XML docs, the algorithm page and the MCP description):** `-ambiguous` on nucleotides is broken in 6.6.0 (an unambiguous base never matches: `len = |list| - 1 = 0`; the first code not an IUB letter, e.g. a gap, loops `(unsigned)-1` times: `NotSupportedException`); Tajima-Nei and the protein Kimura method are called with `mlen` instead of the selected length, so a position other than 123 reads heap memory beyond the buffers (about half of the runs equal the NUL-padded model, the rest do not): `NotSupportedException`, the program's values are recorded in the oracle and the test asserts the refusal; `-position` 23 / 13 / anything else is fatal = `ArgumentException`. Proposed LIMITATIONS entry: distmat reproduction is exact except those two undefined cases.
- **Tests.** `MultipleAlignmentStatistics_Distmat_Tests` (1 100 + 3), `SubstitutionMatrix_Registry_Tests` (90), MCP `AlignmentDistanceMatrix_AllDistmatCorrections_PrintedValuesOfEmboss6_6_0`, `NamedBuiltInMatrices_AreAccepted_EmbossAndNcbiNames`; the two hand-value assertions that pinned the double-precision value (10.0 within 1e-9, a Jukes-Cantor value within 1e-9) now allow the single-precision error / the printed two decimals (distmat 6.6.0 prints 10.00; computes in float).
- Files: `MultipleAlignmentStatistics*.cs`, `SubstitutionMatrix.Registry.cs`, `SubstitutionMatrix.EmbossData.cs`, `AlignmentTools.cs`, `AnalysisTools.cs`, MCP docs (`alignment_distance_matrix` + the matrix descriptions of the alignment tools), ALIGN-STATS-001 TestSpec / Evidence / report / `Alignment_Statistics.md`.

### F91 — GENOMIC-MOTIFS-001 (audit A2-18): fuzznuc's "overhanging hit" decided against the binary
- **Question.** `Known_Motif_Search.md` said fuzznuc reports an overhanging hit on a sequence shorter than the motif ("`AAA` with `-pmismatch 2` on `AC`") and that this is "not reproduced, not in the oracle".
- **Measured (fuzznuc 6.6.0, `scripts/analysis/generate_fuzznuc_short_sequence_oracle.py`, 300 runs, sequence 1 .. m-1 bases, patterns 3-8 bases, k 1 .. m-1, 20 % of the patterns with R/Y/N):** the report exists (`AAA` / `AC` k=2 prints `1..3`, `*mismatch 2`; `AAAA` / `AC` k=3 prints `1..4`, mismatch 3, so the missing positions count as mismatches) but only 49 of 300 runs print anything, 41 printed hits end beyond the end of the sequence (GFF end > sequence length), 10 reverse-strand lines have end < start (e.g. `2..-1`: garbage coordinates), and a model "pad with mismatches" explains the plain-ACGT cases but not those with IUPAC codes (e.g. `CC` / `NNC` k=2: the padded window is within the bound, fuzznuc prints nothing). Classification: an out-of-range artefact of the matcher, not a defined semantics.
- **Decision locked in the test.** `GenomicAnalyzer_KnownMotifs_ShortSequence_Tests` (new file): the oracle must show the artefacts (> 30 runs with hits, > 30 hits ending beyond the sequence, > 5 with end < start) and `FindKnownMotifHits` reports no hit for all 300 rows at any mismatch bound. Doc corrected (`Known_Motif_Search.md`: the overhang statement with the measured evidence; also the false "fuzznuc has no gapped patterns" sentence of A2-17 (5): `fuzznuc -pattern 'AC(2,3)G'` finds `ACCCG` and `ACCG`; the implementation of the pattern language is A2-32 / WP26).

### F93 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-16): the paper-path T-Coffee library had no top-N Lalign alignments and no redundancy weights
- Added `MsaOptions.PrimaryLocalAlignments` (top-N non-intersecting local alignments from `LocalAlignTopN` via the internal `LocalTopNCore`, each at its own integer percent identity, signal-added with the global library; N = 1 keeps the old single-best path bit for bit) and `MsaOptions.SequenceWeights` (`MsaSequenceWeights.TCoffee` = the program path's `TcTCoffeeSequenceWeights` on the program's default k-tuple identities, applied to the extended weight as `min(w_i, w_j)` after the extension; also passed to `TcAlignDetailed` for the `TCoffeeLinked` engine). MCP `multiple_align_consistency`: `primaryLocalAlignments`, `sequenceWeights`.
- **Verification (what is and is not oracle-verified).** The top-N alignments: EMBOSS matcher oracle (F47); the weight function: t_coffee `-seq_weight t_coffee` oracle of the program path (F66); the new library construction: an independent re-derivation in `SequenceAligner_ConsistencyOptions_Tests` (global NW + each top-N alignment at its own integer percent identity, signal-added, equal on every residue pair); the weighted extended weight = unweighted extended x min(w_i, w_j) on sampled pairs. NOT compared with a `t_coffee` run: the paper-formula engine is not the program's pipeline (the program uses SIM with the id matrix, integer library weights, relaxation `-relax_lib 1`, sparse DP); that pipeline is `MultipleAlignTCoffeeProgram`, which already has `lalign_id_pair` + `-seq_weight` and equals the program (F58 / F66).
- Tests: `SequenceAligner_ConsistencyOptions_Tests` (5), files `SequenceAligner.cs`, `SequenceAligner.TCoffee.cs`, `AlignmentTypes.cs`, `AlignmentTools.cs` + tool docs, Multiple_Sequence_Alignment.md.

### F92 — ALIGN-MULTI-001 (audit A2-14, part): ClustalW `-TYPE` guess implemented; `-CLUSTERING=UPGMA` ported (oracle pending); `-ITERATION` / structure masks not done
- **Done and verified.** `SequenceAligner.GuessClustalWSequenceType` (+ MCP `multiple_align_clustalw` `type='auto'`): ClustalW 2.1 decides the residue type from the SECOND sequence only (`FileReader::readFile` sets the flag at `i == 1`; `Sequence::checkDNAFlag`: DNA when >= 85 % of its non-gap characters are A C G T U N; a single sequence stays protein). Oracle: the "Sequence n: name len bp|aa" lines of the installed `clustalw` 2.1 on 500 random sets (`scripts/alignment/generate_clustalw_type_oracle.py`, 176 DNA / 324 protein, sets around the 85 % threshold, 1-sequence sets, lower case): 500 / 500 equal (`SequenceAligner_ClustalWTypeGuess_Tests`). The old statement "4 of 151 random DNA MSAs were classified protein" is this rule.
- **Ported, not oracle-verified.** `-CLUSTERING=UPGMA` (`ClustalWOptions.Clustering` / `ClustalWProteinOptions.Clustering` = `ClustalWClustering.Upgma`, MCP `clustering='upgma'`; `SequenceAligner.ClustalWUpgma.cs`): `UPGMAAlgorithm::doUPGMA` (packed lower-triangular distance rows, `getNodeWithMinDist`, the row / column updates with the 1e-14 relative epsilon of `computeDistsForNodesAfterNode2`), `Node::merge` (both children get the height of the join), `RootedGuideTree::doWeightCalc` / `calcSeqWeights` (float arithmetic), the join order as progressive steps, `makeSimilarityMatrix` (100 - 100 d, no clamp); for two sequences the source stores no step (the sequences stay unaligned). **Proof that the program cannot be the oracle here:** the installed Debian `clustalw` 2.1+lgpl-7 exits 139 (segmentation fault) on EVERY `-CLUSTERING=UPGMA` run (`-ALIGN` and `-TREE`; 2, 3, 4 sequences; only the two-sequence `.dnd` is written before the crash), while the same Debian source compiled at `-O0` with AddressSanitizer ran the UPGMA alignment to the end in the audit sandbox ("Group 1: Sequences: 2 Score:228", "Group 2: Sequences: 3 Score:199", "Alignment Score 216" on three 13-mers); the next self-built run (an `-O2` build) was REFUSED by the sandbox classifier and was not worked around. Per the standing user instruction: Docker package `docs/Validation/review-2026-09/handoff/A2-14-clustalw-upgma` (Dockerfile building the unmodified source at -O0 and -O2, `run.sh`, 600 deterministic sets, README with integration plan). Locked meanwhile: `SequenceAligner_ClustalWUpgma_Tests` (hand-derived 3-taxon joins, heights, float weights 416 / 416 / 166, two-sequence weights 500 / 500, integrity of the DNA and protein alignments) and MCP `ClustalWAutoTypeUpgmaTests`.
- **Not done (DOABLE).** `-ITERATION=TREE | ALIGNMENT` / `-NUMITER` (`multipleAlign/Iteration.cpp` + `alignment/ObjectiveScore.cpp`; the installed program runs it, on a 4-sequence set it leaves the rows unchanged, so an oracle needs larger / divergent sets) and the secondary-structure gap-penalty masks (`-NOSECSTR1/2`, `-SECSTROUT`, `-HELIXGAP`, `-STRANDGAP`, `-LOOPGAP`, `-TERMINALGAP`, `-HELIXENDIN/OUT`, `-STRANDENDIN/OUT`: they act on profile alignments of structure-annotated files, `-PROFILE1/2`, i.e. a profile-alignment entry point (`-PROFILE`, `-SEQUENCES`) that the library does not have yet). Both stay open in the worklist.
- Files: `SequenceAligner.ClustalWUpgma.cs`, `SequenceAligner.ClustalWMultiple.cs` (`CwConfig.Upgma`, `BuildWeightsAndStepsUpgma`, `GuessClustalWSequenceType`), `AlignmentTypes.cs` (`ClustalWClustering`), `AlignmentTools.cs` + MCP docs, handoff package, Multiple_Sequence_Alignment.md, Evidence.

### F94 — GENOMIC-COMMON-001 (audit A2-30): strand, mode, k-mismatch and N-sequence common regions
- **Defect / gap.** `FindCommonRegions` / `FindLongestCommonRegion` searched one strand, one notion (right-maximal), exact, two sequences, `DnaSequence` only (`Common_Region_Detection.md` §5.3 "not implemented").
- **Source.** MUMmer 3 (`-maxmatch`, `-mumreference`, `-mum`, `-r`, `-b`, `-c`; installed 3.23 run), Flouri et al. 2015 / Thankachan et al. 2016 (k-mismatch LCS), Gusfield 1997 §7.6 (k-common substring).
- **Cross-check.** `mummer -b` on 4716 (reference, query, L, mode) rows == an independent Python brute force (positions converted from the reverse-complemented query; the `-c` convention asserted), 0 mismatches; k-mismatch LCS 1680 and N-sequence LCS 694 brute-force rows.
- **Fix.** `CommonRegionMode` + `StrandSelection` overloads of `FindCommonRegions` (canonical `SuffixTree.FindMaximalExactMatches` / `FindMaximalUniqueMatches` + the matching-statistics engine on the reverse-complemented query), `FindLongestCommonRegion(…, strands)`, `FindLongestCommonRegionWithMismatches` (per-diagonal two pointers), `FindLongestCommonRegion(IReadOnlyList<…>, minSupport)` (`SuffixTree.FindLongestCommonSubstrings`), `CommonRegion.IsReverseComplement`; string overloads (non-A/C/G/T = unique separator). MCP `find_common_regions` (`mode`, `strands`, IUPAC), new `find_longest_common_region_mismatches`; N-sequence LCS = existing `suffix_tree_k_common_substrings`.
- **Tests.** `GenomicAnalyzer_CommonRegionStrands_Oracle_Tests` (11); MCP tests. Commit 214a9804.

### F95 — GENOMIC-SIMILARITY-001 (audit A2-31): MinHash / FracMinHash estimators, containment, Jaccard distance, ANI
- **Gap.** Only the exact k-mer Jaccard percentage existed (`Sequence_Similarity.md` §5.3: MinHash, Jaccard distance not implemented).
- **Source.** Ondov et al. 2016 (Mash), sourmash 4.9.4 (`MinHash(n=…)`, `MinHash(scaled=…)`, `distance_utils.containment_to_distance` / `jaccard_to_distance`, source read), Koslicki & Zabeti 2019, Hera et al. 2023.
- **Cross-check.** 4290 rows (990 exact == Python sets == sourmash `scaled=1`, 1980 FracMinHash scaled 2..10, 1320 bottom-s k >= 17), all within 1e-12; the Mash / sourmash / containment machinery is the B06 `KmerAnalyzer` (called, not copied).
- **Fix.** `CompareSimilarity(a, b, k, canonical, sketchSize | scaled)` -> `SimilarityEstimate` (Jaccard, Jaccard distance, Mash distance, Jaccard ANI, containments, containment ANI, shared hashes, p-value), `CalculateSimilarity(…, sketchSize)`, `CalculateJaccardDistance`, string overloads (non-A/C/G/T windows skipped). MCP `calculate_similarity` (`sketchSize`, `scaled`, added fields).
- **Tests.** `GenomicAnalyzer_SimilaritySketch_Oracle_Tests` (6); SuffixTree.Mcp.Core tests. Commit 214a9804.

### F96 — GENOMIC-MOTIFS-001 (audit A2-32): the fuzznuc pattern language
- **Gap.** `FindKnownMotifs` took IUPAC strings only; fuzznuc `-pattern` also has classes, exclusions, repeats, ranges, anchors, pattern files.
- **Source.** `fuzznuc -help` (6.6.0 binary) and `nucleus/embpat.c` / `embpatlist.c` / `fuzznuc.c` of the original program (raw.githubusercontent.com/kimrutherford/EMBOSS): grammar and errors (`embPatClassify`), search classes (`embPatGetTypeII`), recursive matcher (`patOUBrute`), regex all-matches (`embPatMatchFindAllC`), reverse strand = reverse-complemented sequence, same pattern.
- **Findings decided on the binary (documented in the Evidence file).** Ranges report every window length; with -pmismatch > 0 a range pattern tolerates mismatches only in the mandatory part (extra repeats exact) and cannot end a trailing-optional path on the last base; an exact range pattern starting with `<` returns only the longest anchored match; malformed spellings are accepted by the binary in unspecified ways (rejected here); the sequence-ambiguity-code rule (pattern N matches sequence N) differs from the canonical B05 contract and is not reproduced.
- **Cross-check.** 3300 random syntax-tree (sequence, pattern, k) rows, fuzznuc 6.6.0 `-pmismatch k -complement Y` == independent Python reference, 0 disagreements (1758 with ranges, 960 range + mismatches, 987 anchored).
- **Fix.** `FuzznucPattern` (Parse / TryParse / Expand / ExpandWithExtras / ReverseComplement / ParsePatternFile), `GenomicAnalyzer.FindPatternHits` (+ parsed-pattern and named-pattern overloads) compiled to the canonical `ApproximateMatcher` IUPAC Hamming scan; MCP new `find_pattern_hits`.
- **Tests.** `GenomicAnalyzer_PatternHits_Oracle_Tests` (14); MCP tests. Commit 214a9804.

### F97 — GENOMIC-REPEAT-001 (audit A2-33): both-strand repeats and repeat pairs
- **Gap.** Repeat search was forward-strand only (`Repeat_Detection.md` §5.3).
- **Source.** MUMmer `repeat-match` (default both strands, `-f`), Vmatch `-p`, REPuter, Gusfield 1997 §7.12 on `S # revcomp(S)`; the B04 F25 `repeat-match` palindromic-quirk (oracle run on `S + 'N'`).
- **Cross-check.** 1900 (sequence, L) rows: class set of the `repeat-match` pairs == the brute-force maximal classes on every ACGT row; C# classes (maximal and all-substring) and pair lists (both strands and `-f`) equal the oracle.
- **Fix.** `FindRepeatsBothStrands`, `FindAllLongestRepeatsBothStrands`, `FindLongestRepeatBothStrands` (`StrandedRepeatInfo`), `FindRepeatPairs` (`RepeatPair`, `StrandSelection`; the canonical `RepeatFinder` pair engines with the filters lifted); the repeat engine was generalised to any suffix tree (separators never repeat). MCP `find_repeats` (`bothStrands`), new `find_all_longest_repeats`.
- **Tests.** `GenomicAnalyzer_StrandRepeats_Oracle_Tests` (13). Commit 214a9804.

### F98 — GENOMIC-TANDEM-001 (audit A2-34): MUMmer `exact-tandems` oracle and canonical rotation
- **Oracle.** The Debian `exact-tandems` wrapper is broken (stray line break in its `awk -f` line); the two documented stages (`repeat-match -t -n L | sort | tandem-repeat.awk`) were run directly on 3630 (sequence, L) rows. Mapping: per start the smallest-unit run with extent − unit >= L; `FindTandemRepeats(1, 2)` reproduces 3630 / 3630 rows exactly.
- **Fix.** `FindTandemRepeats(…, canonicalRotation: true)` reports the least rotation (Booth 1980; MISA classes) as `Unit`, `TandemRepeat.Rotation` / `OriginalUnit` / `FullSequence` keep the real bases; `RepeatFinder.GetMinimalRotation` made public (B09 for B04, additive). MCP `find_tandem_repeats` (`canonicalRotation`, `originalUnit`).
- **Tests.** `GenomicAnalyzer_ExactTandems_Oracle_Tests` (4). Commit 50563f72.

### F99 — GENOMIC-REPEAT-001 / -MOTIFS-001 / -COMMON-001 / -SIMILARITY-001 (audit A2-35): string overloads with N / IUPAC semantics
- **Gap.** `FindLongestRepeat`, `FindAllLongestRepeats`, `FindRepeats`, `FindMaximalRepeats`, `FindMotif`, `FindKnownMotifs`, `FindKnownMotifHits`, `FindLongestCommonRegion`, `FindCommonRegions`, `CalculateSimilarity` accepted `DnaSequence` only.
- **Contract (the B04 / B05 convention, MUMmer `-n`).** Case-insensitive; only A/C/G/T match; every other symbol matches nothing (not even N), ends a repeat / region / k-mer window and costs a mismatch when a budget is given; an A/C/G/T string equals the `DnaSequence` overload. Repeats and common regions build the suffix tree of a text in which every run of non-matching symbols is a unique separator (an optional `alphabet` makes the repeat engine usable for proteins).
- **Cross-check.** In-test brute force on 600 N / IUPAC / lower-case strings for the repeat overloads and 400 for the motif overloads, plus the MUMmer / repeat-match oracles above (their N rows).
- **Source-compat note.** Adding `string` overloads makes a literal `null` first argument ambiguous: the existing tests that passed `null!` now cast it (`(DnaSequence)null!`); no behaviour change.
- **Tests.** `GenomicAnalyzer_StringOverloads_Tests`, the strand / common / similarity oracle fixtures. Commit 214a9804.
